00621 
00622 class _WindowsApiTable:
00623     """The namespace of the primitive table of the broker.
00624 
00625     The attributes are assigned explicitly.  A class body resolves a
00626     name that it assigns itself in the class namespace and in the global
00627     namespace only, so a class body that copies an enclosing local into
00628     a name of its own raises a ``NameError`` instead of copying the
00629     value.
00630     """
00631 
00632     def __init__(self, *, kernel32, types):
00633         self.kernel32 = kernel32
00634         self.types = dict(types)
00635         for name in ('STARTUPINFOW', 'STARTUPINFOEXW', 'PROCESS_INFORMATION',
00636                      'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES', 'IO_COUNTERS',
00637                      'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
00638                      'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
00639                      'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST'):
00640             setattr(self, name, types[name])
00641 
00642     def __repr__(self):  # pragma: no cover - a diagnostic only
00643         return '<WindowsApiTable types=%d>' % len(self.types)
00644 
00645 
00646 def _load_windows_api():
00647     """Load the Windows primitive table of the broker.
00648 
00649     The load is lazy and it happens only inside the explicit native entry.
00650     An import of this module on a platform that is not Windows never
00651     touches ``WinDLL`` and never resolves a symbol, so the module stays
00652     importable and testable everywhere.  An unsupported platform refuses
00653     here, before anything is created: it is never degraded into an
00654     unconstrained process.
00655 
00656     The declaration of the structures and the declaration of the
00657     prototypes are two separate functions, so that the layout and the
00658     prototypes can be inspected without loading a native library at all.
00659     """
00660     if os.name != 'nt':
00661         raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLATFORM_UNSUPPORTED')
00662     import ctypes
00663     kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL('kernel32', use_last_error=True)
00664     types = _windows_api_types()
00665     _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types)
00666     return _WindowsApiTable(kernel32=kernel32, types=types)
00667 
00668 
00669 class CreatedProcess:
00670     """The original handles of one created process."""
00671 
00672     __slots__ = ('process', 'thread', 'pid', 'creation_time', 'failures')
00673 
00674     def __init__(self, process, thread, pid, creation_time, failures=()):
00675         self.process = process
00676         self.thread = thread
00677         self.pid = pid
00678         self.creation_time = creation_time
00679         self.failures = tuple(failures)
00680 
00681 
00682 class WindowsApi:
00683     """The typed Windows primitive façade of the broker.
00684 
00685     Every method of this class wraps exactly one Windows API call, and the
00686     broker never calls a Windows function itself.  That keeps the native
00687     detail - the pointer sizes, the handle ownership and the last error -
00688     in one place, and it lets the offline validation drive the very same
00689     broker logic through an in-process façade of the same shape.
00690 
00691     The declarations are pointer size safe: a handle is a
00692     ``wintypes.HANDLE``, every pointer is an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer
00693     and every structure declares its fields.  The last error of a failed
00694     call is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is only correct
00695     because the module was loaded with ``use_last_error=True``.
00696     """
00697 
00698     CREATE_SUSPENDED = 0x00000004
00699     EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT = 0x00080000
00700     STARTF_USESTDHANDLES = 0x00000100
00701     CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT = 0x00000400
00702     JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x00002000
00703     JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK = 0x01000000
00704     JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION = 9
00705     JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST = 3
00706     WAIT_OBJECT_0 = 0
00707     WAIT_TIMEOUT = 0x00000102
00708     HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT = 0x00000001
00709     PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST = 0x00020002
00710     GENERIC_READ = 0x80000000
00711     FILE_SHARE_READ = 0x00000001
00712     FILE_SHARE_WRITE = 0x00000002
00713     OPEN_EXISTING = 3
00714     FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL = 0x00000080
00715     ERROR_MORE_DATA = 234
00716     ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE = 109
00717     ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED = 233
00718     ERROR_HANDLE_EOF = 38
00719     ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED = 995
00720     ERROR_NOT_FOUND = 1168
00721     DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS = 0x00000002
00722 
00723     # -- the ownership states of one retained handle ----------------------
00724     #: The façade still owns the handle and nobody else may take it.
00725     API_OWNED = 'API_OWNED'
00726     #: The recovery of the façade has **claimed** the handle and it is
00727     #: preparing to close it.  A handover may not take it and a second
00728     #: recovery may not claim it again while the claim is alive.
00729     RECOVERY_CLAIMED = 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'
00730     #: The handover of the handle is running: the receiver registers it
00731     #: and the façade forgets it in one atomic step.
00732     TRANSFER_PENDING = 'TRANSFER_PENDING'
00733     #: The handle was handed over to its new owner.  It is not in the
00734     #: holding table any more and it is never closed by the recovery.
00735     TRANSFERRED = 'TRANSFERRED'
00736     #: The handle was closed with a confirmed close.
00737     CLOSED = 'CLOSED'
00738 
00739     def __init__(self, table=None):
00740         """The typed façade of the Windows primitives.
00741 
00742         The table is the primitive table of the broker.  A table may be
00743         handed in for the inspection of the declaration and of the
00744         layout: the table of a real run is the one that loads the
00745         native library, and the table of an inspection never loads one.
00746         The code that uses the façade is the same code in both cases.
00747         """
00748         self._api = table if table is not None else _load_windows_api()
00749         # The invalid value of a handle has the width of a pointer: it is
00750         # ``-1`` as a 32 bit value and it is ``0xffffffffffffffff`` as a
00751         # 64 bit one.  A comparison with the Python value ``-1`` would
00752         # never match the 64 bit value.
00753         self.invalid_handle_value = self._api.types['ctypes'].c_void_p(-1).value
00754         # The holding table of the façade.  Every original handle that a
00755         # successful ``CreateProcess`` produced enters this table in the
00756         # moment of its creation, before anything else may fail.  A handle
00757         # that was handed over to its owner leaves the table; a handle
00758         # that could not be handed over stays in it, with its owner, its
00759         # pid and the failures of its release.  The table is the recovery
00760         # entry point of the façade: the core keeps the façade and it can
00761         # therefore always reach the handles that it never received.
00762         self._retained = {}
00763         self._retained_order = []
00764         # The one lock of the holding table.  Every mutation of the
00765         # table and every handover of a handle out of it happen under
00766         # this lock: a handle that is registered by its receiver and
00767         # that the façade still names would have **two** owners, and a
00768         # handle that left the façade before its receiver registered it
00769         # would have none.
00770         self._table_lock = threading.RLock()
00771         # The handovers that really happened.  The list is the evidence
00772         # of the unique owner of a handle: a handle that was handed
00773         # over is not in the holding table any more and it is never
00774         # closed by the recovery of the façade.
00775         self._handovers = []
00776         # The ONE deadline of the recovery of the façade.  It is
00777         # published once: a second recovery of the same round reuses
00778         # the very same value and it never opens a new budget.  It may
00779         # only be **shortened** by a context that arrived later.
00780         self._recovery_deadline = None
00781         # The absolute deadline of the round, when the façade is bound to
00782         # one.  Every wait of the façade is bounded by it: a wait that
00783         # does not fit into the deadline of the round never starts.
00784         self._deadline = None
00785         # The ONE context of the round.  The façade reads the absolute
00786         # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
00787         # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
00788         self._context = None
00789         self._clock = time.monotonic
00790 
00791     def bind_deadline(self, deadline, clock=None):
00792         """Bind the absolute deadline of the round to this façade.
00793 
00794         The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
00795         bound.  The waits of the façade - the wait of a release and the
00796         wait of the recovery - are bounded by it and they never start a
00797         wait with a fresh timeout of their own.
00798         """
00799         value = _require_deadline(deadline)
00800         if value is None:
00801             return self._deadline
00802         if clock is not None:
00803             self._clock = clock
00804         if self._deadline is None or value < self._deadline:
00805             self._deadline = value
00806         return self._deadline
00807 
00808     def bind_context(self, context):
00809         """Bind the ONE context of the round to this façade.
00810 
00811         The façade reads the absolute deadline and the published
00812         cleanup deadline of the round from the context and it never
00813         keeps a budget of its own: a recovery that opened a fresh
00814         budget of its own would outlive the round that it recovers.
00815         """
00816         if context is not None:
00817             self._context = context
00818         return self._context
00819 
00820     def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
00821         """The ONE deadline that a wait of this façade really uses.
00822 
00823         The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
00824         façade, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
00825         cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
00826         the four.  The value is recomputed on every call, so a deadline
00827         that was published after the wait started still shortens it.
00828         """
00829         value = _require_deadline(call)
00830         bound = self._deadline
00831         if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
00832             value = bound
00833         context = self._context
00834         if context is not None:
00835             absolute = context.absolute_deadline
00836             if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
00837                 value = absolute
00838             cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
00839             if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
00840                 value = cleanup
00841         return value
00842 
00843     def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
00844         """The milliseconds that are left until the bound deadline.
00845 
00846         A façade without a bound deadline keeps the closed wait of the
00847         package.  A façade with one never waits longer than the deadline
00848         of the round allows.
00849 
00850         The milliseconds are **floored** and they are never rounded
00851         **up**: a remainder that is smaller than the representable unit
00852         is zero and it asks for no wait at all.  A wait of one
00853         millisecond with a remainder of a fraction of a millisecond
00854         would outlive the round that it belongs to.
00855         """
00856         value = self._effective_deadline()
00857         if value is None:
00858             return WAIT_MILLISECONDS
00859         remaining = value - self._clock()
00860         if remaining <= 0:
00861             return 0
00862         return min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000))
00863 
00864     def _last_error(self):
00865         """The last error of the last failing primitive of the host.
00866 
00867         The value is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is
00868         only correct because the module was loaded with
00869         ``use_last_error=True``.  A host that does not have that
00870         function at all cannot report a last error and reports zero.
00871         """
00872         getter = getattr(self._api.types['ctypes'], 'get_last_error', None)
00873         if getter is None:
00874             return 0
00875         return int(getter())
00876 
00877     def _security_attributes(self, inherit):
00878         """The security attributes of one handle.
00879 
00880         The attributes say that the handle may be inherited.  A handle
00881         that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes is never
00882         inheritable, so the handle list of the process attributes would
00883         name a handle that the child cannot receive.
00884         """
00885         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
00886         attributes = self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']()
00887         attributes.nLength = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES'])
00888         attributes.lpSecurityDescriptor = None
00889         attributes.bInheritHandle = bool(inherit)
00890         return attributes
00891 
00892     # -- the holding table of the original handles -------------------------
00893     def _retain(self, handle, kind, pid=None, stage='CREATED'):
00894         """Take one original handle into the holding table of the façade.
00895 
00896         The table is entered in the moment of the creation of the handle
00897         and it is left only when the handle was closed with a confirmed
00898         close or when it was handed over to its owner.  A handle that
00899         stays in the table is a resource that this round still owns and
00900         it is named with its kind, its pid and its failures.
00901         """
00902         key = id(handle)
00903         with self._table_lock:
00904             if key not in self._retained:
00905                 self._retained_order.append(key)
00906             self._retained[key] = {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind, 'pid': pid,
00907                                    'stage': stage, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
00908                                    'state': self.API_OWNED,
00909                                    'failures': []}
00910             return self._retained[key]
00911 
00912     def _forget(self, handle):
00913         """Leave the holding table: the handle is closed or handed over."""
00914         key = id(handle)
00915         with self._table_lock:
00916             if key in self._retained:
00917                 del self._retained[key]
00918                 if key in self._retained_order:
00919                     self._retained_order.remove(key)
00920                 return True
00921         return False
00922 
00923     def forget_handle(self, handle):
00924         """Leave the holding table, under the lock of the table.
00925 
00926         The method is the public face of ``_forget``: the receiver of a
00927         handle uses it when the façade of the primitives does not offer
00928         the atomic handover.  A façade that offers the handover uses
00929         that one instead.
00930         """
00931         return self._forget(handle)
00932 
00933     def _note(self, handle, failure):
00934         """Name one failure of one retained handle."""
00935         key = id(handle)
00936         with self._table_lock:
00937             entry = self._retained.get(key)
00938             if entry is not None:
00939                 entry['failures'].append(failure)
00940             return entry
00941 
00942     def retained_handles(self):
00943         """The original handles that this façade still holds.
00944 
00945         The list is a copy: a caller that changes it changes nothing of
00946         the holding table of the façade.
00947         """
00948         with self._table_lock:
00949             return [dict(self._retained[key]) for key in self._retained_order
00950                     if key in self._retained]
00951 
00952     def handovers(self):
00953         """The handovers that really happened, as a copy of the log."""
00954         with self._table_lock:
00955             return [dict(item) for item in self._handovers]
00956 
00957     def handover(self, entries, *, owner, register):
00958         """Hand the named handles over to their new owner, in one step.
00959 
00960         The receiver registers the handles and the façade forgets them
00961         under **one** lock.  The two facts are one atomic step: a
00962         handle that the receiver registered and that the façade still
00963         names would have two independent owners, and the recovery of
00964         the façade could close a handle that a live reader of the round
00965         is reading from.  A handle that left the façade before its
00966         receiver registered it would have no owner at all.
00967 
00968         The handover and the recovery of the façade are **the same
00969         ownership decision**.  A handle that the recovery has already
00970         claimed is refused here: the recovery is preparing to close it
00971         and a second owner may never appear.  A refused handle stays
00972         with the façade and it is never registered with the receiver.
00973 
00974         A registration that fails leaves the handle with the façade:
00975         the state of the entry is restored and the handle is never
00976         half registered and closed by two owners at the same time.
00977 
00978         ``entries`` is the list of ``(handle, kind)`` pairs and
00979         ``register`` receives it and registers every handle with the
00980         receiver.  The handover is recorded, so that the evidence of the
00981         round can name the owner that received the handle.
00982         """
00983         items = list(entries)
00984         refused = []
00985         with self._table_lock:
00986             accepted = []
00987             for handle, kind in items:
00988                 entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
00989                 if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
00990                     # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
00991                     # is preparing to close it: the handover may not
00992                     # take it and the receiver may never name it.
00993                     refused.append((handle, kind))
00994                     continue
00995                 if entry is not None:
00996                     entry['state'] = self.TRANSFER_PENDING
00997                 accepted.append((handle, kind))
00998             try:
00999                 register(accepted)
01000             except BaseException:
01001                 # The receiver did not register the handles: they stay
01002                 # with the façade and their state is restored.  A half
01003                 # registered handle is never closed by two owners.
01004                 for handle, _kind in accepted:
01005                     entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01006                     if entry is not None:
01007                         entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
01008                 raise
01009             for handle, kind in accepted:
01010                 entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01011                 if entry is not None:
01012                     entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
01013                 self._forget(handle)
01014                 self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': owner,
01015                                         'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
01016         if refused:
01017             raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDOVER_CLAIMED',
01018                               ','.join(repr(handle) for handle, _kind in refused))
01019         return True
01020 
01021     def _claim_entry(self, key):
01022         """Claim one retained entry for the recovery of the façade.
01023 
01024         The claim is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table:
01025         an entry that another recovery already claimed is never claimed
01026         a second time, and an entry that a handover took is never
01027         claimed at all.  The claim does not hold the lock of the table
01028         while it waits: the blocking primitive of the recovery runs
01029         outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
01030         """
01031         with self._table_lock:
01032             entry = self._retained.get(key)
01033             if entry is None:
01034                 return None
01035             if entry.get('state') != self.API_OWNED:
01036                 # The entry is claimed by a recovery that is running or
01037                 # it is in the middle of a handover: it is not the owner
01038                 # of this recovery.
01039                 return None
01040             entry['state'] = self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
01041             return dict(entry)
01042 
01043     def _decide_entries(self, entries, state):
01044         """One table decision for a set of retained entries.
01045 
01046         The decision is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table
01047         and it covers **every** named entry at once: the process and
01048         the thread of one creation are decided together and a decision
01049         that reserved one of them and refused the other one is never
01050         taken.
01051 
01052         The entries that the decision reserved leave the table in the
01053         state ``state`` and the entries that it refused keep their own
01054         state.  An entry that is **not** in the table any more is
01055         refused as well: it was already closed or already handed over
01056         and it may never be delivered or closed a second time.
01057 
01058         The lock of the table is **never** held while a blocking
01059         primitive runs: the decision is taken, the primitive runs
01060         outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
01061         """
01062         reserved = []
01063         refused = []
01064         with self._table_lock:
01065             for handle, kind in entries:
01066                 entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01067                 if entry is None:
01068                     refused.append((handle, kind, 'GONE'))
01069                     continue
01070                 current = entry.get('state')
01071                 if current == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
01072                     # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
01073                     # is preparing to close it: nobody else may take it.
01074                     refused.append((handle, kind, 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'))
01075                     continue
01076                 if current != self.API_OWNED:
01077                     refused.append((handle, kind, str(current)))
01078                     continue
01079                 entry['state'] = state
01080                 reserved.append((handle, kind))
01081         return reserved, refused
01082 
01083     def _restore_entries(self, entries, state):
01084         """Give the reserved entries back to the table of the façade.
01085 
01086         A decision that could not be completed gives its entries back:
01087         the entries keep their original reference and a later recovery
01088         of the round may claim them again.
01089         """
01090         with self._table_lock:
01091             for handle, _kind in entries:
01092                 entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01093                 if entry is None:
01094                     continue
01095                 if entry.get('state') == self.TRANSFER_PENDING:
01096                     entry['state'] = state
01097         return True
01098 
01099     def _commit_entries(self, entries):
01100         """The reserved entries left the table: they are the property of
01101         their new owner.
01102 
01103         The handover of the entries is recorded and it happens under the
01104         lock of the table: a handle that the receiver registered and
01105         that the façade still names would have two owners.
01106         """
01107         with self._table_lock:
01108             for handle, kind in entries:
01109                 entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01110                 if entry is not None:
01111                     entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
01112                 self._forget(handle)
01113                 self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
01114                                         'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
01115         return True
01116 
01117     def _release_claim(self, key):
01118         """Give a claim back: a later recovery may claim the entry again."""
01119         with self._table_lock:
01120             entry = self._retained.get(key)
01121             if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
01122                 entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
01123 
01124     def _owns_claim(self, key):
01125         """Whether the claim of this recovery is still the owner of the entry."""
01126         with self._table_lock:
01127             entry = self._retained.get(key)
01128             if entry is None:
01129                 return False
01130             return entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
01131 
01132     def _recovery_timeout_ms(self):
01133         """The milliseconds that are left until the ONE recovery deadline.
01134 
01135         The deadline of the recovery is published **once**: a second
01136         recovery of the same round reuses the very same value and it
01137         never opens a new budget of its own.  It may only be **shortened**
01138         by a context that arrived later: a recovery that re-read the
01139         context of the round may not use the long lease of the round
01140         after the round published its cleanup deadline.
01141 
01142         A wait of a recovery that arrives after the deadline never
01143         starts.
01144         """
01145         with self._table_lock:
01146             current = self._effective_deadline()
01147             if self._recovery_deadline is None:
01148                 value = current
01149                 if value is None:
01150                     value = self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0)
01151                 self._recovery_deadline = min(
01152                     value, self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0))
01153             elif current is not None and current < self._recovery_deadline:
01154                 self._recovery_deadline = current
01155             remaining = self._recovery_deadline - self._clock()
01156         if remaining <= 0:
01157             return 0
01158         # The milliseconds are floored and they are never rounded up: a
01159         # wait of one millisecond with a remainder of a fraction of a
01160         # millisecond outlives the round that it belongs to.
01161         return min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000))
01162 
01163     def _confirm_process_exit(self, handle, failures, cancellations,
01164                               timeout_ms=None):
01165         """Try to confirm the exit of one process, once.
01166 
01167         The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close
01168         of a handle are two different facts.  A wait that timed out is
01169         **not** an exit: the process may still be alive and it may even
01170         be a suspended process that never ran.  Its control handle is
01171         the only handle that may stop it.
01172 
01173         The method returns ``True`` only when the exit was really
01174         confirmed.  A cancellation of the wait is named and it is handed
01175         to the caller, which raises it after every safe step was
01176         executed.
01177         """
01178         if timeout_ms is None:
01179             timeout_ms = self._wait_timeout_ms()
01180         if timeout_ms <= 0:
01181             failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:no_budget')
01182             return False
01183         try:
01184             result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(handle, timeout_ms)
01185         except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01186             failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:CANCELLED')
01187             cancellations.append(error)
01188             return False
01189         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01190             failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:' + type(error).__name__)
01191             return False
01192         if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
01193             failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exited')
01194             return True
01195         if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
01196             failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed')
01197             return False
01198         failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:wait=%d' % int(result))
01199         return False
01200 
01201     def release_retained(self):
01202         """Try to release every handle that the façade still holds.
01203 
01204         This is the recovery entry point of the façade.  It is explicit:
01205         a caller that wants the handles of a failed creation released
01206         asks for it, and the result is named.  A handle that could not
01207         be closed stays in the holding table.
01208 
01209         Every handle is released on its own and every step of one handle
01210         is independent of the others: a cancellation in the middle of the
01211         recovery never skips the close of the next handle.  The
01212         cancellation is raised at the end of the recovery, after every
01213         safe step was executed, and it never replaces an ordinary
01214         failure of a handle.
01215 
01216         The **control handle of a process** is released only when its
01217         exit was really confirmed.  A process whose exit was not
01218         confirmed may still be alive and its control handle is the only
01219         handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays in the
01220         holding table with its owner and the recovery of the façade can
01221         reach it again: a later recovery that confirms the exit closes
01222         it and forgets it.  A close that succeeds is never a proof that
01223         the process ended.
01224 
01225         The recovery and the handover are **one ownership decision**.
01226         Every entry is **claimed** under the lock of the table before
01227         the recovery touches it, and a claim that is alive keeps every
01228         other recovery and every handover away from the entry.  The
01229         blocking primitive of the recovery runs **outside** of the lock
01230         of the table: the table is never held while the recovery waits.
01231         The claim is verified again before the close: an entry that
01232         changed its owner in the meantime is never closed.
01233         """
01234         report = []
01235         cancellations = []
01236         for key in list(self._retained_order):
01237             claimed = self._claim_entry(key)
01238             if claimed is None:
01239                 # The entry is not owned by this recovery: it is claimed
01240                 # by a recovery that is running or it was handed over.
01241                 # It is never closed here.
01242                 entry = self._retained.get(key)
01243                 if entry is None:
01244                     continue
01245                 report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01246                                'released': False,
01247                                'failures': ['entry_not_claimed:' + str(
01248                                    entry.get('state'))]})
01249                 continue
01250             entry = claimed
01251             handle = entry['handle']
01252             failures = []
01253             try:
01254                 if entry['kind'] == 'process':
01255                     try:
01256                         if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(handle, 1):
01257                             failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
01258                                             % self._last_error())
01259                     except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01260                         failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
01261                         cancellations.append(error)
01262                     except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01263                         failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
01264                     confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(
01265                         handle, failures, cancellations,
01266                         timeout_ms=self._recovery_timeout_ms())
01267                     if not confirmed:
01268                         # The process may still be alive: the control
01269                         # handle stays with the façade and it is never
01270                         # closed under a process that nobody may stop
01271                         # any more.
01272                         failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:'
01273                                         'exit_not_confirmed')
01274                         self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
01275                         report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01276                                        'released': False,
01277                                        'failures': list(failures)})
01278                         continue
01279                 # The claim is verified again before the close: an entry
01280                 # that changed its owner while the recovery waited is
01281                 # never closed by this recovery.
01282                 if not self._owns_claim(key):
01283                     failures.append('entry_claim_lost:before_close')
01284                     report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01285                                    'released': False,
01286                                    'failures': list(failures)})
01287                     continue
01288                 if self._close_quietly(handle, failures, cancellations):
01289                     self._forget(handle)
01290                 else:
01291                     self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
01292                 report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01293                                'released': not any(failure.startswith('CloseHandle')
01294                                                    for failure in failures),
01295                                'failures': list(failures)})
01296             finally:
01297                 # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is
01298                 # given back: a later recovery of the round may claim
01299                 # the entry again and it may confirm the exit.
01300                 self._release_claim(key)
01301         if cancellations:
01302             # The cancellation is raised after every safe step of the
01303             # recovery was executed.  It is never swallowed and it never
01304             # replaces an ordinary failure that is named in the report.
01305             raise cancellations[0]
01306         return report
01307 
01308     # -- the job object ---------------------------------------------------
01309     def create_job(self):
01310         handle = self._api.kernel32.CreateJobObjectW(None, None)
01311         if not handle:
01312             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_CREATE_FAILED',
01313                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01314         return handle
01315 
01316     def set_job_kill_on_close(self, job):
01317         """Set the kill-on-close limit of the job and nothing else.
01318 
01319         ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK`` is deliberately *not* set: a
01320         child of the round may not break away from the job.
01321         """
01322         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01323         limit = self._api.JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION()
01324         limit.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags = self.JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE
01325         if not self._api.kernel32.SetInformationJobObject(
01326                 job, self.JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
01327                 ctypes.byref(limit), ctypes.sizeof(limit)):
01328             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_LIMIT_FAILED',
01329                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01330         return True
01331 
01332     def terminate_job(self, job, exit_code):
01333         if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateJobObject(job, exit_code):
01334             raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
01335                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01336         return True
01337 
01338     def job_process_ids(self, job):
01339         """The bounded list of the process ids of the job.
01340 
01341         The query is bounded by the descendant cap of this package.  A
01342         job that holds more processes than the cap is *not* reported as an
01343         empty job: the answer is unknown, and an unknown answer never
01344         becomes a zero.
01345 
01346         The structure of Windows holds two counts and an **inline** array
01347         of ``ULONG_PTR``.  The array is read from the buffer at the
01348         offset of the array and never through a pointer field.
01349 
01350         The answer is only accepted when **every** fact of it agrees:
01351 
01352         * the returned length is at least the size of the header, so that
01353           the two counts were really written and a legal empty job still
01354           carries its complete header;
01355         * the returned length is at most the size of the buffer, so that
01356           a length that is larger than the buffer was never accepted;
01357         * the returned length is at least the length that the count of
01358           the list needs, so that the list was not truncated;
01359         * the two counts are equal, so that the number of the assigned
01360           processes and the number of the listed processes agree;
01361         * the count of the list is within the cap of this package.
01362 
01363         Every other result is **unknown**: an exception, a returned
01364         length of zero, a reversed pair of counts, a count over the cap
01365         and a truncated list.  An unknown answer is never an empty job
01366         and never a success.
01367         """
01368         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01369         capacity = MAX_DESCENDANTS + 1
01370         pointer_size = ctypes.sizeof(ctypes.c_size_t)
01371         header_size = 2 * ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['DWORD'])
01372         size = header_size + capacity * pointer_size
01373         buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
01374         returned = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01375         if not self._api.kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject(
01376                 job, self.JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST, buffer, size,
01377                 ctypes.byref(returned)):
01378             error = self._last_error()
01379             if error == self.ERROR_MORE_DATA:
01380                 raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01381             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_UNKNOWN', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01382         header = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(
01383             self._api.JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST)).contents
01384         assigned = int(header.NumberOfAssignedProcesses)
01385         listed = int(header.NumberOfProcessIdsInList)
01386         reported = int(returned.value)
01387         # The returned length has to carry the whole header and it may
01388         # never be larger than the buffer that was handed to Windows.
01389         if reported < header_size or reported > size:
01390             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01391                               'returned=%d:header=%d:allocated=%d'
01392                               % (reported, header_size, size))
01393         if assigned > capacity or listed > capacity:
01394             raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
01395                               'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01396         if assigned != listed:
01397             # The two counts disagree: the answer is unknown and it is
01398             # never an empty job.
01399             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01400                               'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01401         if listed > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
01402             raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
01403                               'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01404         needed = header_size + listed * pointer_size
01405         if needed > reported:
01406             raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01407                               'returned=%d:needed=%d' % (reported, needed))
01408         if listed == 0:
01409             # A legal empty job: the header was complete and the two
01410             # counts were zero.
01411             return []
01412         offset = header_size // pointer_size
01413         identifiers = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_size_t))
01414         return [int(identifiers[offset + index]) for index in range(listed)]
01415 
01416     # -- the processes -----------------------------------------------------
01417     def create_process_suspended(self, *, executable, arguments, working_directory,
01418                                   stdout_handle, stderr_handle, environment=None):
01419         """Create one suspended process and return its original handles.
01420 
01421         The child inherits exactly three handles: the write end of its
01422         standard output, the write end of its standard error and the null
01423         device as its standard input.  The inheritance set is passed
01424         explicitly as the handle list of the process attributes, so that
01425         the control channel of the broker, the job handle and every other
01426         handle of the broker stay in the broker.
01427 
01428         The size of the start up information is the size of the whole
01429         ``STARTUPINFOEXW``, because the extended flag is set and the
01430         attribute list follows the start up information inside it.
01431 
01432         The creation has **independent** steps.  The deletion of the
01433         attribute list, the close of the null device, the birth query of
01434         the created process and the release of a process that could not
01435         be adopted do not depend on each other: a failure of one of them
01436         never skips the others.  The first error of the creation is the
01437         error that leaves the method and the later errors are named next
01438         to it; the first error is never replaced by a later one.
01439 
01440         The original handles of a created process enter the holding table
01441         of this façade in the moment of the creation.  The null device
01442         of the child enters it in the moment of its creation as well: a
01443         null device that could not be closed is a resource of this round
01444         and it stays with its owner.  A handle that was handed over to
01445         the caller leaves the table; a handle that could not be closed
01446         stays in it with its owner and its failures, so that the recovery
01447         of the façade can always reach it.
01448         """
01449         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01450         kernel32 = self._api.kernel32
01451         failures = []
01452         command = ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(_windows_command_line(executable, arguments))
01453         environment_block = _environment_block(environment)
01454         attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
01455         null = kernel32.CreateFileW('NUL', self.GENERIC_READ,
01456                                     self.FILE_SHARE_READ | self.FILE_SHARE_WRITE,
01457                                     ctypes.byref(attributes), self.OPEN_EXISTING,
01458                                     self.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL, None)
01459         if not null or null == self.invalid_handle_value:
01460             error = self._last_error()
01461             raise BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_FAILED',
01462                               'last_error=%d' % error)
01463         # The moment of the creation of the null device.  The handle of
01464         # the null device enters the holding table of this façade right
01465         # here: a null device whose close fails later is a resource of
01466         # this round and it keeps its original owner and its recovery
01467         # entry point.
01468         self._retain(null, 'null_device')
01469         attribute_list = None
01470         information = None
01471         process = None
01472         thread = None
01473         pid = None
01474         primary = None
01475         cleanup_error = None
01476         cancellations = []
01477         try:
01478             try:
01479                 inherited = (ctypes.c_void_p * 3)(stdout_handle, stderr_handle, null)
01480                 size = ctypes.c_size_t(0)
01481                 kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(None, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size))
01482                 attribute_list = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size.value)
01483                 if not kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(
01484                         attribute_list, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size)):
01485                     error = self._last_error()
01486                     raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
01487                                       'last_error=%d' % error)
01488                 if not kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute(
01489                         attribute_list, 0, self.PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST,
01490                         inherited, ctypes.sizeof(inherited), None, None):
01491                     error = self._last_error()
01492                     raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
01493                                       'last_error=%d' % error)
01494                 startup = self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW()
01495                 # The extended start up information is passed, so the
01496                 # size is the size of the whole structure and not the
01497                 # size of the plain start up information.
01498                 startup.StartupInfo.cb = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW)
01499                 startup.StartupInfo.dwFlags = self.STARTF_USESTDHANDLES
01500                 startup.StartupInfo.hStdInput = null
01501                 startup.StartupInfo.hStdOutput = stdout_handle
01502                 startup.StartupInfo.hStdError = stderr_handle
01503                 startup.lpAttributeList = ctypes.cast(attribute_list, ctypes.c_void_p)
01504                 information = self._api.PROCESS_INFORMATION()
01505                 flags = (self.CREATE_SUSPENDED | self.EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT
01506                          | self.CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT)
01507                 environment_pointer = (ctypes.cast(environment_block, ctypes.c_void_p)
01508                                        if environment_block is not None else None)
01509                 created = kernel32.CreateProcessW(
01510                     str(executable), command, None, None, True, flags,
01511                     environment_pointer, str(working_directory),
01512                     ctypes.byref(startup.StartupInfo), ctypes.byref(information))
01513                 if not created:
01514                     error = self._last_error()
01515                     raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_CREATE_FAILED',
01516                                       'last_error=%d' % error)
01517                 # The moment of the creation.  The original handles of
01518                 # the process enter the holding table of this façade
01519                 # before anything else may fail: they are never only an
01520                 # integer in a local list or in the detail of an error.
01521                 process = information.hProcess
01522                 thread = information.hThread
01523                 pid = int(information.dwProcessId)
01524                 self._retain(process, 'process', pid)
01525                 self._retain(thread, 'thread', pid)
01526             except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01527                 primary = error
01528             finally:
01529                 # The cleanups of the creation are independent of each
01530                 # other and independent of the failure that is on the way
01531                 # out.  A cleanup that fails never skips the other one and
01532                 # a cancellation in the middle of a cleanup never skips
01533                 # the cleanup that follows it: the cancellation is named
01534                 # and it is raised after every safe step was executed.
01535                 attribute_failures = []
01536                 if attribute_list is not None:
01537                     try:
01538                         kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList(attribute_list)
01539                     except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01540                         attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:CANCELLED')
01541                         cancellations.append(error)
01542                     except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01543                         attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:'
01544                                                   + type(error).__name__)
01545                 null_failures = []
01546                 if self._close_quietly(null, null_failures, cancellations):
01547                     # The null device was closed with a confirmed close:
01548                     # it may be forgotten.
01549                     self._forget(null)
01550                 failures.extend(attribute_failures)
01551                 failures.extend(null_failures)
01552                 if attribute_failures:
01553                     cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_CLEANUP_FAILED',
01554                                                 '|'.join(attribute_failures))
01555                 elif null_failures:
01556                     cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_CLOSE_FAILED',
01557                                                 '|'.join(null_failures))
01558         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01559             if primary is None:
01560                 primary = error
01561         if cancellations and primary is None:
01562             # The cancellation of a cleanup is the primary error of the
01563             # creation when nothing else failed.  The cleanups that were
01564             # safe were executed before it was raised and the ordinary
01565             # failures that were named stay named next to it.
01566             primary = cancellations[0]
01567             failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
01568         creation = None
01569         if process is not None:
01570             # The birth query is the identity of the created process.  It
01571             # is independent of the cleanups above: it always runs when a
01572             # process was created.
01573             try:
01574                 creation = self._process_creation_time(process)
01575             except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01576                 if primary is None:
01577                     primary = error
01578         if primary is None and cleanup_error is None:
01579             # The creation is handed over to the caller: the original
01580             # handles are the property of the caller now and they leave
01581             # the holding table.  The handover is **one** decision of
01582             # the table: the process and the thread are reserved
01583             # together, and a handle that the recovery of the façade
01584             # claimed is never delivered and never forgotten here.
01585             reserved, refused = self._decide_entries(
01586                 [(process, 'process'), (thread, 'thread')],
01587                 self.TRANSFER_PENDING)
01588             if not refused:
01589                 self._commit_entries(reserved)
01590                 return CreatedProcess(process, thread, pid, creation, failures)
01591             # The recovery of the façade owns one of the original
01592             # handles: the creation may not deliver a stale handle to
01593             # the core.  The creation failed and the direct cleanup
01594             # below claims the entries that it may claim.
01595             self._restore_entries(reserved, self.API_OWNED)
01596             primary = BrokerError(
01597                 'BROKER_PROCESS_HANDOVER_REFUSED',
01598                 ','.join('%s:%s' % (kind, reason)
01599                          for _handle, kind, reason in refused))
01600         # The creation failed.  A process that was created is released
01601         # through its own original handle and every failure is named.
01602         release_failures = []
01603         if process is not None:
01604             self._release_created(process, thread, release_failures, cancellations)
01605         failures.extend(release_failures)
01606         if cancellations and primary is None:
01607             # The cancellation of the release is the primary error of the
01608             # creation when nothing else failed.  Every safe step was
01609             # executed before it is raised.
01610             primary = cancellations[0]
01611             failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
01612         error = primary if primary is not None else cleanup_error
01613         detail = getattr(error, 'detail', None)
01614         if failures:
01615             error.detail = ((detail or '') + '|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01616         if cancellations and error is not cancellations[0]:
01617             # The primary error and the cancellation coexist: the
01618             # cancellation object stays reachable next to the primary
01619             # error and it is never replaced by a copy of a message.
01620             try:
01621                 error.lvw_cancelled = cancellations[0]
01622             except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01623                 pass
01624         raise error
01625 
01626     def _close_quietly(self, handle, failures, cancellations=None):
01627         """Close one handle and name a failure of the close.
01628 
01629         A cancellation in the middle of a close never skips the close
01630         that follows it: it is named in the failures and it is handed to
01631         the caller, which raises it after every safe step was executed.
01632         """
01633         try:
01634             if not self._api.kernel32.CloseHandle(handle):
01635                 failures.append('CloseHandle:last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01636                 return False
01637         except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01638             failures.append('CloseHandle:CANCELLED')
01639             if cancellations is not None:
01640                 cancellations.append(error)
01641             return False
01642         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01643             failures.append('CloseHandle:' + type(error).__name__)
01644             return False
01645         return True
01646 
01647     def _release_created(self, process, thread, failures, cancellations=None):
01648         """Release a process that was created but never adopted.
01649 
01650         The process is stopped through its own original handle, its exit
01651         is confirmed and both of its handles are closed.  A failure of
01652         one of the steps is named and the other steps still run: a handle
01653         is never left to the garbage collector.  A cancellation in the
01654         middle of the release never skips the close that follows it: it
01655         is named and the release continues.
01656 
01657         The direct cleanup of a failed creation is **one** decision of
01658         the table, exactly like the recovery of the façade and exactly
01659         like the handover of a pipe: the original entries are claimed
01660         before they are touched, the decision covers the process and
01661         the thread together and a handle that the recovery of the
01662         façade already claimed is **never** closed here.  The claim is
01663         taken under the lock of the table and the blocking primitives
01664         of the release run **outside** of it.
01665 
01666         The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close of
01667         a handle are two separate facts and they are recorded separately:
01668         a wait that timed out is not an exit and a close that failed is
01669         not a close.  A handle that could not be closed stays in the
01670         holding table of the façade: the original reference is kept and
01671         the recovery of the façade can reach it.
01672 
01673         The **control handle of the process** is the one exception: it
01674         is closed only when the exit was really confirmed.  A process
01675         whose exit was not confirmed may still be alive - it may be a
01676         suspended process that never ran - and its control handle is the
01677         only handle that may stop it.  The handle stays in the holding
01678         table of the façade with its owner, so that the recovery of the
01679         façade can reach it again.
01680 
01681         The cancellations of the release are collected in the list of
01682         the caller: they are raised after every safe step was executed
01683         and they never replace the primary error of the creation.
01684         """
01685         if cancellations is None:
01686             cancellations = []
01687         entries = [(process, 'process')]
01688         if thread is not None:
01689             entries.append((thread, 'thread'))
01690         claimed, refused = self._decide_entries(entries, self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED)
01691         for _handle, kind, reason in refused:
01692             failures.append('create_release_refused:%s:%s' % (kind, reason))
01693         owned = {id(handle) for handle, _kind in claimed}
01694         closed = set()
01695         if id(process) in owned:
01696             try:
01697                 if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(process, 1):
01698                     failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
01699                                     % self._last_error())
01700             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01701                 failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
01702                 cancellations.append(error)
01703             except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01704                 failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
01705             confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(process, failures, cancellations)
01706             if not confirmed:
01707                 # The process may still be alive.  Its control handle
01708                 # stays with the façade: it is never closed and never
01709                 # forgotten.
01710                 failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:exit_not_confirmed')
01711                 self._note(process, 'exit_not_confirmed:control_handle_retained')
01712             elif self._close_quietly(process, failures, cancellations):
01713                 self._forget(process)
01714                 closed.add(id(process))
01715             else:
01716                 self._note(process, 'close_not_confirmed')
01717         # The thread handle is independent of the exit of the process: it
01718         # is closed on its own and it never waits for the process.
01719         if thread is not None and id(thread) in owned:
01720             if self._close_quietly(thread, failures, cancellations):
01721                 self._forget(thread)
01722                 closed.add(id(thread))
01723             else:
01724                 self._note(thread, 'close_not_confirmed')
01725         # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is given back:
01726         # a later recovery of the round may claim the entry again and it
01727         # may confirm the exit.
01728         for handle, _kind in claimed:
01729             if id(handle) not in closed:
01730                 self._release_claim(id(handle))
01731         if cancellations:
01732             failures.append('release_cancelled:' + type(cancellations[0]).__name__)
01733         return failures
01734 
01735     def _process_creation_time(self, process):
01736         """The birth time of one process, as the 100 nanosecond tick count.
01737 
01738         The query is the identity of the process: a process whose birth
01739         time cannot be read has no proven identity and is not used.
01740         """
01741         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01742         creation = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01743         exit_time = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01744         kernel = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01745         user = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01746         if not self._api.kernel32.GetProcessTimes(
01747                 process, ctypes.byref(creation), ctypes.byref(exit_time),
01748                 ctypes.byref(kernel), ctypes.byref(user)):
01749             error = self._last_error()
01750             raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_BIRTH_QUERY_FAILED',
01751                               'last_error=%d' % error)
01752         return (int(creation.dwHighDateTime) << 32) | int(creation.dwLowDateTime)
01753 
01754     def assign_process_to_job(self, job, process):
01755         if not self._api.kernel32.AssignProcessToJobObject(job, process):
01756             raise BrokerError('BROKER_ASSIGN_FAILED',
01757                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01758         return True
01759 
01760     def resume_thread(self, thread):
01761         result = self._api.kernel32.ResumeThread(thread)
01762         if result == 0xFFFFFFFF:
01763             raise BrokerError('BROKER_RESUME_FAILED',
01764                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01765         return int(result)
01766 
01767     def terminate_process(self, process, exit_code):
01768         if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(process, exit_code):
01769             raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
01770                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01771         return True
01772 
01773     def wait_process(self, process, timeout_ms):
01774         result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(process, timeout_ms)
01775         if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
01776             return True
01777         if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
01778             return False
01779         raise BrokerError('BROKER_WAIT_FAILED', 'wait=%d' % int(result))
01780 
01781     def exit_code(self, process):
01782         code = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01783         if not self._api.kernel32.GetExitCodeProcess(
01784                 process, self._api.types['ctypes'].byref(code)):
01785             raise BrokerError('BROKER_EXIT_CODE_FAILED',
01786                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01787         return int(code.value)
01788 
01789     def close_handle(self, handle):
01790         if not self._api.kernel32.CloseHandle(handle):
01791             raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED',
01792                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01793         # The close was confirmed: the handle leaves the holding table
01794         # of the façade.  A handle that could not be closed stays in it
01795         # with its owner and it is reachable by the recovery.
01796         self._forget(handle)
01797         return True
01798 
01799     # -- the pipes ---------------------------------------------------------
01800     def create_pipe(self, name):
01801         """Create one pipe.  Only the write end is inheritable.
01802 
01803         The security attributes of the pipe say that the ends are
01804         inheritable and the inherit flag of the *read* end is cleared
01805         right afterwards.  A pipe that was created with ``NULL``
01806         security attributes can never be inherited at all.
01807 
01808         Both ends enter the holding table of this façade in the moment
01809         of their creation.  A pipe whose inherit flag could not be
01810         cleared therefore keeps its original ends with their owner: the
01811         core never received the pair and the round never loses the
01812         ownership of a handle that it created.
01813 
01814         The failure of the inherit flag is cleaned up **independently**
01815         of the two ends.  A cancellation of the inherit flag and a
01816         cancellation of the close of one end never skip the close of the
01817         other end: the safe close of both ends always runs and the
01818         cancellation is raised afterwards.  The end that could not be
01819         closed keeps its original reference with the façade and the end
01820         that was closed with a confirmed close leaves the table.
01821         """
01822         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01823         read = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01824         write = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01825         attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
01826         if not self._api.kernel32.CreatePipe(ctypes.byref(read), ctypes.byref(write),
01827                                              ctypes.byref(attributes), 0):
01828             error = self._last_error()
01829             raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_FAILED', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01830         # The moment of the creation of the two ends.
01831         self._retain(read, 'pipe_read')
01832         self._retain(write, 'pipe_write')
01833         primary = None
01834         inherited = False
01835         try:
01836             inherited = bool(self._api.kernel32.SetHandleInformation(
01837                 read, self.HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT, 0))
01838         except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01839             # The cancellation of the inherit flag is the primary error
01840             # of the pipe and it never skips the safe close of the ends.
01841             primary = error
01842         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01843             primary = BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED',
01844                                   'last_error=%d:%s' % (self._last_error(),
01845                                                         type(error).__name__))
01846         if inherited:
01847             return read, write
01848         error = self._last_error()
01849         failures = []
01850         cancellations = []
01851         if self._close_quietly(read, failures, cancellations):
01852             self._forget(read)
01853         if self._close_quietly(write, failures, cancellations):
01854             self._forget(write)
01855         if primary is None:
01856             primary = BrokerError(
01857                 'BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED',
01858                 'last_error=%d%s' % (error,
01859                                      ('|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01860                                      if failures else ''))
01861         else:
01862             try:
01863                 primary.detail = ((getattr(primary, 'detail', None) or '')
01864                                   + '|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01865             except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01866                 pass
01867         if cancellations:
01868             # The cancellation of a close is the error that leaves the
01869             # method and the primary error stays reachable next to it:
01870             # a cancellation never becomes an ordinary evidence and it
01871             # never hides the error that caused the pipe.
01872             cancel = cancellations[0]
01873             try:
01874                 cancel.lvw_primary = primary
01875                 primary.lvw_cancelled = cancel
01876             except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01877                 pass
01878             raise cancel
01879         raise primary
01880 
01881     def read_stream(self, handle, size):
01882         """Read one block of a child stream.
01883 
01884         The end of a stream is an end and not an error, but only in the
01885         branches that Windows documents as the end of a pipe: a broken
01886         pipe, a pipe that is not connected any more, the end of the file
01887         and an operation that was aborted.  Every other failure of the
01888         read is a failure of the round and it is named.
01889         """
01890         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01891         buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
01892         read = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01893         if not self._api.kernel32.ReadFile(handle, buffer, size,
01894                                            ctypes.byref(read), None):
01895             error = self._last_error()
01896             if error in (self.ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE, self.ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED,
01897                          self.ERROR_HANDLE_EOF, self.ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED):
01898                 return b''
01899             raise BrokerError('BROKER_STREAM_READ_FAILED', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01900         return buffer.raw[:read.value]
01901 
01902     def write_stream(self, handle, data):
01903         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01904         buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(bytes(data), len(data))
01905         written = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01906         if not self._api.kernel32.WriteFile(handle, buffer, len(data),
01907                                             ctypes.byref(written), None):
01908             raise BrokerError('BROKER_STREAM_WRITE_FAILED',
01909                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01910         return int(written.value)
01911 
01912     # -- the cancellation of a synchronous operation -----------------------
01913     def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01914         """The original handle of the calling thread.
01915 
01916         The handle is the handle of the thread itself and never a self
01917         reported identifier: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` cancels the
01918         operation of one thread and it needs the real handle of that
01919         thread.
01920         """
01921         ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01922         thread = self._api.kernel32.GetCurrentThread()
01923         process = self._api.kernel32.GetCurrentProcess()
01924         duplicate = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01925         if not self._api.kernel32.DuplicateHandle(process, thread, process,
01926                                                   ctypes.byref(duplicate), 0,
01927                                                   False, self.DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS):
01928             raise BrokerError('BROKER_THREAD_HANDLE_FAILED',
01929                               'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01930         return duplicate
01931 
01932     def cancel_synchronous_io(self, thread_handle):
01933         """Ask for the cancellation of the synchronous operation of a thread.
01934 
01935         The call is a **request** and not a completion.  It may report
01936         ``ERROR_NOT_FOUND`` because the operation had not started yet or
01937         because it had just finished: the caller has to read the real
01938         completion of the thread and to join it.  The return value of
01939         this method names the state of the request.
01940         """
01941         if not self._api.kernel32.CancelSynchronousIo(thread_handle):
01942             error = self._last_error()
01943             if error == self.ERROR_NOT_FOUND:
01944                 return 'NOT_FOUND'
01945             raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCEL_FAILED',
01946                               'last_error=%d' % error)
01947         return 'REQUESTED'
01948

01950 # -- the fixed command of a role ------------------------------------------
01951 
01952 def _base_environment():
01953     environment = {key: value for key, value in os.environ.items()
01954                    if key.upper() not in REMOVED_ENVIRONMENT}
01955     environment['PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE'] = '1'
01956     environment['PYTHONNOUSERSITE'] = '1'
01957     environment['NO_PROXY'] = '127.0.0.1,localhost'
01958     return environment
01959 
01960 
01961 def _comfy_environment(root):
01962     environment = _base_environment()
01963     temporary = str(Path(root) / 'temp' / 'service-os')
01964     environment.update({
01965         'HF_HUB_OFFLINE': '1',
01966         'TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE': '1',
01967         'HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE': '1',
01968         'TEMP': temporary,
01969         'TMP': temporary,
01970         'TORCH_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'torch'),
01971         'HF_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'huggingface'),
01972         'XDG_CACHE_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'cache'),
01973     })
01974     return environment
01975 
01976 
01977 def _ollama_environment():
01978     environment = _base_environment()
01979     environment['OLLAMA_HOST'] = '127.0.0.1:11434'
01980     environment['OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD'] = '1'
01981     return environment
01982 
01983 
01984 def role_command(role, run_root, owned_pids):
01985     """Assemble the fixed command of one role.
01986 
01987     The command form of a role is frozen in this module and it is
01988     assembled from the tools that the plan closed.  The plan never carries
01989     an argument vector, a shell, a script or an environment: it carries
01990     the tools and the closed data of the role, and this function is the
01991     only place where a command line of this package exists.
01992 
01993     The forms are the ones of the already authorised launch of this pack:
01994     the ComfyUI launch of ``reports/v0-s1/wp-s1-03-phase-a/live_probe.py``
01995     and of ``tools/v0_s2_driver.py``, the Ollama launch of the same
01996     wrapper, and the sampler launch of ``tools/v0_s2_runtime.py``.
01997 
01998     The private process ids of the sampler are passed as *separate*
01999     arguments: the parser of the sampler is ``type=int, nargs='+'`` and
02000     one argument that carries a space would not be an integer.
02001     """
02002     name = role['role']
02003     tools = role['tools']
02004     data = role['data']
02005     root = str(run_root)
02006     if name == 'COMFY':
02007         python = tools[0]['path']
02008         main = tools[1]['path']
02009         arguments = ['-s', '-B', main,
02010                      '--listen', '127.0.0.1', '--port', '8191',
02011                      '--base-directory', root,
02012                      '--user-directory', str(Path(root) / data['user_directory']),
02013                      '--disable-auto-launch', '--disable-api-nodes',
02014                      '--disable-all-custom-nodes',
02015                      '--whitelist-custom-nodes', 'lvw_v0_bridge']
02016         return {'executable': python, 'arguments': arguments,
02017                 'working_directory': root, 'environment': _comfy_environment(root)}
02018     if name == 'OLLAMA':
02019         executable = tools[0]['path']
02020         return {'executable': executable, 'arguments': ['serve'],
02021                 'working_directory': root, 'environment': _ollama_environment()}
02022     if name == 'SAMPLER':
02023         python = tools[0]['path']
02024         script = tools[1]['path']
02025         directory = str(Path(root) / 'temp' / 'resource-sampler')
02026         arguments = ['-I', '-B', script, '--execute-sampler',
02027                      '--run-root', directory,
02028                      '--nvidia-smi', data['nvidia_exe'],
02029                      '--binary-sha256', data['nvidia_sha256'],
02030                      '--private-pids'] + [str(pid) for pid in owned_pids] + \
02031                     ['--max-seconds', str(data['max_seconds'])]
02032         return {'executable': python, 'arguments': arguments,
02033                 'working_directory': directory, 'environment': _base_environment()}
02034     raise BrokerError('BROKER_ROLE_UNKNOWN', name)
02035 
02036 
02037 # -- the bounded reader of a child stream ----------------------------------
02038 
02039 class _BoundedStreamReader(threading.Thread):
02040     """One bounded background reader of one stream of a child.
02041 
02042     The thread only reads.  It never writes, it never calls the broker and
02043     it never waits for the broker.  The broker takes the published counts
02044     out of it at its own checkpoints, so the deadline of the round is never
02045     held up by a silent child.
02046 
02047     A read that failed for a reason that Windows does not document as the
02048     end of a pipe is a named error of the round: the reader keeps it and
02049     the round is not a release.
02050     """
02051 
02052     def __init__(self, win32, handle, *, limit, name):
02053         super().__init__(daemon=True, name='lvw-native-custody-stream-' + name)
02054         self.win32 = win32
02055         self.handle = handle
02056         self.limit = limit
02057         self.observed = 0
02058         self.kept = 0
02059         self.overflow = False
02060         self.error = None
02061         self.finished = threading.Event()
02062 
02063     def run(self):
02064         try:
02065             while True:
02066                 block = self.win32.read_stream(self.handle, 65536)
02067                 if not block:
02068                     break
02069                 self.observed += len(block)
02070                 if self.observed > self.limit:
02071                     self.overflow = True
02072                     break
02073                 self.kept += len(block)
02074         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02075             self.error = getattr(error, 'code', None) or type(error).__name__
02076         finally:
02077             self.finished.set()
02078 
02079 
02080 # -- the pending resources of one round ------------------------------------
02081 
02082 def _json_fact(value):
02083     """One fact of the evidence of the round that is always serialisable.
