06589                 except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
06590                     pass
06591                 try:
06592                     # The bound is read **again** for the retry as well:
06593                     # a deadline that was published while the first wait
06594                     # ran shortens the retry too.
06595                     current = self._cleanup_deadline(deadline)
06596                     self._process.wait(timeout=max(0.0, current - self._clock()))
06597                     facts['process_exited'] = True
06598                     facts['exit_code'] = self._process.returncode
06599                 except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
06600                     pass
06601         if not facts['process_handle_released']:
06602             facts.update(self._release_process_handle(facts['process_exited']))
06603         self.close_facts = facts
06604         self.reader_finished = facts['channel_reader_finished']
06605         return facts
06606 
06607     @staticmethod
06608     def _keep_reader_error_history(facts, previous):
06609         """Keep the history of the error of the reader of the channel.
06610 
06611         The **first** error that the reader of the channel really had is
06612         the error of the round: every later close keeps it and it is
06613         never turned into a ``None`` by a repeated close.  The resource
06614         facts of the close may converge, but a real error of the reader
06615         is never lost by the retry: the copy of the facts of a previous
06616         attempt keeps the facts that are ``True`` and it does **not**
06617         keep a code, so the error is restored here from the history
06618         itself.
06619 
06620         A later error is a **secondary** error: it is kept beside the
06621         first one and it never replaces it.  The source of the error is
06622         published with it: ``FIRST_CLOSE`` when the close that is running
06623         now is the one that really observed the error and
06624         ``REPEATED_CLOSE`` when this attempt carries the error of an
06625         earlier one.  The fact of the error itself is the history and it
06626         is never replaced by a boolean.
06627 
06628         The named fact of the cancellation of the close is independent
06629         from this history and it is never merged with it.
06630         """
06631         previous = previous if isinstance(previous, dict) else {}
06632         first = previous.get('channel_reader_error')
06633         current = facts.get('channel_reader_error')
06634         secondary = list(previous.get('channel_reader_secondary_errors') or [])
06635         if first:
06636             if current and current != first:
06637                 # A second error of the reader is kept beside the first
06638                 # one: the first error stays the error of the round.
06639                 if current not in secondary:
06640                     secondary.append(current)
06641             facts['channel_reader_secondary_errors'] = secondary
06642             facts['channel_reader_error'] = first
06643             facts['channel_reader_error_source'] = 'REPEATED_CLOSE'
06644             return facts
06645         if current:
06646             facts['channel_reader_error_source'] = 'FIRST_CLOSE'
06647         return facts
06648 
06649     @staticmethod
06650     def _channel_complete(facts):
06651         """Whether the close of the channel confirmed every item of it.
06652 
06653         The items are the two ends of the channel, the reader of the
06654         channel, the workers of the endpoints and the original handles
06655         of their threads.  A fact that is missing is a missing fact and
06656         not a success.
06657         """
06658         if facts.get('channel_reader_finished') is not True:
06659             return False
06660         if facts.get('read_end_closed') is not True:
06661             return False
06662         if facts.get('write_end_closed') is not True:
06663             return False
06664         if int(facts.get('channel_retained_workers') or 0) != 0:
06665             return False
06666         if facts.get('worker_thread_handle_released') is not True:
06667             return False
06668         return True
06669 
06670     def _close_channel(self, facts):
06671         """Close the channel of the round and report its facts.
06672 
06673         The method returns the facts of the close of the channel and it
06674         never raises: the failure of the close of the channel is a fact
06675         of the round and it is named in the facts.
06676         """
06677         try:
06678             channel_facts = self._channel.close()
06679         except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
06680             facts['channel_closed'] = False
06681             return None
06682         facts['channel_closed'] = True
06683         facts['channel_reader_finished'] = getattr(self._channel,
06684                                                     'reader_finished', None)
06685         if not isinstance(channel_facts, dict):
06686             return None
06687         facts['write_end_closed'] = bool(channel_facts.get('write_end_closed'))
06688         facts['read_end_closed'] = bool(channel_facts.get('read_end_closed'))
06689         facts['channel_reader_error'] = channel_facts.get('reader_error')
06690         # The overflow of the channel is a **named** failure of the
06691         # round and it travels with the facts of the close: a channel
06692         # that overflowed is never a channel that ended normally.
06693         facts['channel_overflow'] = bool(channel_facts.get('overflow'))
06694         facts['channel_overflow_facts'] = dict(
06695             channel_facts.get('overflow_facts') or {})
06696         facts['channel_retained_workers'] = int(
06697             (channel_facts.get('write_retained_workers') or 0)
06698             + (channel_facts.get('read_retained_workers') or 0))
06699         facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = bool(
06700             channel_facts.get('worker_thread_handle_released'))
06701         return channel_facts
06702 
06703     def _cleanup_deadline(self, deadline=None):
06704         """The ONE absolute deadline of the cleanup of the broker.
06705 
06706         The deadline is the one that the parent bound before it closed
06707         the handle.  It may only be shortened, never extended, and every
06708         wait of the cleanup recomputes the time that is left.
06709 
06710         The **context** of the launch is read as well: a cleanup that
06711         runs after the round published its cleanup deadline uses that
06712         deadline and it never falls back to a fresh budget of its own.
06713         """
06714         value = _require_deadline(deadline)
06715         if value is None:
06716             value = self.write_deadline
06717         context = self._context
06718         if context is not None:
06719             for candidate in (context.absolute_deadline,
06720                               context.cleanup_deadline_value()):
06721                 if candidate is None:
06722                     continue
06723                 if value is None or candidate < value:
06724                     value = candidate
06725         if value is None:
06726             value = self._clock() + CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS
06727         elif self.write_deadline is not None and value > self.write_deadline:
06728             value = self.write_deadline
06729         return value
06730 
06731     def _release_process_handle(self, exited=False):
06732         """Release the original process handle, explicitly and by platform.
06733 
06734         The release is recorded and it is never left to the garbage
06735         collector: on Windows the original handle of the process is closed
06736         through its own handle object and on POSIX the child process has
06737         no separate handle that the parent owns.
06738 
06739         The original handle of the process is closed **only** when the
06740         exit of the process was really confirmed.  A process whose exit
06741         was not confirmed may still be alive and its original handle is
06742         the only handle that the parent owns for it: the handle stays
06743         with its owner and a later close may release it.  A close that
06744         succeeds is never a proof that the process ended.
06745         """
06746         if not exited:
06747             return {'process_handle_released': False,
06748                     'process_handle_release_method': 'EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED'}
06749         if os.name == 'nt':
06750             handle = getattr(self._process, '_handle', None)
06751             close = getattr(handle, 'Close', None)
06752             if close is None:
06753                 return {'process_handle_released': False,
06754                         'process_handle_release_method': 'WINDOWS_NO_EXPLICIT_HANDLE'}
06755             try:
06756                 close()
06757             except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
06758                 return {'process_handle_released': False,
06759                         'process_handle_release_method': 'WINDOWS_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED'}
06760             return {'process_handle_released': True,
06761                     'process_handle_release_method': 'WINDOWS_HANDLE_CLOSE'}
06762         return {'process_handle_released': True,
06763                 'process_handle_release_method': 'POSIX_PROCESS_EXIT'}
06764 
06765 
06766 class _LaunchProgress:
06767     """The **monotonic** progress record of the original resources of a launch.
06768 
06769     Every fact of this record is written **immediately** after it was
06770     really confirmed and it is never rewritten: the first value of a
06771     fact is the only value of it.  The record belongs to the **owner**
06772     of the launch and it outlives an interruption of the release: a
06773     later recovery of the same owner reads the facts that were already
06774     confirmed and it never repeats them.
06775 
06776     The record is the reason why a helper that is interrupted before it
06777     returns does not lose the work that it really did.  A return value
06778     of a helper is not a fact: the facts are written **before** the
06779     next cancellable step and not after the return.
06780     """
06781 
06782     def __init__(self):
06783         self._lock = threading.Lock()
06784         self._facts = {}
06785 
06786     def record(self, key, **facts):
06787         """Record the confirmed facts of one original resource."""
06788         with self._lock:
06789             record = self._facts.setdefault(key, {})
06790             for name, value in facts.items():
06791                 record.setdefault(name, value)
06792             return dict(record)
06793 
06794     def fact(self, key):
06795         """The confirmed facts that were recorded for one resource."""
06796         with self._lock:
06797             return dict(self._facts.get(key) or {})
06798 
06799 
06800 class _LaunchAggregate:
06801     """The thin persistent owner of **one** launch.
06802 
06803     Every launch of this package has exactly one aggregate and it is
06804     created **before** the first side effect of the launch.  It holds
06805     the original references of the launch: the process, the channel,
06806     the partial endpoints, the workers of the endpoints and the context
06807     of the launch.
06808 
06809     The aggregate is the entry that the outer owner of a failed launch
06810     uses.  A construction that did not return, a channel whose reader
06811     thread did not start and a channel that could not be handed over
06812     are found **here** and never through a local variable that is
06813     ``None`` at that moment.
06814 
06815     The release of the aggregate is ordered by the **dependency**: the
06816     channel owns the files of the process and the worker of an endpoint
06817     owns the handle of its thread, so the channel is coordinated first
06818     and the process is coordinated after it.  A process whose channel
06819     took the files is never closed by the process helper: a file that
06820     is closed under a live worker is a file that a worker reads from.
06821     """
06822 
06823     def __init__(self, *, launch_id=None, custody=None, clock=None,
06824                  context=None):
06825         self.launch_id = launch_id or 'launch'
06826         self.custody = custody
06827         self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
06828         if context is not None and clock is None:
06829             # The launch shares the clock of the **owner** of its
06830             # context: a deadline of the round is measured with the
06831             # clock of that round and never with the clock of another
06832             # domain.
06833             self._clock = getattr(context, 'clock', None) or self._clock
06834         self._lock = threading.RLock()
06835         self.process = None
06836         self.channel = None
06837         self.endpoints = []
06838         self.workers = []
06839         # The **original raw streams** of the launch that could not be
06840         # closed.  A raw stream is a file of the process of the round:
06841         # the launch that created it is its owner and it is retried by
06842         # that owner alone.  A stream that is not in this list was
06843         # closed with a confirmed close.
06844         self.raw_streams = []
06845         self.context = context
06846         self.facts = {}
06847         # The **progress record** of the original resources of this
06848         # launch.  Every confirmed fact - the exit of the process, the
06849         # release of its original handle and the confirmed close of
06850         # each of its original raw streams - is written into it
06851         # **immediately** after its confirmation.  The record is the
06852         # single owner of those facts: the helper of the process and the
06853         # retry of the raw streams read it and they never close the same
06854         # original twice.
06855         self.progress = _LaunchProgress()
06856         # The **durable ownership history** of the original files of
06857         # this launch.  It is written at the **actual** registration of
06858         # the channel of the launch - the channel registers itself
06859         # before it builds anything - and it is **never** revoked:
06860         # clearing the active slot of the channel does not give the
06861         # ownership of the original files back to the launch and it
06862         # never creates a new raw closer.  A channel that completed
06863         # really closed the original files of the process and a later
06864         # recovery of the same owner may not close them a second time.
06865         self._channel_owns_raw = False
06866         # The **original identities** that the channel of this launch
06867         # took.  They are recorded at the actual registration of the
06868         # channel and they are never revoked: the aggregate may not
06869         # close them, not even after the channel completed.
06870         self._channel_owned_raw = {}
06871         # Whether the exit of the process of this launch was already
06872         # **confirmed**.  A retry of the same owner coordinates only the
06873         # parts that were not confirmed yet: it never terminates and
06874         # never waits a second time.
06875         self.process_exit_confirmed = False
06876 
06877     # -- the bindings of the launch ---------------------------------------
06878     def bind_process(self, process):
06879         """Bind the original process of this launch."""
06880         with self._lock:
06881             self.process = process
06882         return process
06883 
06884     def bind_channel(self, channel):
06885         """Bind the channel of this launch.
06886 
06887         The binding is the **transfer** of the original files of the
06888         process to the channel: the channel is the only owner of them
06889         from now on.  The transfer is recorded in the durable ownership
06890         history of this launch and it is never revoked.
06891         """
06892         with self._lock:
06893             if channel is not None:
06894                 self.channel = channel
06895                 self._record_channel_ownership()
06896         return channel
06897 
06898     def bind_endpoint(self, endpoint):
06899         """Bind one endpoint of this launch."""
06900         with self._lock:
06901             self.endpoints.append(endpoint)
06902         return endpoint
06903 
06904     def bind_worker(self, worker):
06905         """Bind one worker of this launch."""
06906         with self._lock:
06907             self.workers.append(worker)
06908         return worker
06909 
06910     def bind_context(self, context):
06911         """Bind the ONE context of this launch."""
06912         with self._lock:
06913             if context is not None:
06914                 self.context = context
06915         return self.context
06916 
06917     # -- the custody protocol of the launcher -----------------------------
06918     def retain(self, kind, resource, detail=None):
06919         """Take one original resource of this launch.
06920 
06921         The protocol is the protocol of the table of the launcher: the
06922         channel of a launch registers itself **before** it builds
06923         anything, so that a partial channel is always reachable.
06924 
06925         The registration of the channel is also the **actual** transfer
06926         of the original files of the process to the channel: the durable
06927         ownership history of this launch records it and it is never
06928         revoked, not even when the active slot of the channel is
06929         cleared after a **completed** close.
06930         """
06931         with self._lock:
06932             if kind == 'channel' and resource is not None:
06933                 self.channel = resource
06934                 self._record_channel_ownership()
06935             elif kind == 'endpoint':
06936                 self.endpoints.append(resource)
06937             elif kind == 'worker':
06938                 self.workers.append(resource)
06939         return resource
06940 
06941     def _record_channel_ownership(self):
06942         """Record the **actual** transfer of the original files.
06943 
06944         The channel of a launch takes the original standard files of the
06945         process of the round: the process helper may never close them.
06946         The identities that the channel took are recorded here, at the
06947         actual registration of the channel, and they are never revoked:
06948         a channel that completed really closed them and a later
06949         recovery of the same owner may not close them a second time.
06950 
06951         The **identity** of the original file is the authority and not
06952         its name and not a summary of a failure.
06953         """
06954         with self._lock:
06955             self._channel_owns_raw = True
06956             process = self.process
06957             if process is None:
06958                 return
06959             for name in ('stdin', 'stdout', 'stderr'):
06960                 stream = getattr(process, name, None)
06961                 if stream is None:
06962                     continue
06963                 self._channel_owned_raw.setdefault(name, stream)
06964 
06965     def forget(self, resource):
06966         """Leave the launch: the resource was handed over or released."""
06967         with self._lock:
06968             if self.channel is resource:
06969                 self.channel = None
06970             self.endpoints = [item for item in self.endpoints
06971                               if item is not resource]
06972             self.workers = [item for item in self.workers
06973                             if item is not resource]
06974         return None
06975 
06976     def items(self):
06977         """The original resources that this launch still owns."""
06978         with self._lock:
06979             return {'process': self.process, 'channel': self.channel,
06980                     'endpoints': list(self.endpoints),
06981                     'workers': list(self.workers),
06982                     'raw_streams': list(self.raw_streams)}
06983 
06984     # -- the ownership of the original raw streams -------------------------
06985     def retain_raw_stream(self, name, stream):
06986         """Take the ownership of one original raw stream of this launch.
06987 
06988         A raw stream is a file of the process of the round.  It enters
06989         the ownership of **this** launch when its close could not be
06990         confirmed: the launch is then the only owner that may retry it
06991         and the table of the launcher never releases it behind the back
06992         of the launch.
06993         """
06994         with self._lock:
06995             for item in self.raw_streams:
06996                 if item['stream'] is stream:
06997                     return item
06998             item = {'name': name, 'stream': stream}
06999             self.raw_streams.append(item)
07000             return item
07001 
07002     def forget_raw_stream(self, stream):
07003         """Leave the ownership of one raw stream: its close was confirmed."""
07004         with self._lock:
07005             self.raw_streams = [item for item in self.raw_streams
07006                                 if item['stream'] is not stream]
07007         return None
07008 
07009     def pending_raw_streams(self):
07010         """The original raw streams that this launch still owns."""
07011         with self._lock:
07012             return list(self.raw_streams)
07013 
07014     def _register_pending_raw(self, process):
07015         """Register the originals that the helper is about to close.
07016 
07017         The registration happens **before** the first interruptible step
07018         of the helper: an interruption may not lose the identity of an
07019         original and an original that is never visited keeps an
07020         executable same-owner recovery route.
07021 
07022         Only the originals that **this** launch may close are
07023         registered: the originals that the channel took are never
07024         registered, because the channel is their owner.
07025         """
07026         with self._lock:
07027             for name in ('stdin', 'stdout', 'stderr'):
07028                 stream = getattr(process, name, None)
07029                 if stream is None:
07030                     continue
07031                 if self._channel_owned_raw.get(name) is stream:
07032                     # The channel took this original: it is not ours.
07033                     continue
07034                 record = self.progress.fact('stream:' + name)
07035                 if record.get('stream') is stream and record.get('closed'):
07036                     # The close was already confirmed: it is never
07037                     # registered and it is never repeated.
07038                     continue
07039                 self.retain_raw_stream(name, stream)
07040 
07041     def _reconcile_pending_raw(self, process, close_pipes):
07042         """Reconcile the registered originals with the facts of the helper.
07043 
07044         An original whose close was confirmed is forgotten by its owner
07045         and by the table of the launcher.  Every other original - the
07046         one whose close failed and the one that was **never visited** -
07047         keeps its **original identity** and its owner, so that the same
07048         owner may really retry it.  The return value names the originals
07049         that are still unresolved.
07050         """
07051         unresolved = []
07052         if not close_pipes:
07053             return unresolved
07054         with self._lock:
07055             owned = dict(self._channel_owned_raw)
07056         for name in ('stdin', 'stdout', 'stderr'):
07057             stream = getattr(process, name, None)
07058             if stream is None:
07059                 continue
07060             if owned.get(name) is stream:
07061                 continue
07062             record = self.progress.fact('stream:' + name)
07063             if record.get('stream') is stream and record.get('closed'):
07064                 # The close was confirmed: the original is forgotten by
07065                 # its owner and by the table of the launcher.
07066                 self.forget_raw_stream(stream)
07067                 if self.custody is not None:
07068                     self.custody.forget(stream)
07069                 continue
07070             unresolved.append((name, stream))
07071         return unresolved
07072 
07073     # -- the coordinated release ------------------------------------------
07074     def _release_deadline(self, deadline=None):
07075         """The absolute deadline that the release of this launch uses.
07076 
07077         The deadline of the caller and the deadlines of the **original**
07078         context of this launch are one value: the earliest of them.  The
07079         context is read **again** at the moment of the release and it is
07080         never copied into a number of the launch: a layer that copied
07081         the number of a deadline would have a deadline of its own.
07082         """
07083         return self._release_bound(deadline)[0]
07084 
07085     def _release_bound(self, deadline=None, clock=None):
07086         """The absolute deadline of this release and the clock of it.
07087 
07088         The deadline of the caller and the deadlines of the **original**
07089         context of this launch are one value: the earliest of them.  The
07090         clock of the domain of that value is returned with it: a
07091         deadline that belongs to the context of the round is compared in
07092         the clock of that context and never in the clock of another
07093         domain.  A facade that shares the deadline of the owner and not
07094         its clock compares two numbers of two domains.
07095         """
07096         try:
07097             value = _require_deadline(deadline)
07098         except BrokerError:
07099             value = None
07100         owner_clock = clock or self._clock
07101         context = self.context
07102         if context is not None:
07103             for candidate in (context.absolute_deadline,
07104                               context.cleanup_deadline_value()):
07105                 if candidate is None:
07106                     continue
07107                 if value is None or candidate < value:
07108                     value = candidate
07109                     owner_clock = getattr(context, 'clock', None) or owner_clock
07110         return value, owner_clock
07111 
07112     def release(self, deadline=None, clock=None):
07113         """Coordinate the resources of this launch, in the dependency order.
07114 
07115         The channel is coordinated **first** and the process is
07116         coordinated after it.  A close that returned is not a release:
07117         the channel is released only when **every** fact of it is
07118         closed - the reader, the two ends, the workers and the original
07119         handles of their threads.  An incomplete close keeps the
07120         channel with its owner and it is named.
07121 
07122         The original handle of the process is released only after the
07123         exit of the process was really confirmed.
07124 
07125         The release never raises before every safe step was executed:
07126         the original cancellation of the launch is raised at the end and
07127         the ordinary failures are named beside it.
07128         """
07129         released = []
07130         retained = []
07131         failures = {}
07132         cancellations = []
07133         # The original raw streams that this launch already owns and
07134         # whose close was not confirmed yet: they are retried by **this**
07135         # owner and by no other one.
07136         pending_raw = self.pending_raw_streams()
07137         channel = self.channel
07138         if channel is not None:
07139             facts = {}
07140             try:
07141                 facts = channel.close()
07142             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
07143                 cancellations.append(error)
07144             except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07145                 failures['channel'] = type(error).__name__
07146             if not isinstance(facts, dict):
07147                 facts = {}
07148             complete = (bool(facts.get('reader_finished'))
07149                         and bool(facts.get('read_end_closed'))
07150                         and bool(facts.get('write_end_closed'))
07151                         and int(facts.get('channel_retained_workers') or 0) == 0
07152                         and bool(facts.get('worker_thread_handle_released')))
07153             if complete:
07154                 released.append('channel')
07155                 self.forget(channel)
07156                 if self.custody is not None:
07157                     self.custody.forget(channel)
07158             else:
07159                 # A close that returned is not a release: the channel
07160                 # keeps its owner and its original reference.
07161                 failures['channel'] = 'BROKER_CHANNEL_CLOSE_INCOMPLETE'
07162                 retained.append('channel')
07163                 if self.custody is not None:
07164                     self.custody.retain('channel', channel,
07165                                          'release_incomplete', launch=self)
07166         process = self.process
07167         if process is not None:
07168             # The files of the process belong to the channel when the
07169             # channel took them: the process helper never closes them.
07170             # The **durable ownership history** of this launch is the
07171             # authority and not the presence of the channel in the
07172             # active slot: a channel that completed really closed the
07173             # original files of the process and a later release of the
07174             # same owner may not close them a second time.
07175             close_pipes = channel is None and not self._channel_owns_raw
07176             outcome = {'failures': {}, 'exited': False, 'retained_streams': []}
07177             # The original raw streams that **this** launch owns are
07178             # closed by the retry of the raw streams below and by no
07179             # other route: the helper of the process and the retry
07180             # never close the same original.
07181             owned = {item['name']: item['stream'] for item in pending_raw}
07182             # The deadline of the launch is read **again** after the
07183             # channel was coordinated: a cleanup deadline that was
07184             # published in the meantime shortens the wait of the
07185             # process and it is never a number that was copied once.
07186             bound, bound_clock = self._release_bound(deadline, clock)
07187             # The originals that the helper is about to close are
07188             # registered with **this** owner **before** the first
07189             # interruptible step: an interruption may not lose them and
07190             # an original that was never visited keeps an executable
07191             # same-owner recovery route.
07192             if close_pipes:
07193                 self._register_pending_raw(process)
07194             # The original references that the helper could not confirm
07195             # are collected **outside** of its report: a helper that is
07196             # interrupted before it returns does not return its report
07197             # and the unconfirmed references may not be lost with it.
07198             helper_retained = []
07199             try:
07200                 outcome = _release_launch_process(
07201                     process, deadline=bound,
07202                     close_pipes=close_pipes,
07203                     exited=self.process_exit_confirmed,
07204                     context=self.context, progress=self.progress,
07205                     owned_streams=owned, clock=bound_clock,
07206                     retained_out=helper_retained, failures_out=failures)
07207             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
07208                 cancellations.append(error)
07209             except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07210                 outcome['failures']['process'] = type(error).__name__
07211             failures.update(outcome['failures'])
07212             # The **facts** of the process are read from the progress
07213             # record of this launch and not only from the return value
07214             # of the helper: a helper that was interrupted before it
07215             # returned does not lose the exit or the handle that it
07216             # really confirmed.
07217             record = self.progress.fact('process')
07218             if record.get('exit_confirmed'):
07219                 self.process_exit_confirmed = True
07220             # A raw stream that could not be closed stays a reachable
07221             # owned resource: it keeps its **original** reference and it
07222             # is never replaced by a summary of the failure.  This
07223             # launch is the owner of it and it retries it.
07224             retained_raw = list(outcome.get('retained_streams') or [])
07225             retained_raw.extend(helper_retained)
07226             # The originals whose close **failed** are known: they were
07227             # exported by the helper as it happened.  Every other
07228             # original that is still unresolved was never visited.
07229             failed_ids = {id(stream) for _name, stream in retained_raw}
07230             # The originals that the helper was about to close are
07231             # reconciled with the facts of the progress record: a
07232             # confirmed close is forgotten and every other original -
07233             # failed **or** never visited - keeps its identity and its
07234             # owner, so that the same owner may really retry it.
07235             for name, stream in self._reconcile_pending_raw(process,
07236                                                              close_pipes):
07237                 if not any(known[1] is stream for known in retained_raw):
07238                     retained_raw.append((name, stream))
07239             for name, stream in retained_raw:
07240                 retained.append(name)
07241                 self.retain_raw_stream(name, stream)
07242                 if self.custody is not None:
07243                     # The detail is a label and never the authority:
07244                     # the **identity** of the original is the authority.
07245                     # A close that failed and a close that was never
07246                     # attempted are named apart.
07247                     detail = ('close_failed' if id(stream) in failed_ids
07248                               else 'close_unconfirmed')
07249                     self.custody.retain(name, stream, detail, launch=self)
07250             # The original process is forgotten **only** when its exit
07251             # was confirmed and the original handle of the process was
07252             # released.  A close of the handle that failed keeps the
07253             # process with its owner and the same owner may retry it.
07254             handle_released = bool(record.get('handle_released'))
07255             if self.process_exit_confirmed and handle_released:
07256                 released.append('process')
07257                 with self._lock:
07258                     self.process = None
07259                 if self.custody is not None:
07260                     self.custody.forget(process)
07261             else:
07262                 retained.append('process')
07263                 if self.custody is not None:
07264                     self.custody.retain('process', process, 'release_failed',
07265                                          launch=self)
07266         # The retry of the original raw streams that this launch owns.
07267         # The retry happens **after** the channel and the process were
07268         # coordinated: a raw file of a process whose channel took the
07269         # files is never closed by the launch.
07270         self._release_raw_streams(pending_raw, released, retained, failures,
07271                                   cancellations, deadline, clock)
07272         self.facts = {'released': list(released), 'retained': list(retained),
07273                       'failures': dict(failures)}
07274         if cancellations:
07275             # The original cancellation is raised after every safe step
07276             # of the release was executed and the ordinary failures stay
07277             # named next to it.
07278             raise cancellations[0]
07279         return self.facts
07280 
07281     def _release_raw_streams(self, items, released, retained, failures,
07282                              cancellations, deadline, clock=None):
07283         """Retry the original raw streams that this launch still owns.
07284 
07285         A raw stream is a file of the process of the round.  It is owned
07286         by the launch that created it and it is retried **only** by that
07287         owner: the table of the launcher never closes it and it never
07288         becomes a detached entry that nobody may reach.
07289 
07290         The retry happens under the **current** bound of the release:
07291         the deadline of the caller and the deadlines of the original
07292         context of the launch are read again **before every close** and
07293         an expired bound never starts a new close.
07294 
07295         A stream that is owned by a channel - a live channel or a
07296         partial one - is never closed by the launch: the files of the
07297         process belong to the channel and a file that is closed under a
07298         live worker is a file that a worker reads from.
07299 
07300         A stream whose close is confirmed is forgotten **before** it
07301         leaves the table of the launcher: the confirmed close is
07302         recorded in the progress record of this launch and the table
07303         never resurrects the stream.  A confirmed close is never
07304         repeated by a later release of the same owner.
07305         """
07306         if not items:
07307             return
07308         if self.channel is not None:
07309             # The channel owns the files of the process: the raw
07310             # streams are never closed under a live or a partial
07311             # channel endpoint.
07312             for item in items:
07313                 retained.append(item['name'])
07314                 failures['raw_stream'] = 'BROKER_RAW_STREAM_OWNED_BY_CHANNEL'
07315             return
07316         for item in items:
07317             stream = item['stream']
07318             name = item['name']
07319             # The originals that the channel of this launch took are
07320             # **never** closed by the launch: the ownership history of
07321             # the launch is durable and a channel that completed really
07322             # closed them.
07323             if self._channel_owned_raw.get(name) is stream:
07324                 retained.append(name)
07325                 failures['raw_stream'] = 'BROKER_RAW_STREAM_OWNED_BY_CHANNEL'
07326                 continue
07327             # The confirmed close of an original is **never** repeated:
07328             # the progress record of this launch is the owner of that
07329             # fact and it outlives every later release.
07330             record = self.progress.fact('stream:' + name)
07331             if record.get('stream') is stream and record.get('closed'):
07332                 self.forget_raw_stream(stream)
07333                 if self.custody is not None:
07334                     self.custody.forget(stream)
07335                 released.append(name)
07336                 continue
07337             # The bound of the release is read **again** before **each**
07338             # close: a bound that was tightened while the previous close
07339             # ran refuses this one and it never opens a cleanup lease of
07340             # its own.
07341             value, bound_clock = self._release_bound(deadline, clock)
07342             if value is not None and bound_clock() >= value:
07343                 # An expired bound never starts a new close: the stream
07344                 # keeps its owner and the round keeps the fact.
07345                 failures['raw_stream'] = 'BROKER_RAW_STREAM_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
07346                 retained.append(name)
07347                 continue
07348             try:
07349                 stream.close()
07350             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
07351                 # The original cancellation survives: it is raised after
07352                 # every safe step of the release and the stream keeps
07353                 # its owner.
07354                 cancellations.append(error)
07355                 failures['raw_stream'] = 'CANCELLED'
07356                 retained.append(name)
07357                 continue
07358             except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07359                 failures['raw_stream'] = type(error).__name__
07360                 retained.append(name)
07361                 continue
07362             # The close was confirmed: the fact is recorded **before**
07363             # the next cancellable step and the stream is forgotten by
07364             # its owner and by the table of the launcher.
07365             self.progress.record('stream:' + name, stream=stream, closed=True)
07366             self.forget_raw_stream(stream)
07367             if self.custody is not None:
07368                 self.custody.forget(stream)
07369             released.append(name)
07370 
07371 
07372 class _LauncherCustody:
07373     """The persistent owner of the original resources of a failed launch.
07374 
07375     The launcher owns the original process of the round until the
07376     channel of the round exists.  A channel that could not be built
07377     would leave the launcher with a running process and with pipes that
07378     nobody owns.
07379 
07380     Every original resource of such a launch enters this table **with
07381     its original reference** and it stays there until a confirmed
07382     release: the round never loses the ownership of a resource that it
07383     created, and a pid is never used to find a process again.
07384 
07385     The table is the recovery entry point of the launcher.  It is
07386     explicit and it is finite: a caller that wants the resources of a
07387     failed launch released asks for it.
07388 
07389     The resources of a launch are coordinated by the **aggregate** of
07390     that launch and not by the order of this table: the channel of a
07391     launch is closed before the process of that launch, because the
07392     channel owns the files of the process.  A resource that belongs to
07393     a launch is never released a second time by this table.
07394     """
07395 
07396     def __init__(self):
07397         self._items = []
07398         self._lock = threading.Lock()
07399         # The ONE context of the round, when the table is bound to it.
07400         # A recovery that runs without a deadline of its own reads the
07401         # absolute deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the
07402         # round from it and it never opens a fresh budget.
07403         self._context = None
07404 
07405     def bind_context(self, context):
07406         """Bind the ONE context of the round to this table."""
07407         if context is not None:
07408             self._context = context
07409         return self._context
07410 
07411     def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
07412         """The ONE deadline that a release of this table really uses."""
07413         return self._effective_bound(deadline)[0]
07414 
07415     def _effective_bound(self, deadline=None):
07416         """The ONE deadline of a release of this table and its clock.
07417 
07418         The clock of the domain of the deadline is returned with it: a
07419         deadline that belongs to the context of the round is measured
07420         with the clock of that context and never with the clock of
07421         another domain.
07422         """
07423         value = _require_deadline(deadline)
07424         owner_clock = time.monotonic
07425         context = self._context
07426         if context is not None:
07427             for candidate in (context.absolute_deadline,
07428                               context.cleanup_deadline_value()):
07429                 if candidate is None:
07430                     continue
07431                 if value is None or candidate < value:
07432                     value = candidate
07433                     owner_clock = getattr(context, 'clock', None) or owner_clock
07434         return value, owner_clock
07435 
07436     def retain(self, kind, resource, detail=None, launch=None):
07437         """Take one original resource into the table of the launcher.
07438 
07439         The table never names the same resource twice: a resource that
07440         is already with its owner is not entered a second time and the
07441         table converges.
07442 
07443         The ``launch`` is the aggregate that owns the resource.  A
07444         resource that belongs to a launch is coordinated by that
07445         launch and it is never released a second time by this table.
07446         """
07447         with self._lock:
07448             for item in self._items:
07449                 if item['kind'] == kind and item['resource'] is resource:
07450                     return resource
07451             self._items.append({'kind': kind, 'resource': resource,
07452                                 'detail': detail, 'launch': launch})
07453         return resource
07454 
07455     def forget(self, resource):
07456         """Leave the table: the resource was handed over to its owner."""
07457         with self._lock:
07458             remaining = [item for item in self._items
07459                          if item['resource'] is not resource]
07460             self._items = remaining
07461 
07462     def items(self):
07463         """The original resources that the launcher still owns."""
07464         with self._lock:
07465             return list(self._items)
07466 
07467     def release(self, deadline=None, context=None):
07468         """Try to release every original resource of the table.
07469 
07470         The release of every resource is independent of the others: a
07471         resource that could not be released stays in the table with its
07472         original reference and it is named in the report.
07473 
07474         The **launches** are coordinated first: every aggregate closes
07475         the channel of its launch before the process of that launch.
07476         The resources that belong to no launch are released afterwards
07477         and a resource of a launch is never released twice.
07478 
07479         The wait of every resource uses the **same** absolute deadline.
07480         A recovery never opens a budget of its own and it never waits a
07481         fixed number of seconds for a process that may still be alive.
07482         """
07483         released = []
07484         retained = []
07485         cancellations = []
07486         with self._lock:
07487             items = list(self._items)
07488         keep = []
07489         for item in items:
07490             if item['kind'] == 'aggregate':
07491                 launch = item['resource']
07492                 bound, bound_clock = self._effective_bound(deadline)
07493                 try:
07494                     # The deadline of the table is read **again** for
07495                     # every launch: a cleanup deadline that was
07496                     # published in the meantime shortens the release of
07497                     # the launch.  The clock of its domain travels with
07498                     # it: a launch never compares a deadline of the
07499                     # round with a clock of another domain.
07500                     report = launch.release(deadline=bound, clock=bound_clock)
07501                 except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
07502                     cancellations.append(error)
07503                     report = {'released': [], 'retained': ['launch']}
07504                 except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07505                     report = {'released': [], 'retained': ['launch']}
07506                 released.extend(report.get('released') or [])
07507                 names = list(report.get('retained') or [])
07508                 retained.extend(names)
07509                 if names:
07510                     keep.append(item)
07511                 continue
07512             if item.get('launch') is not None:
07513                 # The launch of this resource coordinates it: the table
07514                 # never releases it a second time.  A resource that the
07515                 # launch released **while** this release was running is
07516                 # not named by the table any more: the stale snapshot of
07517                 # the table never resurrects it and never names it as
07518                 # retained.
07519                 with self._lock:
07520                     owned = any(current['resource'] is item['resource']
07521                                 for current in self._items)
07522                 if not owned:
07523                     continue
07524                 keep.append(item)
07525                 retained.append(item['kind'])
07526                 continue
07527             resource = item['resource']
07528             try:
07529                 if item['kind'] == 'process':
07530                     bound, bound_clock = self._effective_bound(deadline)
07531                     outcome = _release_launch_process(
07532                         resource, deadline=bound, close_pipes=True,
07533                         context=self._context, clock=bound_clock)
07534                     if outcome['failures']:
07535                         keep.append(item)
07536                         retained.append(item['kind'])
07537                         # A raw stream that could not be closed stays a
07538                         # reachable owned resource of the launcher.
07539                         for name, stream in outcome.get('retained_streams') or []:
07540                             self.retain(name, stream, 'close_failed')
07541                         continue
07542                 else:
07543                     close = getattr(resource, 'close', None)
07544                     if callable(close):
07545                         close()
07546             except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07547                 keep.append(item)
07548                 retained.append(item['kind'])
07549                 continue
07550             released.append(item['kind'])
07551         with self._lock:
07552             # The table is rebuilt from the entries that are **still**
07553             # in it: an entry that was forgotten while the release ran -
07554             # a process that the aggregate of its launch released - is
07555             # never resurrected as a detached entry.  The entries that
07556             # were added while the release ran are new resources of the
07557             # round and they are kept.
07558             kept = {id(item['resource']) for item in keep}
07559             snapshot = {id(item['resource']) for item in items}
07560             self._items = [item for item in self._items
07561                            if id(item['resource']) not in snapshot
07562                            or id(item['resource']) in kept]
07563         if cancellations:
07564             raise cancellations[0]
07565         return {'released': list(released), 'retained': list(retained)}
07566 
07567 
07568 #: The one table of the original resources of the launches of this
07569 #: package.  It is empty after a launch that was handed over and it
07570 #: names the resources of a launch that was not.
07571 _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY = _LauncherCustody()
07572 
07573 
07574 def release_retained_launches(deadline=None, *, context=None):
07575     """The recovery entry point of the launcher of this package.
07576 
07577     The caller asks for the original resources of the launches that were
07578     not handed over to be released, and the report names the resources
07579     that could not be released.
07580 
07581     The wait of every resource uses the **same** absolute deadline of the
07582     round: a recovery never opens a budget of its own and it never
07583     waits a fixed number of seconds for a process that may still be
07584     alive.
07585 
07586     The deadline of the context is **read again** here: a recovery
07587     that runs after the round published its cleanup deadline uses that
07588     deadline and it never falls back to a fresh budget of its own.
07589     """
07590     value = _require_deadline(deadline)
07591     if context is not None:
07592         _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.bind_context(context)
07593     return _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.release(deadline=value)
07594 
07595 
07596 def _launcher_custody_items():
07597     """The original resources that the launcher of this package owns.
07598 
07599     The list is a copy and every entry carries the **original** object:
07600     a pid is never used to find a process of the round again.
07601     """
07602     return _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.items()
07603 
07604 
07605 def _release_minimum(deadline=None, context=None, clock=None):
07606     """The **current** absolute minimum of a release and the clock of it.
07607 
07608     The deadline of the caller, the absolute deadline of the original
07609     context and the **published** cleanup deadline of the round are one
07610     value: the earliest of them.  The value is read **again** at every
07611     call: a context that was tightened while a previous step of the
07612     release ran shortens the bound of the next one.
07613 
07614     The clock of the domain of that value is returned with it: a
07615     deadline that belongs to the context of the round is measured with
07616     the clock of that context and never with the clock of another
07617     domain.
07618     """
07619     try:
07620         value = _require_deadline(deadline)
07621     except BrokerError:
07622         value = None
07623     owner_clock = clock or time.monotonic
07624     if context is not None:
07625         for candidate in (context.absolute_deadline,
07626                           context.cleanup_deadline_value()):
07627             if candidate is None:
07628                 continue
07629             if value is None or candidate < value:
07630                 value = candidate
07631                 owner_clock = getattr(context, 'clock', None) or owner_clock
07632     return value, owner_clock
07633 
07634 
07635 def _release_budget(deadline=None, context=None, clock=None):
07636     """The seconds that are left for the wait of a process of a launch.
07637 
07638     The deadline of the caller and the deadlines of the **original**
07639     context of the launch are one value: the earliest of them.  The
07640     context is read **again** at the moment of the wait: a cleanup
07641     deadline that was published while the channel of the launch was
07642     closed shortens the wait of the process.  A recovery never opens a
07643     budget of its own and it never waits a fixed number of seconds for
07644     a process that may still be alive.
07645 
07646     The bound of the package is used only when neither the caller nor
07647     the context of the launch brought a deadline at all.
07648     """
07649     value, owner_clock = _release_minimum(deadline, context, clock)
07650     if value is None:
07651         return CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
07652     # The clock is the clock of the domain of the deadline: a deadline
07653     # that belongs to the context of the round is measured with the
07654     # clock of that context.
07655     return max(0.0, value - owner_clock())
07656 
07657 
07658 def _release_launch_process(process, *, deadline=None, close_pipes=True,
07659                             exited=None, context=None, progress=None,
07660                             owned_streams=None, clock=None, retained_out=None,
07661                             failures_out=None):
07662     """Terminate one process of a launch and report the two facts.
07663 
07664     The release is explicit and it is bounded: the process is
07665     terminated, its exit is confirmed and its pipes are closed when
07666     nobody else owns them.
07667 
07668     The wait uses the **same** absolute deadline of the round: the
07669     deadline is read **again** from the context of the launch at the
07670     moment of the wait and a recovery never opens a budget of its own.
07671 
07672     The ``exited`` argument is the exit that an **earlier** attempt of
07673     the same owner already confirmed: a retry coordinates only the
07674     parts that were not confirmed yet and it never terminates and
07675     never waits a second time.
07676 
07677     The original handle of the process is closed **only** when its exit
07678     was really confirmed.  A process that may still be alive keeps its
07679     handle and its owner: the handle is the only thing that may stop
07680     it and it is never closed under it.
07681 
07682     Every **confirmed** fact is written into the ``progress`` record of
07683     the owner **immediately** after its confirmation and before the next
07684     cancellable step.  The record is the reason why an interruption
07685     that prevents this helper from returning does not lose the work
07686     that it really did: a return value is not a fact.
07687 
07688     The original raw streams that the **owner** already owns are never
07689     closed here: the ``owned_streams`` argument names them and the
07690     retry of the owner closes them.  The helper and the retry never
07691     close the same original and a confirmed close is never repeated.
07692 
07693     The failures are written into the ``failures_out`` dictionary of
07694     the owner **as they happen**: a helper that is interrupted before it
07695     returns does not return its report and an ordinary failure that
07696     happened before the interruption may not be lost with it.
07697 
07698     The report names every step that did not succeed and it names the
07699     **exit** of the process as a separate fact: a close that succeeded
07700     is never a proof that the process ended.  A raw stream that could
07701     not be closed is returned with its **original** reference: it stays
07702     a reachable owned resource and it is never replaced by a summary.
07703     """
07704     failures = failures_out if isinstance(failures_out, dict) else {}
07705     retained_streams = []
07706     owned = owned_streams if isinstance(owned_streams, dict) else {}
07707     if progress is not None:
07708         confirmed = bool(progress.fact('process').get('exit_confirmed'))
07709     else:
07710         confirmed = False
07711     confirmed = confirmed or bool(exited)
07712     if not confirmed:
07713         try:
07714             process.terminate()
07715         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07716             failures['terminate'] = type(error).__name__
07717         budget = _release_budget(deadline, context, clock=clock)
07718         try:
07719             process.wait(timeout=budget)
07720             confirmed = True
07721         except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07722             failures['wait'] = type(error).__name__
07723     if confirmed:
07724         # The exit is a fact of the owner and it is recorded before the
07725         # next cancellable step: a later release of the same owner
07726         # never terminates and never waits a second time.
07727         if progress is not None:
07728             progress.record('process', exit_confirmed=True)
07729     if confirmed and os.name == 'nt':
07730         # The original handle of the process is closed explicitly and it
07731         # is never left to the garbage collector.  A handle that could
07732         # not be closed keeps its owner.
07733         handle = getattr(process, '_handle', None)
07734         closer = getattr(handle, 'Close', None)
07735         if callable(closer):
07736             try:
07737                 closer()
07738             except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07739                 failures['handle'] = type(error).__name__
07740             else:
07741                 if progress is not None:
07742                     progress.record('process', handle_released=True)
07743         elif progress is not None:
07744             # There is no original handle to release: the handle of the
07745             # process is not a resource of this platform and its
07746             # absence is not a failure.
07747             progress.record('process', handle_released=True)
07748     elif confirmed and progress is not None:
07749         progress.record('process', handle_released=True)
07750     if close_pipes:
07751         for name in ('stdin', 'stdout', 'stderr'):
07752             stream = getattr(process, name, None)
07753             if stream is None:
07754                 continue
07755             if owned.get(name) is stream:
07756                 # The owner of this original closed it in its own
07757                 # retry: the helper never closes it a second time.
07758                 continue
07759             if progress is not None:
07760                 record = progress.fact('stream:' + name)
07761                 if record.get('stream') is stream and record.get('closed'):
07762                     # The close was confirmed by an earlier release of
07763                     # the same owner: it is never repeated.
07764                     continue
07765             # The **current** minimum of the release is read **again**
07766             # before **each** initial close: the deadline of the caller,
07767             # the absolute deadline of the original context and the
07768             # **published** cleanup deadline of the round are one value
07769             # and they are read at the moment of the close.  A context
07770             # that was tightened by the wait of the process or by a
07771             # previous close of an original is honoured here: an
07772             # expired bound never starts a new close and the original
07773             # keeps its owner and its identity.
07774             current, current_clock = _release_minimum(deadline, context,
07775                                                       clock)
07776             if current is not None and current_clock() >= current:
07777                 failures[name] = 'BROKER_RAW_STREAM_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
07778                 retained_streams.append((name, stream))
07779                 if retained_out is not None:
07780                     retained_out.append((name, stream))
07781                 continue
07782             try:
07783                 stream.close()
07784             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
07785                 # The original cancellation survives and the facts that
07786                 # were confirmed before it are kept: the stream keeps
07787                 # its owner and its original reference.  The reference
07788                 # is handed to the owner **before** the cancellation
07789                 # leaves, because a helper that does not return does
07790                 # not return its report either.
07791                 if retained_out is not None:
07792                     retained_out.append((name, stream))
07793                 raise
07794             except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07795                 failures[name] = type(error).__name__
07796                 # The stream keeps its **original** reference: it stays
07797                 # a reachable owned resource of the round.  The failure
07798                 # is exported **as it happens** and not only with the
07799                 # report of the helper: an interruption that follows
07800                 # this failure may not lose it.
07801                 retained_streams.append((name, stream))
07802                 if retained_out is not None:
07803                     retained_out.append((name, stream))
07804                 continue
07805             if progress is not None:
07806                 progress.record('stream:' + name, stream=stream, closed=True)
07807     return {'failures': failures, 'exited': confirmed,
07808             'retained_streams': retained_streams}
07809 
07810 
07811 def _terminate_and_close_process(process, *, deadline=None, close_pipes=True,
07812                                  context=None):
07813     """Terminate one process of this round and close its pipes.
07814 
07815     The release is explicit and it is bounded: the process is
07816     terminated, its exit is confirmed and its pipes are closed.  The
07817     method never raises: it is the last step of a failure and it never
07818     replaces the failure that is on the way out.
07819 
07820     The wait uses the **same** absolute deadline of the round: a
07821     recovery never opens a budget of its own and it never waits a fixed
07822     number of seconds for a process that may still be alive.
07823 
07824     The original handle of the process is closed **only** when its exit
07825     was really confirmed.  A process that may still be alive keeps its
07826     handle and its owner: the handle is the only thing that may stop
07827     it and it is never closed under it.
07828 
07829     The report names every step that did not succeed, so that the
07830     caller can keep the original reference of a resource that is still
07831     open.
07832     """
07833     return _release_launch_process(process, deadline=deadline,
07834                                    close_pipes=close_pipes,
07835                                    context=context)['failures']
07836 
07837 
07838 def _is_startup_refusal(error):
07839     """Whether the error is a refusal of the admission of the launch.
07840 
07841     A refusal of the admission is **not** a failure of the resources of
07842     the launch: the process that was already admitted keeps its owner
07843     in the table of the launcher and it stays reachable and recoverable
07844     by the recovery of the launcher.  The launch never pretends to undo
07845     the operating system work that it really admitted and it never
07846     skips the registration of its original resource.
07847     """
07848     code = getattr(error, 'code', None)
07849     return code in ('BROKER_STARTUP_CANCELLED',
07850                     'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED')
07851 
07852 
07853 def _adopt_broker_process(process, *, channel_factory, executable,
07854                           executable_sha256, deadline=None, aggregate=None,
07855                           custody=None):
07856     """Take the original process of the broker into the custody of a channel.
07857 
07858     The launcher owns the original process of the round until the
07859     channel of the round exists.  A channel that could not be built
07860     would leave the launcher with a running process and with pipes that
07861     nobody owns.
07862 
07863     The **channel** is the only owner of the files of the process.  A
07864     channel that exists and that could not be handed over is closed by
07865     the launcher through the channel and never by closing the standard
07866     input and the standard output of the process by itself: a file that
07867     is closed under a live worker of the channel is a file that a
07868     worker is reading from.
07869 
07870     The **aggregate** of the launch is the owner that the outer code
07871     uses: a partial channel - a channel whose reader thread did not
07872     start - is found through the aggregate and never through a local
07873     variable that is ``None`` at that moment.
07874 
07875     A resource that could not be released keeps its **original**
07876     reference in the table of the launcher: the session never received
07877     the process and the round never loses the ownership of it.
07878 
07879     The handover happens exactly once: the process that the channel
07880     received is the process that the parent owns.
07881     """
07882     table = custody if custody is not None else _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY
07883     launch = aggregate if aggregate is not None else _LaunchAggregate(
07884         custody=table)
07885     launch.bind_process(process)
07886     # The launch is registered with the table of the launcher: the
07887     # recovery of the table coordinates the resources of the launch in
07888     # the dependency order - the channel before the process.
07889     table.retain('aggregate', launch, 'launch')
07890     table.retain('process', process, 'popened', launch=launch)
07891     channel = None
07892     try:
07893         channel = channel_factory()
07894         if channel is not None:
07895             launch.bind_channel(channel)
07896         broker = BrokerProcess(process, executable, executable_sha256, channel,
07897                                context=launch.context)
07898     except BaseException as error:
07899         # The resources of the launch are coordinated by the aggregate:
07900         # the channel first and the process after it.  The resources
07901         # that could not be released keep their original reference and
07902         # their owner.
07903         #
07904         # A **refusal of the admission** of the launch is not a failure
07905         # of the resources of the launch: the process that was already
07906         # admitted keeps its owner in the table of the launcher and it
07907         # stays reachable and recoverable by the recovery of the
07908         # launcher.  The launch never pretends to undo the operating
07909         # system work that it really admitted.
07910         if not _is_startup_refusal(error):
07911             try:
07912                 launch.release(deadline=deadline)
07913             except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as cancellation:
07914                 # The cancellation of the release never replaces the
07915                 # original failure of the launch: the original object
07916                 # continues to propagate and the cancellation stays
07917                 # **reachable** next to it.  The primary failure and the
07918                 # cancellation of the recovery coexist.
07919                 try:
07920                     error.lvw_cancelled = cancellation
07921                 except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07922                     pass
07923             except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
07924                 pass
07925         raise
07926     # The handover happened: the launcher does not own the process of
07927     # the round any more.
07928     table.forget(process)
07929     table.forget(channel)
07930     table.forget(launch)
07931     launch.forget(channel)
07932     launch.forget(process)
07933     return broker
07934 
07935 
07936 def _check_launch_budget(context, stage):
07937     """Check the budget of the round before a side effect of a launch.
07938 
07939     The check is asked **before** the first side effect of the launch
07940     - the creation of the directory, the writing of the plan and the
07941     creation of the process - and it is asked **again** after the
07942     preparatory work and before the next effect.  A launch that starts
07943     after the deadline of the round arrived never creates a process.
07944 
07945     The **original** context of the round is the authority: the
07946     launcher never keeps a copy of the deadline and it never opens a
07947     budget of its own.  The authority is the **effective minimum** of
07948     the context - the absolute deadline of the round **and** the
07949     published cleanup deadline of the round - and not the absolute
07950     deadline alone: a round that published its cleanup deadline and
07951     whose cleanup expired is a round that may not create a process any
07952     more, even when its absolute lease is still in the future.  The
07953     stop of the round, the cancel of the round and the unknown state of
07954     the round are honoured here as well.
07955     """
07956     if context is None:
07957         return True
07958     if context.cancel.is_set() or context.is_unknown():
07959         raise BrokerError('BROKER_STARTUP_CANCELLED', stage)
07960     if context.stopping.is_set():
07961         # The public stop of the round published the stop of the round:
07962         # a launch that continues after it would create a process that
07963         # nobody may stop any more.
07964         raise BrokerError('BROKER_STARTUP_CANCELLED', stage)
07965     deadline = context.effective_deadline()
07966     if deadline is not None and context.clock() >= deadline:
07967         raise BrokerError('BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED', stage)
07968     return True
07969 
07970 
07971 def launch_broker(plan, run_root, nonce, *, context=None):
07972     """Start the broker process of one round and keep its original handle.
07973 
07974     The launcher is a Windows only operation.  It refuses on every other
07975     platform before anything is started: a broker that cannot be started
07976     on Windows is never replaced by an unconstrained process and never by
07977     a simulation that pretends to be one.
07978 
07979     The nonce and the plan are checked **before** the first directory and
07980     the first file of the round exist, so that a refused launch leaves
07981     nothing behind.
07982 
07983     The ONE context of the round is held **before** the first side
07984     effect of the launch: the creation of the process, the reading of
07985     the executable, the channel and its endpoints all read the deadline
07986     of the round from that context.  A launcher that waits for the end
07987     of the launcher to bind the deadline would leave a window in which
07988     the process of the round runs without a budget.
07989 
07990     The **aggregate** of the launch is created before the process and it
07991     holds every original resource of the launch: a failure at any point
07992     of the launch finds the partial owner of the launch through it.
07993     """
07994     if os.name != 'nt':
07995         raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLATFORM_UNSUPPORTED')
07996     if plan is None:
07997         raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLAN_INVALID')
07998     if not re.fullmatch(r'[0-9a-f]{32}', str(nonce)):
07999         raise BrokerError('BROKER_NONCE_INVALID')
08000     # The aggregate of this launch and the deadline of the round are
08001     # taken **before** the first side effect of the launch.
08002     launch = _LaunchAggregate(custody=_LAUNCHER_CUSTODY, context=context)
08003     launch.bind_context(context)
08004     # The table of the launcher reads the deadline of the round from
08005     # the same context: a recovery of the launcher never opens a fresh
08006     # budget of its own.
08007     _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.bind_context(context)
08008     _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.retain('aggregate', launch, 'launch')
08009     deadline = None
08010     if context is not None:
08011         deadline = context.absolute_deadline
08012     root = Path(run_root)
08013     raw = plan.sealed_bytes()
08014     digest = plan.sealed_sha256()
08015     directory = root / 'temp' / 'native'
08016     # The budget of the round is checked **before** the first side
08017     # effect of the launch: a launch that starts after the deadline of
08018     # the round arrived creates no directory and no file.
08019     _check_launch_budget(context, 'LAUNCH:BEFORE_DIRECTORY')
08020     directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
08021     plan_path = directory / 'custody-plan.json'
08022     _check_launch_budget(context, 'LAUNCH:BEFORE_PLAN')
08023     with plan_path.open('xb') as stream:
08024         stream.write(raw)
08025     command = build_broker_command(plan_path=plan_path, plan_sha256=digest,
08026                                    run_root=root, nonce=nonce)
08027     # The budget is checked **again** after the preparatory work and
08028     # before the creation of the process: a plan file that consumed the
08029     # whole lease of the round never produces a process.
08030     _check_launch_budget(context, 'LAUNCH:BEFORE_POPEN')
08031     process = subprocess.Popen(command, stdin=subprocess.PIPE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
08032                                stderr=None, close_fds=True)
08033     # The original process of the round is with the launcher from the
08034     # moment of its creation.  The window between the creation of the
08035     # process and the handover of the channel is not a window without an
08036     # owner: a cancellation of the reading of the executable leaves the
08037     # process with its owner and it never disappears.
08038     launch.bind_process(process)
08039     _LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.retain('process', process, 'popened', launch=launch)
08040     executable = Path(command[0])
08041     # The **next** distinct effect of the launch is the read of the
08042     # executable.  The public stop of the round, the cancel of the
08043     # round, the unknown state of the round and the **current**
08044     # effective minimum of the round are asked again **before** it: a
08045     # stop that was published while the process was admitted refuses the
08046     # hash.  The process of the round is **not** undone - it keeps its
08047     # owner in the table of the launcher and it stays reachable and
08048     # recoverable by the recovery of the launcher.
08049     _check_launch_budget(context, 'LAUNCH:BEFORE_HASH')
08050     try:
08051         executable_sha256 = hashlib.sha256(executable.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
08052     except OSError:
08053         executable_sha256 = None
08054     except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
08055         # The cancellation of the reading of the executable is cleaned
08056         # up and it continues to propagate.  The process keeps its owner
08057         # in the table of the launcher and the recovery of the launcher
08058         # is the entry that releases it.
08059         raise
08060
