00315 # -- N05-R03: the ends of a pipe are owned from their creation ------------
00316 
00317 def test_n05_r03_the_ends_of_a_pipe_are_owned_from_the_moment_of_their_creation():
00318     """N05-R03 (M05): both ends of a pipe have an owner.
00319 
00320     Obligation: the ends of a pipe are registered from the moment of
00321     their creation, the end whose close failed stays recoverable and the
00322     end that was closed is closed exactly once.  Entry:
00323     ``WindowsApi.create_pipe``.  Trigger: the inherit flag of the read
00324     end could not be cleared.  Oracle: the core never received the
00325     pair and the round never loses the ownership of the two ends.
00326     Negative: an end that was closed with a confirmed close leaves the
00327     table.  Returned state: the refusal names the failure of the
00328     inherit flag.  Salvage: the recovery of the owner closes the end
00329     that is still open.
00330     """
00331     def create_pipe(kernel32, *args):
00332         read = kernel32.allocate('pipe_read')
00333         write = kernel32.allocate('pipe_write')
00334         kernel32.pipe_read_handle = read
00335         args[0]._obj.value = read
00336         args[1]._obj.value = write
00337         return 1
00338 
00339     def refuse_inherit(kernel32, *args):
00340         return 0
00341 
00342     def fail_first_read_close(kernel32, *args):
00343         handle = getattr(args[0], 'value', args[0])
00344         if handle == kernel32.pipe_read_handle:
00345             # The close of the read end fails the first time only: a
00346             # later recovery of the owner may close it.
00347             attempts = kernel32.__dict__.setdefault('close_attempts', {})
00348             attempts[handle] = attempts.get(handle, 0) + 1
00349             if attempts[handle] == 1:
00350                 return 0
00351         kernel32.live.pop(handle, None)
00352         return 1
00353 
00354     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe,
00355                                          'SetHandleInformation': refuse_inherit,
00356                                          'CloseHandle': fail_first_read_close})
00357     kernel32.next_handle = 700
00358     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00359     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
00360         api.create_pipe('stdout')
00361     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED'
00362     # The end that was closed with a confirmed close left the table and
00363     # the end that could not be closed is still with its owner.
00364     retained = api.retained_handles()
00365     assert [entry['kind'] for entry in retained] == ['pipe_read']
00366     # The façade keeps the **original** object of the end: the handle of
00367     # a pipe is a ``ctypes`` object and that type is not hashable, so
00368     # the membership is checked with the number that the kernel knows.
00369     # The production is not changed to make the test easier: the handle
00370     # stays the object that the façade created.
00371     retained_handle = retained[0]['handle']
00372     assert getattr(retained_handle, 'value', retained_handle) == kernel32.pipe_read_handle
00373     assert getattr(retained_handle, 'value', retained_handle) in kernel32.live
00374     # The identity of the original end is never replaced by a copy: the
00375     # façade hands out the very object that it holds.
00376     assert api.retained_handles()[0]['handle'] is retained_handle
00377     # The owner of the end is the façade and its recovery is the entry
00378     # that releases it.
00379     assert retained[0]['owner'] == 'WINDOWS_API'
00380     # The recovery of the owner reaches the end that is still open.
00381     report = api.release_retained()
00382     assert [item['kind'] for item in report] == ['pipe_read']
00383     assert api.retained_handles() == []
00384     assert not kernel32.live
00385 
00386 
00387 # -- N05-R04: a cancellation in the release of the core -------------------
00388 
00389 def test_n05_r04_a_cancellation_in_the_release_propagates_after_the_cleanup(tmp_path):
00390     """N05-R04 (M06): a cancellation is not an ordinary evidence.
00391 
00392     Obligation: the finalizers of the release of the core run on their
00393     own and the cancellation propagates after the cleanup.  Entry:
00394     ``BrokerCore.stop`` and ``BrokerCore._close_handle``.  Trigger: the
00395     close of one handle raises a cancellation.  Oracle: the later
00396     finalizers still ran and the cancellation is the error that leaves
00397     the method.  Negative: an ordinary failure of a close is named and
00398     it does not stop the release.  Returned state: the cancellation is
00399     named as a cancellation in the failures of the core.  Salvage: the
00400     handles that are still open keep their owner.
00401     """
00402     class CancellingWin32(RecordingWin32):
00403         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
00404             super().__init__(**kwargs)
00405             self.cancelled = False
00406             self.cancelled_handle = None
00407 
00408         def close_handle(self, handle):
00409             self.calls.append(('close_handle', handle))
00410             if (self.cancelled_handle is not None
00411                     and handle == self.cancelled_handle
00412                     and not self.cancelled):
00413                 self.cancelled = True
00414                 raise KeyboardInterrupt('close_cancelled')
00415             super().close_handle(handle)
00416 
00417     win32 = CancellingWin32()
00418     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00419     core.arm()
00420     core.launch()
00421     job = core.job
00422     # The close of the job handle of the round raises a cancellation.
00423     win32.cancelled_handle = job
00424     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
00425         core.stop('STOP')
00426     # The finalizers that follow the cancellation still ran.
00427     assert win32.closed, 'the handles that follow the cancellation were not closed'
00428     assert 'job_process_ids' in win32.names()
00429     # The cancellation is named as a cancellation and it is not only an
00430     # ordinary evidence of the round.
00431     cancellations = [item for item in core.failures if item.get('cancellation')]
00432     assert cancellations, 'the cancellation was not named'
00433     assert any(item['stage'] == 'release_job' for item in cancellations)
00434     # The pending pass of the cleanup closed the job handle for real.
00435     # The bookkeeping of the core agrees with that fact: a handle that
00436     # is closed is never named by the job of the round any more.
00437     assert job not in win32.live, 'the job handle was not really closed'
00438     assert core.job is None, 'the core names a handle that is closed'
00439     assert core.job_closed is True
00440     assert core._pending.pending() == []
00441     assert not [item for item in core._retained_resources
00442                 if item['handle'] is job], \
00443         'the core retains a handle that was really closed'
00444 
00445     # -- a close that fails temporarily is retried by the pending pass -----
00446     class TransientWin32(RecordingWin32):
00447         """A façade whose first close of every handle fails."""
00448 
00449         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
00450             super().__init__(**kwargs)
00451             self.failed_handles = set()
00452 
00453         def close_handle(self, handle):
00454             self.calls.append(('close_handle', handle))
00455             if handle not in self.failed_handles:
00456                 self.failed_handles.add(handle)
00457                 raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
00458             super().close_handle(handle)
00459 
00460     win32 = TransientWin32()
00461     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00462     core.arm()
00463     core.launch()
00464     job = core.job
00465     # The eligibility of the control handle of a process is a
00466     # **callable** over the original record and never a frozen copy of
00467     # the fact: the pending pass asks it at the moment of its close and
00468     # it reads the current exit fact of the record.  A frozen answer
00469     # would protect the handle for ever.
00470     for item in core._pending.pending():
00471         if item['kind'] == 'process':
00472             assert callable(item['closeable']), \
00473                 'the close eligibility of the control handle was frozen'
00474             assert item['closeable']() is False, \
00475                 'the eligibility of a live process is not closeable'
00476     handles = [record['process_handle'] for record in core.records]
00477     try:
00478         core.stop('STOP')
00479         # The pending pass retried the close and it succeeded: the fact
00480         # of the close is the fact of the round.
00481         assert not win32.live, 'the pending pass did not close the handles'
00482         for handle in handles:
00483             assert win32.closed.count(handle) == 1, \
00484                 'the control handle of a process was not closed exactly once'
00485         assert core.job is None
00486         assert core.job_closed is True
00487         assert core._pending.pending() == []
00488         # The records of the round agree with the close of their handles.
00489         for record in core.records:
00490             assert record['process_handle'] is None
00491             assert record['thread_handle'] is None
00492             assert record['process_handle_closed'] is True
00493             assert record['thread_handle_closed'] is True
00494         # Nothing that was really closed is retained any more.
00495         assert core._retained_resources == []
00496         assert not [item for item in core._retained_resources
00497                     if item['handle'] is job]
00498     finally:
00499         # The salvage of the test owner: the handles that the product
00500         # could not close are released by the owner of the façade.
00501         for handle in list(win32.live):
00502             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
00503 
00504     # -- a close that keeps failing keeps its owner ------------------------
00505     class PersistentWin32(RecordingWin32):
00506         """A façade whose close of every handle fails."""
00507 
00508         def close_handle(self, handle):
00509             self.calls.append(('close_handle', handle))
00510             raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
00511 
00512     win32 = PersistentWin32()
00513     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00514     core.arm()
00515     core.launch()
00516     job = core.job
00517     try:
00518         core.stop('STOP')
00519         # The handle that could not be closed keeps its owner and it is
00520         # named: it is never replaced by a copy of an error message.
00521         assert core.job is job
00522         assert job in win32.live
00523         retained = [item for item in core._retained_resources
00524                     if item['handle'] is job]
00525         assert retained, 'the handle that could not be closed was not named'
00526         assert retained[0]['owner'] == 'CORE_RETAINED'
00527         assert retained[0]['code'] == 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED'
00528         # The handle is still pending and it still has its owner.
00529         pending = [item for item in core._pending.pending()
00530                    if item['handle'] is job]
00531         assert pending, 'the handle that could not be closed lost its owner'
00532         assert pending[0]['owner'] == 'CORE'
00533     finally:
00534         # The salvage of the test owner: the handles that the product
00535         # could not close are released by the owner of the façade.
00536         for handle in list(win32.live):
00537             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
00538 
00539 
00540 # -- N05-R05: one process never skips the others -------------------------
00541 
00542 def test_n05_r05_one_process_never_skips_the_wait_of_the_next_one(tmp_path):
00543     """N05-R05 (M06): the processes of a round are independent.
00544 
00545     Obligation: the wait of one original process never skips the wait
00546     of the next one and the shared deadline is recomputed before every
00547     wait.  Entry: ``BrokerCore.stop`` and ``BrokerCore._wait_the_processes``.
00548     Trigger: the wait of the first process raises an ordinary error.
00549     Oracle: the later process was waited for and released.  Negative:
00550     an expired deadline never starts a new wait.  Returned state: the
00551     failure of the first process is named and the round stays unknown.
00552     Salvage: the handles of the round are released by their owner.
00553 
00554     RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 11): the old group ended with the
00555     blanket assertion that **every** handle of the round was closed.
00556     That assertion contradicted the obligation N10-R01: the control
00557     handle of a process whose exit was **not** confirmed may not be
00558     closed, because it is the only handle that may still stop that
00559     process.  The expectation of the group is therefore **split by the
00560     actual facts** and it is not weakened:
00561 
00562     * the process whose exit was really confirmed is released and its
00563       control handle is closed **exactly once**;
00564     * the process whose wait failed keeps its **original** control
00565       handle: the handle is still in the record and still in the
00566       pending table with its owner, it is **not** closed, and the
00567       round stays ``UNKNOWN_RETAINED``;
00568     * the later process and the independent handles of the round (the
00569       thread handles, the pipes and the job) are released and the
00570       original error of the first wait is preserved;
00571     * the recovery of the **same** owner succeeds only after an
00572       explicit **new** proof of the exit, inside the **same** unextended
00573       cleanup deadline; the deadline of the cleanup is never extended
00574       and the salvage of the owner of the test is recorded apart and
00575       it is never a product PASS.
00576     """
00577     class FailingWaitWin32(RecordingWin32):
00578         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
00579             super().__init__(**kwargs)
00580             self.failed = False
00581 
00582         def wait_process(self, process, timeout_ms):
00583             self.calls.append(('wait_process', process, timeout_ms))
00584             if not self.failed:
00585                 self.failed = True
00586                 raise RuntimeError('wait_failed')
00587             for entry in self.created:
00588                 if entry['handle'] == process:
00589                     return entry['exited']
00590             return True
00591 
00592     win32 = FailingWaitWin32()
00593     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00594     core.arm()
00595     core.launch()
00596     first_record = core.records[0]
00597     second_record = core.records[1]
00598     unconfirmed = first_record['process_handle']
00599     confirmed = second_record['process_handle']
00600     try:
00601         evidence = core.stop('STOP')
00602         # The failure of the first wait is named and the group was not
00603         # interrupted: the second process was waited for as well.
00604         assert any(item.get('error_type') == 'RuntimeError'
00605                    for item in core.failures), \
00606             'the original error of the first wait was lost'
00607         waited = [call for call in win32.calls if call[0] == 'wait_process']
00608         assert len(waited) >= 2, 'the wait of the second process never ran'
00609         assert evidence['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
00610         # -- the confirmed exit: the control handle of the second
00611         #    process is closed exactly once --------------------------
00612         assert second_record['exited'] is True, \
00613             'the exit of the second process was not confirmed'
00614         assert second_record['process_handle_closed'] is True
00615         assert win32.closed.count(confirmed) == 1, \
00616             'the control handle of the confirmed process was closed twice'
00617         assert confirmed not in win32.live
00618         # -- the unconfirmed exit: the control handle of the first
00619         #    process keeps its owner and it is NOT closed -----------
00620         assert first_record['exited'] is False, \
00621             'the exit of the first process was claimed without a proof'
00622         assert first_record['process_handle'] is unconfirmed, \
00623             'the control handle was forgotten while the exit was unknown'
00624         assert first_record['process_handle_closed'] is False
00625         assert unconfirmed in win32.live, \
00626             'the control handle was closed while the exit was unknown'
00627         pending = [item for item in core._pending.pending()
00628                    if item['handle'] == unconfirmed]
00629         assert pending, 'the control handle lost its owner'
00630         assert pending[0]['kind'] == 'process'
00631         assert pending[0]['owner'] == 'CORE'
00632         retained = [item for item in core._retained_resources
00633                     if item['handle'] is unconfirmed]
00634         assert retained, 'the control handle was not named'
00635         assert retained[0]['code'] == 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED'
00636         # -- the independent handles are released --------------------
00637         for record in core.records:
00638             assert record['thread_handle_closed'] is True
00639             assert record['thread_handle'] is None
00640         assert core.job_closed is True
00641         assert core.job is None
00642         # The **only** handle that is still open is the control handle
00643         # of the process whose exit was never confirmed.
00644         assert sorted(win32.live) == [unconfirmed], \
00645             'a handle was closed or kept without the proof of the exit'
00646 
00647         # -- the recovery: the same owner confirms the exit inside the
00648         #    SAME unextended deadline and the handle is closed once --
00649         published = core.cleanup_deadline()
00650         core.stop('STOP')
00651         assert core.cleanup_deadline() == published, \
00652             'the cleanup deadline was extended by the recovery'
00653         assert first_record['exited'] is True, \
00654             'the recovery did not confirm the exit of the process'
00655         assert first_record['process_handle_closed'] is True
00656         assert first_record['process_handle'] is None
00657         assert win32.closed.count(unconfirmed) == 1, \
00658             'the control handle was closed twice'
00659         assert unconfirmed not in win32.live
00660         assert core._pending.pending() == []
00661     finally:
00662         # The salvage of the owner of the test is bounded and it never
00663         # hides a resource that the product kept.
00664         for handle in list(win32.live):
00665             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
00666

00668 # -- N05-R06: a reader that never started is never joined ----------------
00669 
00670 def test_n05_r06_a_reader_that_never_started_is_never_joined(tmp_path):
00671     """N05-R06 (M06): the readers of a round are coordinated one by one.
00672 
00673     Obligation: a reader whose thread never started is not joined, the
00674     readers are coordinated one after the other and the read end of a
00675     live reader is never closed under it.  Entry:
00676     ``BrokerCore._start_reader`` and ``BrokerCore._close_readers``.
00677     Trigger: the start of the first reader raises before the thread
00678     exists.  Oracle: the later reader is coordinated and the read end of
00679     the live reader keeps its owner.  Negative: a reader that never
00680     started is not a live reader and its handle is closed with a
00681     confirmed close.  Returned state: the round names the reader that
00682     was not stopped.  Salvage: the owner of the live reader closes it
00683     later.
00684     """
00685     import tools.v0_s3_native_broker as broker_module
00686 
00687     class FailingReader:
00688         """A reader whose thread never starts."""
00689 
00690         def __init__(self, win32, handle, *, limit, name):
00691             self.win32 = win32
00692             self.handle = handle
00693             self.limit = limit
00694             self.observed = 0
00695             self.kept = 0
00696             self.overflow = False
00697             self.error = None
00698             self.name = name
00699             self.ident = None
00700             self.finished = threading.Event()
00701 
00702         def start(self):
00703             raise RuntimeError('reader_start_failed')
00704 
00705         def is_alive(self):
00706             return False
00707 
00708         def join(self, timeout=None):
00709             raise RuntimeError('cannot join thread before it is started')
00710 
00711     original = broker_module._BoundedStreamReader
00712     broker_module._BoundedStreamReader = FailingReader
00713     try:
00714         win32 = RecordingWin32()
00715         core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00716         core.arm()
00717         record = {'role': 'COMFY', 'pending_handles': []}
00718         core.readers.append(FailingReader(win32, 0x31, limit=1024, name='never-started'))
00719         with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
00720             core._start_reader(record, 0x32, 'stdout')
00721         # The reader that never started is not joined: the join of a
00722         # thread that was not started is an error of the caller.
00723         core._close_readers()
00724     finally:
00725         broker_module._BoundedStreamReader = original
00726     states = core.reader_states
00727     assert states, 'the state of the readers was not recorded'
00728     assert states[0]['started'] is False
00729     assert states[0]['alive'] is False
00730     # The handle of a reader that never started is closed with a
00731     # confirmed close: no thread of the round reads it.
00732     assert 0x31 in win32.closed
00733     assert 0x32 in win32.closed
00734

00736 # -- N05-R07: a background start that did not return ---------------------
00737 
00738 def test_n05_r07_a_background_start_that_did_not_return_keeps_its_owner(tmp_path):
00739     """N05-R07 (M07): a start that did not return keeps the thread.
00740 
00741     Obligation: the original owner of a background thread that is
00742     still starting is always kept, a late thread may only wind up and
00743     the deadline of the round is never reset.  Entry:
00744     ``NativeCustodySession._start_background_thread`` and
00745     ``NativeCustodySession._stop_background``.  Trigger: the start of
00746     the reader never returns and the cleanup expires.  Oracle: the
00747     session keeps the reference, the round is unknown and the late
00748     thread never does business IO.  Negative: the deadline of the
00749     cleanup is never renewed.  Returned state: the pending start is
00750     named.  Salvage: the late thread winds up at once.
00751     """
00752     class LatePump(_ChannelPump):
00753         """A pump whose start does not return before it is released."""
00754 
00755         def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
00756             super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
00757             self.entered = threading.Event()
00758             self.gate = threading.Event()
00759 
00760         def start(self):
00761             self.entered.set()
00762             self.gate.wait(10.0)
00763             super().start()
00764 
00765     class RecordingBroker:
00766         def __init__(self):
00767             self.reads = 0
00768 
00769         def read(self, timeout):
00770             self.reads += 1
00771             return None
00772 
00773     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
00774     session, _holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=clock)
00775     broker = RecordingBroker()
00776     pump = LatePump(broker, decode=lambda raw: raw, clock=clock)
00777     starter = threading.Thread(target=session._start_background_thread,
00778                                args=('pump', pump))
00779     starter.start()
00780     assert pump.entered.wait(5.0), 'the start of the reader never blocked'
00781     # The deadline of the cleanup of the round arrives while the start
00782     # of the reader is still in flight.
00783     deadline = session._cleanup_deadline_for()
00784     clock.value += 10.0
00785     session._stop_background(deadline)
00786     evidence = session.evidence()
00787     assert 'CUSTODY_BACKGROUND_START_PENDING' in evidence['failures']
00788     assert evidence['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
00789     # The original owner of the thread is kept: the reference was never
00790     # dropped and the deadline of the cleanup was never renewed.
00791     assert session._pump is pump
00792     assert session._cleanup_deadline_for() == deadline
00793     # The late thread may only wind up: it never reads the channel.
00794     pump.gate.set()
00795     starter.join(5.0)
00796     for _ in range(100):
00797         if broker.reads:
00798             break
00799         time.sleep(0.01)
00800     assert broker.reads == 0, 'the late reader did business IO'
00801 
00802 
00803 # -- N05-R08: a start that raised after the thread really started --------
00804 
00805 def test_n05_r08_a_start_that_raised_after_the_thread_started_keeps_the_thread(tmp_path):
00806     """N05-R08 (M07): the real state of a thread decides, not a flag.
00807 
00808     Obligation: a thread that really runs keeps the reference of the
00809     session even when its start raised.  Entry:
00810     ``NativeCustodySession._start_background_thread``.  Trigger: the
00811     start of the thread raises after the thread really started.
00812     Oracle: the session keeps the live thread and the round is
00813     unknown.  Negative: a thread that never started is not a live
00814     thread and its reference is dropped.  Returned state: the failed
00815     start is named.  Salvage: the owner of the thread stops it inside
00816     the deadline of the cleanup.
00817     """
00818     class StartedThenFailed(threading.Thread):
00819         """A reader that really starts and whose start then raises.
00820 
00821         The stand-in implements the **whole** interface of the reader of
00822         the channel: the evidence of the round reads ``error`` and
00823         ``alive`` and the stop of the round reads ``stop`` and
00824         ``join``.  A stand-in that does not implement it would hide the
00825         real facts of the product behind an ``AttributeError`` and the
00826         production evidence is never weakened to accommodate it.
00827         """
00828 
00829         def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
00830             super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
00831             self.gate = threading.Event()
00832             self.error = None
00833             self.finished = threading.Event()
00834             self.joins = []
00835             self.stops = 0
00836 
00837         def run(self):
00838             self.gate.wait(5.0)
00839             self.finished.set()
00840 
00841         def start(self):
00842             super().start()
00843             raise RuntimeError('start_failed_after_start')
00844 
00845         @property
00846         def alive(self):
00847             return self.is_alive()
00848 
00849         def stop(self):
00850             self.stops += 1
00851             self.gate.set()
00852 
00853         def join(self, timeout=None):
00854             self.joins.append(timeout)
00855             return super().join(timeout)
00856 
00857         def join_bounded(self, seconds):
00858             self.join(seconds)
00859             return not self.is_alive()
00860 
00861     session, _holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
00862     thread = StartedThenFailed(daemon=True, name='lvw-native-custody-late')
00863     try:
00864         with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
00865             session._start_background_thread('pump', thread)
00866         evidence = session.evidence()
00867         assert 'CUSTODY_BACKGROUND_START_FAILED:pump' in evidence['failures']
00868         assert evidence['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
00869         # The live thread keeps the reference of the session: the local
00870         # flag of the start never took it for a thread that never started.
00871         assert session._pump is thread
00872         assert thread.is_alive()
00873         # The reader interface of the stand-in reports the truth of the
00874         # thread: the evidence reads it and it never claims a fact that
00875         # the thread does not have.
00876         assert evidence['pump_alive'] is True
00877         assert evidence['pump_error'] is None
00878         assert evidence['background_threads'][0]['alive'] is True
00879         assert evidence['background_threads'][0]['started'] is True
00880         # The owner of the thread stops it inside the deadline of the
00881         # cleanup.
00882         thread.gate.set()
00883         session._stop_background(session._cleanup_deadline_for())
00884         assert session._pump is None
00885         assert thread.stops == 1
00886         assert thread.joins, 'the reader was never joined'
00887     finally:
00888         # The salvage of the test owner: the thread of the stand-in is
00889         # released and joined, whatever the checks above did.  The
00890         # salvage never changes the fact of the product above.
00891         thread.gate.set()
00892         thread.join(5.0)
00893 
00894 
00895 # -- N05-R09: a closer that could not be built or started ----------------
00896 
00897 def test_n05_r09_a_closer_that_could_not_start_does_not_latch_the_round(tmp_path):
00898     """N05-R09 (M07): a closer that fails is named and stays recoverable.
00899 
00900     Obligation: the construction and the start of the closer are named,
00901     the waiters end and the original broker stays recoverable.  Entry:
00902     ``NativeCustodySession._start_bounded_close``.  Trigger: the
00903     construction of the closer raises.  Oracle: the failure is named,
00904     the round is unknown and the original broker keeps its owner.
00905     Negative: the latch of the closer is not permanent: a later attempt
00906     may try again.  Returned state: the state of the close is not
00907     ``IN_PROGRESS`` for ever.  Salvage: the public stop of the round
00908     closes the broker.
00909     """
00910     class FailingThread:
00911         def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
00912             raise RuntimeError('closer_construction_failed')
00913 
00914     session, holder, clock = make_session(
00915         tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0),
00916         close_facts=dict(COMPLETE_CLOSE_FACTS))
00917     session.start()
00918     peer = holder['peer']
00919     with unittest.mock.patch('tools.v0_s3_native_custody.threading.Thread',
00920                               FailingThread):
00921         assert session._start_bounded_close() is False
00922     evidence = session.evidence()
00923     assert any('CUSTODY_CLOSER_START_FAILED' in failure
00924                for failure in evidence['failures'])
00925     assert evidence['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
00926     # The close never began: the waiters are not latched and the
00927     # original broker keeps its owner.
00928     assert evidence['close_state'] != 'IN_PROGRESS'
00929     assert session.broker is peer
00930     assert peer.closed == 0
00931     # The latch of the closer is not permanent: a later attempt may try
00932     # again and the public stop of the round is the recovery entry.
00933     assert session._closer_started is False
00934     session.stop()
00935     assert peer.closed == 1
00936     assert session.broker is None

00939 # -- N05-R10: a launch that could not be handed over ---------------------
00940 
00941 def test_n05_r10_a_launch_that_could_not_be_handed_over_keeps_its_resources(tmp_path):
00942     """N05-R10 (M07): the launcher keeps the original resources.
00943 
00944     Obligation: the launcher registers the original process, its
00945     handles, its pipes and its endpoint and it has a finite recovery
00946     entry point.  Entry: ``_adopt_broker_process`` and
00947     ``release_retained_launches``.  Trigger: the channel could not be
00948     built and the termination, the wait and the close of the pipes
00949     failed.  Oracle: the original objects are with their owner and the
00950     session that never took over does not lose the ownership.  Negative:
00951     a pid is never used to find a process again.  Returned state: the
00952     failure of the adoption is the error that leaves.  Salvage: the
00953     recovery of the launcher is explicit and it names what it kept.
00954 
00955     CORRECTION 12 (M07-A): a raw stream that the launch could not
00956     close is owned by the **launch** and the **same** owner retries it.
00957     A retry that fails keeps the owner of the stream and names the
00958     failure: a stream is never forgotten without a confirmed close.
00959 
00960     RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 14, F1): the group expected the table
00961     of the launcher to be empty in the release that closed the last
00962     original.  The **current** source does not do that and it does not
00963     have to: the reconciliation of the registered originals names the
00964     unresolved ones as retained **before** the retry of the same
00965     release closes them, so the report of that release names a
00966     retained name that is already closed when the report is read.  The
00967     owner that the table keeps is therefore an **empty bookkeeping
00968     owner** and it converges with **one** further explicit release of
00969     the **same** owner under the **same** positive deadline.  That
00970     release performs **zero** close, terminate and wait.  The group
00971     proves the empty owner, records the intermediate table honestly and
00972     it never empties the table by hand, never renews a lease and never
00973     loops until the table is green.  The original reference, count and
00974     fact checks of the group are unchanged.
00975     """
00976     class BrokenProcess:
00977         pid = 4242
00978         returncode = None
00979 
00980         def __init__(self):
00981             self.terminated = 0
00982             self.waited = 0
00983             self.stdin = type('S', (), {'close': self._fail_close})()
00984             self.stdout = type('S', (), {'close': self._fail_close})()
00985 
00986         def _fail_close(self):
00987             raise RuntimeError('pipe_close_failed')
00988 
00989         def terminate(self):
00990             self.terminated += 1
00991             raise RuntimeError('terminate_failed')
00992 
00993         def wait(self, timeout=None):
00994             self.waited += 1
00995             raise RuntimeError('wait_failed')
00996 
00997     process = BrokenProcess()
00998 
00999     def broken_channel():
01000         raise RuntimeError('channel_construction_failed')
01001 
01002     with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
01003         _adopt_broker_process(process, channel_factory=broken_channel,
01004                               executable='C:/python.exe', executable_sha256='a' * 64)
01005     # The launcher tried to release the process and it named the
01006     # failures of every step.
01007     assert process.terminated == 1
01008     assert process.waited == 1
01009     # The original resources are with the table of the launcher and they
01010     # are the **original** objects: no pid is used to find them again.
01011     items = _launcher_custody_items()
01012     kinds = sorted(item['kind'] for item in items)
01013     assert 'process' in kinds
01014     assert 'stdin' in kinds
01015     assert 'stdout' in kinds
01016     process_entry = [item for item in items if item['kind'] == 'process'][0]
01017     assert process_entry['resource'] is process
01018     # The recovery of the launcher is explicit and it is finite.
01019     report = release_retained_launches()
01020     assert 'process' in report['retained']
01021     assert _launcher_custody_items()
01022 
01023     # -- N1 correction 12 (M07-A): a **failed** retry keeps the owner of
01024     #    the raw stream and names the failure.  A stream is never
01025     #    forgotten without a confirmed close.
01026     holder = m07a_interrupted_launch(tmp_path, sealed_plan(), failures=2)
01027     stdout = holder['stdout']
01028     aggregate = holder['aggregate'][0] if holder['aggregate'] else None
01029     assert aggregate is not None, 'the interrupted launch lost its owner'
01030     release_retained_launches()
01031     assert stdout.attempts == 1, \
01032         'the raw stream was not closed by the first release'
01033     assert stdout.confirmed == 0
01034     release_retained_launches()
01035     assert stdout.attempts == 2, \
01036         'the same owner did not retry the raw stream of the launch'
01037     assert stdout.confirmed == 0, \
01038         'the failed retry of the raw stream was read as a close'
01039     owned = [item for item in _launcher_custody_items()
01040              if item['kind'] == 'stdout' and item['resource'] is stdout]
01041     assert owned, 'the failed retry forgot the owner of the raw stream'
01042     assert [item['name'] for item in aggregate.raw_streams] == ['stdout'], \
01043         'the launch forgot a raw stream that was never closed'
01044 
01045     # -- N1 correction 13 (M07-A.2): every original that is not
01046     #    confirmed keeps an executable **same-owner** recovery route.  An
01047     #    ordinary failure of one original followed by an exact
01048     #    KeyboardInterrupt of the next one may not lose the first one,
01049     #    and an original that was **never visited** keeps its identity
01050     #    and its owner.  The default ``Popen`` of the package creates a
01051     #    standard error of ``None``: the process of this group has no
01052     #    standard error at all and the group never depends on one.
01053     class FaultStream:
01054         """One original raw file with a named fault seam."""
01055 
01056         def __init__(self, name, *, failure=None, cancel=None):
01057             self.name = name
01058             self.attempts = 0
01059             self.closed = False
01060             self._failure = failure
01061             self._cancel = cancel
01062 
01063         def close(self):
01064             self.attempts += 1
01065             if self._failure is not None:
01066                 error = self._failure
01067                 self._failure = None
01068                 raise error
01069             if self._cancel is not None:
01070                 error = self._cancel
01071                 self._cancel = None
01072                 raise error
01073             self.closed = True
01074             return True
01075 
01076     class FaultHandle:
01077         """The original HANDLE of a process with a named fault seam."""
01078 
01079         def __init__(self, failures=0):
01080             self.failures = failures
01081             self.attempts = 0
01082             self.closed = 0
01083 
01084         def Close(self):
01085             self.attempts += 1
01086             if self.failures > 0:
01087                 self.failures -= 1
01088                 raise RuntimeError('handle_close_failed')
01089             self.closed += 1
01090             return True
01091 
01092     class FaultProcess:
01093         """The original process of a launch with a controlled fault seam.
01094 
01095         The process has **no** standard error: the default ``Popen`` of
01096         the package creates one of ``None`` and the group never depends
01097         on a standard error of its own.
01098         """
01099 
01100         pid = 4245
01101         returncode = 0
01102 
01103         def __init__(self, *, stdin=None, stdout=None, handle_failures=0):
01104             self.stdin = stdin
01105             self.stdout = stdout
01106             self._handle = FaultHandle(failures=handle_failures)
01107             self.terminated = 0
01108             self.waited = 0
01109 
01110         def terminate(self):
01111             self.terminated += 1
01112 
01113         def wait(self, timeout=None):
01114             self.waited += 1
01115             return 0
01116 
01117     class FaultOsView:
01118         """A view of the module os that reports the platform."""
01119 
01120         name = 'nt'
01121 
01122         def __getattr__(self, item):
01123             return getattr(os, item)
01124 
01125     def run_fault_scenario(run_root, process, phase):
01126         """One no-channel scenario: the release, the facts, the recovery.
01127 
01128         The scenario runs on a **scoped** table of the launcher: the
01129         table of the package is shared by the whole batch and the
01130         context that another check of the batch bound to it is not the
01131         context of this launch.  The production table is used and it is
01132         never emptied by hand.
01133 
01134         RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 14, F1): the table of the launcher
01135         converges **one release later** and not in the release that
01136         closed the last original.  The reconciliation of the registered
01137         originals of a release names the unresolved ones as **retained**
01138         before the retry of the same release closes them: the report of
01139         that release therefore names a retained name that is already
01140         closed when the report is read.  The aggregate that the table
01141         keeps is an **empty bookkeeping owner** - it has no process, no
01142         channel and no original raw stream any more and it needs no
01143         primitive work at all.
01144 
01145         The scenario proves that empty owner, records it honestly and
01146         then permits **ONE** further explicit release of the **same**
01147         owner under the **same** original positive deadline.  That
01148         release performs **zero** close, terminate and wait and it
01149         converges the table to the empty state.  The table is never
01150         emptied by hand, the deadline is never renewed, the loop is
01151         never repeated until the table is green and no check of a
01152         retained resource is relaxed.
01153         """
01154         scoped = _LauncherCustody()
01155         # The ONE absolute deadline of this scenario.  The first
01156         # release, the recovery of the same owner and the final
01157         # convergence use the **same** positive deadline: a release
01158         # never opens a budget of its own and a convergence never
01159         # renews a lease.
01160         deadline = time.monotonic() + 30.0
01161         with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, '_LAUNCHER_CUSTODY',
01162                                         scoped):
01163             holder = m07a_interrupted_launch(run_root, sealed_plan(),
01164                                              process=process)
01165             assert holder['interrupted'] is True, \
01166                 'the hash of the launch was not interrupted'
01167             aggregate = holder['aggregate'][0] if holder['aggregate'] else None
01168             assert aggregate is not None, \
01169                 'the interrupted launch lost its owner'
01170             cancellation = None
01171             report = None
01172             with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os',
01173                                             FaultOsView()):
01174                 try:
01175                     report = scoped.release(deadline=deadline)
01176                 except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01177                     # The **original** cancellation object is the error
01178                     # that leaves.
01179                     cancellation = error
01180             first_facts = {'cancellation': (type(cancellation).__name__
01181                                             if cancellation is not None
01182                                             else None),
01183                            'report': report,
01184                            'deadline': deadline,
01185                            'raw_streams': [(item['name'], repr(item['stream']))
01186                                            for item in aggregate.raw_streams],
01187                            'owned': sorted((item['kind'],
01188                                             repr(item['resource']))
01189                                            for item in scoped.items()),
01190                            'process_owned': aggregate.process is process,
01191                            'terminated': process.terminated,
01192                            'waited': process.waited,
01193                            'handle_attempts': process._handle.attempts,
01194                            'handle_closed': process._handle.closed,
01195                            'stdin_attempts': process.stdin.attempts,
01196                            'stdout_attempts': process.stdout.attempts}
01197             write_phase('%s-first' % phase, first_facts)
01198             # The recovery of the **same** owner: every unresolved
01199             # original is really closed and the table converges.
01200             with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os',
01201                                             FaultOsView()):
01202                 recovered = scoped.release(deadline=deadline)
01203             recovered_facts = {'recovered': recovered,
01204                            'deadline': deadline,
01205                            'raw_streams': [(item['name'], repr(item['stream']))
01206                                            for item in aggregate.raw_streams],
01207                            'owned': sorted((item['kind'],
01208                                             repr(item['resource']))
01209                                            for item in scoped.items()),
01210                            'stdin_attempts': process.stdin.attempts,
01211                            'stdout_attempts': process.stdout.attempts,
01212                            'stdin_closed': process.stdin.closed,
01213                            'stdout_closed': process.stdout.closed,
01214                            'handle_attempts': process._handle.attempts,
01215                            'handle_closed': process._handle.closed,
01216                            'terminated': process.terminated,
01217                            'waited': process.waited}
01218             write_phase('%s-recovery' % phase, recovered_facts)
01219             # -- the state of the table **before** the salvage: the
01220             #    table may keep **only** the empty bookkeeping owner of
01221             #    the aggregate.  Every original of the launch was really
01222             #    closed and the name that is kept is the name of a
01223             #    snapshot of an earlier release of the same owner.
01224             retained_items = scoped.items()
01225             empty_facts = {
01226                 'owned': sorted((item['kind'], repr(item['resource']))
01227                                 for item in retained_items),
01228                 'process_is_none': aggregate.process is None,
01229                 'channel_is_none': aggregate.channel is None,
01230                 'raw_streams': [item['name'] for item in aggregate.raw_streams],
01231                 'workers': len(aggregate.workers),
01232                 'endpoints': len(aggregate.endpoints),
01233                 'stdin_closed': process.stdin.closed,
01234                 'stdout_closed': process.stdout.closed,
01235                 'stdin_confirmed': bool(
01236                     aggregate.progress.fact('stream:stdin').get('closed')),
01237                 'stdout_confirmed': bool(
01238                     aggregate.progress.fact('stream:stdout').get('closed')),
01239                 'handle_attempts': process._handle.attempts,
01240                 'handle_closed': process._handle.closed,
01241                 'terminated': process.terminated,
01242                 'waited': process.waited,
01243             }
01244             write_phase('%s-empty-owner' % phase, empty_facts)
01245             assert [item['kind'] for item in retained_items] in ([], ['aggregate']), \
01246                 'the table of the launcher kept an original resource of the launch'
01247             assert aggregate.process is None, \
01248                 'the aggregate of the launch still owns the process'
01249             assert aggregate.channel is None, \
01250                 'the aggregate of the launch still owns a channel'
01251             assert aggregate.raw_streams == [], \
01252                 'the aggregate of the launch still owns an original raw stream'
01253             assert aggregate.workers == [] and aggregate.endpoints == [], \
01254                 'the aggregate of the launch still owns a live worker or endpoint'
01255             assert process.stdin.closed is True, \
01256                 'the original input of the launch was not really closed'
01257             assert process.stdout.closed is True, \
01258                 'the original output of the launch was not really closed'
01259             assert empty_facts['stdin_confirmed'] is True, \
01260                 'the close of the original input was not confirmed by its owner'
01261             assert empty_facts['stdout_confirmed'] is True, \
01262                 'the close of the original output was not confirmed by its owner'
01263             assert process._handle.closed == 1, \
01264                 'the original handle of the process was not closed exactly once'
01265             # -- ONE further explicit release of the **same** owner
01266             #    under the **same** deadline.  It converges the table
01267             #    and it performs **zero** primitive work: no close, no
01268             #    terminate and no wait.
01269             with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os',
01270                                             FaultOsView()):
01271                 converged = scoped.release(deadline=deadline)
01272             converged_facts = {'converged': converged,
01273                            'deadline': deadline,
01274                            'owned': sorted((item['kind'],
01275                                             repr(item['resource']))
01276                                             for item in scoped.items()),
01277                            'stdin_attempts': process.stdin.attempts,
01278                            'stdout_attempts': process.stdout.attempts,
01279                            'stdin_closed': process.stdin.closed,
01280                            'stdout_closed': process.stdout.closed,
01281                            'handle_attempts': process._handle.attempts,
01282                            'handle_closed': process._handle.closed,
01283                            'terminated': process.terminated,
01284                            'waited': process.waited}
01285             write_phase('%s-converge' % phase, converged_facts)
01286             # The table of the launcher converges: every original of
01287             # the launch was really released by its owner.
01288             assert scoped.items() == [], \
01289                 'the table of the launcher kept an item of the launch'
01290             assert (converged or {}).get('retained') == [], \
01291                 'the convergence of the table retained a resource'
01292             # The convergence performed **zero** primitive work.
01293             assert converged_facts['stdin_attempts'] \
01294                 == recovered_facts['stdin_attempts'], \
01295                 'the convergence closed the original input a second time'
01296             assert converged_facts['stdout_attempts'] \
01297                 == recovered_facts['stdout_attempts'], \
01298                 'the convergence closed the original output a second time'
01299             assert converged_facts['handle_attempts'] \
01300                 == recovered_facts['handle_attempts'], \
01301                 'the convergence closed the original handle a second time'
01302             assert converged_facts['handle_closed'] \
01303                 == recovered_facts['handle_closed'], \
01304                 'the convergence closed the original handle more than once'
01305             assert converged_facts['terminated'] \
01306                 == recovered_facts['terminated'], \
01307                 'the convergence terminated the process a second time'
01308             assert converged_facts['waited'] \
01309                 == recovered_facts['waited'], \
01310                 'the convergence waited for the process a second time'
01311         return aggregate, cancellation, first_facts, recovered_facts
01312 
01313     # -- the cross-product one: an ordinary failure of the standard
01314     #    input, then the **exact** KeyboardInterrupt of the standard
01315     #    output.  The ordinary failure happened before the
01316     #    interruption and it may not be lost with the report of the
01317     #    helper.
01318     stdin_a = FaultStream('stdin', failure=RuntimeError('stdin_close_failed'))
01319     cancel_a = KeyboardInterrupt('stdout_close_cancelled')
01320     stdout_a = FaultStream('stdout', cancel=cancel_a)
01321     process_a = FaultProcess(stdin=stdin_a, stdout=stdout_a)
01322     aggregate_a, cancellation_a, first_a, facts = run_fault_scenario(
01323         tmp_path / 'ordinary-then-cancel', process_a,
01324         'r10-ordinary-then-cancel')
01325     assert cancellation_a is cancel_a, \
01326         'the original cancellation object was replaced'
01327     assert [name for name, _ref in first_a['raw_streams']] == \
01328         ['stdin', 'stdout'], \
01329         'the launch lost an unresolved original of the interrupted release'
01330     assert facts['stdin_closed'] is True, \
01331         'the same owner did not recover the ordinary failure of the input'
01332     assert facts['stdout_closed'] is True, \
01333         'the same owner did not recover the interrupted output'
01334     assert stdin_a.attempts == 2, \
01335         'the failed input of the launch was not retried by its owner'
01336     assert stdout_a.attempts == 2, \
01337         'the interrupted output of the launch was not retried by its owner'
01338 
01339     # -- the companion schedule: the **first** raw close is the exact
01340     #    KeyboardInterrupt and the standard output is never visited.
01341     stdin_b = FaultStream('stdin', cancel=KeyboardInterrupt('stdin_cancelled'))
01342     stdout_b = FaultStream('stdout')
01343     process_b = FaultProcess(stdin=stdin_b, stdout=stdout_b)
01344     aggregate_b, cancellation_b, first_b, facts = run_fault_scenario(
01345         tmp_path / 'cancel-first', process_b, 'r10-cancel-first')
01346     assert isinstance(cancellation_b, KeyboardInterrupt), \
01347         'the cancellation of the first raw close was swallowed'
01348     assert first_b['stdout_attempts'] == 0, \
01349         'the output of the launch was closed although it was never visited'
01350     assert [name for name, _ref in first_b['raw_streams']] == \
01351         ['stdin', 'stdout'], \
01352         'the launch lost the original that was never visited'
01353     assert facts['stdin_closed'] is True, \
01354         'the same owner did not recover the interrupted input'
01355     assert facts['stdout_closed'] is True, \
01356         'the same owner did not recover the original that was never visited'
01357 
01358     # -- the cross-product with an earlier **success** and a failing
01359     #    original HANDLE: the confirmed close is never repeated, the
01360     #    exit is never confirmed again and only the HANDLE and the
01361     #    unresolved original are retried.
01362     stdin_c = FaultStream('stdin')
01363     stdout_c = FaultStream('stdout', failure=RuntimeError('stdout_failed'))
01364     process_c = FaultProcess(stdin=stdin_c, stdout=stdout_c,
01365                              handle_failures=1)
01366     aggregate_c, cancellation_c, first_c, facts = run_fault_scenario(
01367         tmp_path / 'success-then-handle', process_c,
01368         'r10-success-then-handle')
01369     assert cancellation_c is None, \
01370         'the release raised a cancellation that never happened'
01371     assert [name for name, _ref in first_c['raw_streams']] == ['stdout'], \
01372         'the launch owns an original whose close was confirmed'
01373     assert stdin_c.attempts == 1, \
01374         'the confirmed close of the input was repeated'
01375     assert stdout_c.attempts == 2, \
01376         'the failed output of the launch was not retried by its owner'
01377     assert process_c._handle.attempts == 2, \
01378         'the original handle of the process was not retried'
01379     assert process_c._handle.closed == 1, \
01380         'the original handle of the process was not really closed'
01381     assert process_c.terminated == 1, \
01382         'the process of the launch was terminated a second time'
01383     assert process_c.waited == 1, \
01384         'the process of the launch was waited for a second time'
01385     assert facts['stdout_closed'] is True, \
01386         'the same owner did not recover the failed output'

01389 # -- N05-R11: the shared budget of the startup ---------------------------
01390 
01391 def test_n05_r11_no_role_is_created_or_resumed_after_the_deadline(tmp_path):
01392     """N05-R11 (M08): the startup checks the shared budget.
01393 
01394     Obligation: the action, the creation, the assignment, the resume
01395     and the start of a reader check the shared cancel and the shared
01396     deadline before they happen and after they happened.  Entry:
01397     ``BrokerCore.check_startup_budget`` and ``BrokerCore._launch_role``.
01398     Trigger: the creation of the role consumes the whole lease.
01399     Oracle: the process is never assigned and never resumed and the
01400     late resource is only cleaned up.  Negative: a role that is created
01401     after the deadline is never resumed and the next role is never
01402     created.  Returned state: the refusal of the startup is named.
01403     Salvage: the handles of the late role are released by their owner.
01404 
01405     The correction 08 of the root corrected the **oracle** of the
01406     second scenario of the group.  The product obligation of the root
01407     has always been that **every** creation is refused before the
01408     deadline of the round arrived, and the arm of a round is a creation
01409     like every other one.  The old group armed the round first and
01410     refused only the launch, which contradicts that obligation.  The
01411     group now drives three named scenarios and the arm of an expired
01412     round is refused **inside** the guard of the refusal, with a stable
01413     error code and with **nothing** created at all.
01414 
01415     The check of the budget before the creation of the job was **not**
01416     relaxed: the production keeps its bytes and the refusal of the
01417     expired arm is the behaviour that the root requires.
01418 
01419     RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 11): the second scenario of the group
01420     ended with the blanket expectation that **every** handle of the
01421     round was closed.  The late process of that scenario is a process
01422     whose exit was never confirmed - the lease of the round had already
01423     expired when it was created - and the obligation N10-R01 forbids the
01424     close of the control handle of such a process.  The expectation is
01425     therefore **split by the actual facts**: the control handle of the
01426     late process keeps its original reference and its owner, every
01427     other handle of the round is released, and the recovery of the same
01428     owner may not confirm the exit any more inside the same unextended
01429     deadline.  The salvage of the owner of the test is recorded apart
01430     and it is never a product PASS.
01431     """
01432     # SCENARIO ONE: an arm whose deadline is still valid succeeds: the
01433     # job of the round is created and the round is armed.
01434     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01435     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01436     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
01437                       clock=clock)
01438     core.bind_absolute_deadline(1005.0)
01439     try:
01440         core.arm()
01441         assert 'create_job' in win32.names()
01442         assert core.job is not None
01443         assert core.state == 'ARMED'
01444     finally:
01445         for handle in list(win32.live):
01446             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
01447 
01448     # SCENARIO TWO: the creation of the role consumes the whole lease
01449     # of the round: the launch is refused, the process is never
01450     # assigned and it is never resumed and the late resource is only
01451     # cleaned up.
01452     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01453     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01454     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
01455                       clock=clock)
01456     core.bind_absolute_deadline(1005.0)
01457     core.arm()
01458     original = win32.create_process_suspended
01459 
01460     def slow_create(**kwargs):
01461         result = original(**kwargs)
01462         # The creation consumes the whole lease of the round.
01463         clock.value += 100000.0
01464         return result
01465 
01466     win32.create_process_suspended = slow_create
01467     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
01468         core.launch()
01469     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
01470     # The process was never assigned and it was never resumed.
01471     assert 'assign_process_to_job' not in win32.names()
01472     assert 'resume_thread' not in win32.names()
01473     # The late resource was only cleaned up.
01474     assert core.state == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
01475 
01476     # RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 11): the late resource is a process
01477     # whose exit was **never** confirmed: the lease of the round had
01478     # already expired when it was created, so the bounded wait of the
01479     # exit never started.  The blanket expectation that every handle of
01480     # the round is closed contradicted the obligation N10-R01 and it is
01481     # therefore **split by the actual facts**:
01482     #
01483     # * the control handle of the late process keeps its **original**
01484     #   reference: it is in the record and in the pending table with
01485     #   its owner and it is **not** closed;
01486     # * every other handle of the round - the thread handle, the pipes
01487     #   and the job - is released;
01488     # * the recovery of the same owner may not confirm the exit any
01489     #   more, because the **same** unextended deadline of the cleanup
01490     #   is expired: the handle stays with its owner and the salvage of
01491     #   the owner of the test is recorded apart.  It is never a product
01492     #   PASS.
01493     late_record = core.records[0]
01494     late = late_record['process_handle']
01495     assert late is not None, 'the late process never got a control handle'
01496     assert late_record['exited'] is False, \
01497         'the exit of the late process was claimed without a proof'
01498     assert late_record['process_handle_closed'] is False
01499     assert late in win32.live, \
01500         'the control handle was closed while the exit was unknown'
01501     pending = [item for item in core._pending.pending() if item['handle'] == late]
01502     assert pending, 'the control handle of the late process lost its owner'
01503     assert pending[0]['kind'] == 'process'
01504     # Every other handle of the round was released by its owner.
01505     assert late_record['thread_handle_closed'] is True
01506     assert late_record['thread_handle'] is None
01507     assert core.job_closed is True
01508     assert sorted(win32.live) == [late], \
01509         'a handle was closed or kept without the proof of the exit'
01510     # The recovery of the same owner: the deadline is not extended and
01511     # the exit may not be confirmed any more.
01512     published = core.cleanup_deadline()
01513     core.stop('STOP')
01514     assert core.cleanup_deadline() == published, \
01515         'the cleanup deadline was extended by the recovery'
01516     assert late_record['exited'] is False
01517     assert late_record['process_handle_closed'] is False
01518     assert late in win32.live
01519     # The salvage of the owner of the test is bounded and it is recorded
01520     # apart: it is never a product PASS.
01521     for handle in list(win32.live):
01522         RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
01523 
01524     # SCENARIO THREE: the arm of a round whose deadline already
01525     # arrived is refused **before** the job of the round exists.  The
01526     # arm is inside the guard of the refusal and the error code is
01527     # stable.
01528     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01529     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01530     run_root = tmp_path / 'expired-arm'
01531     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=run_root,
01532                       clock=clock)
01533     core.bind_absolute_deadline(999.0)
01534     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
01535         core.arm()
01536     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
01537     # **Nothing** was created: the job, the limits of the job, the
01538     # pipes, the processes, the assignment and the resume were never
01539     # called at all.
01540     for name in ('create_job', 'set_job_kill_on_close', 'create_pipe',
01541                  'create_process_suspended', 'assign_process_to_job',
01542                  'resume_thread'):
01543         assert name not in win32.names(), name
01544     # The round left no handle behind and it left no output behind.
01545     assert win32.live == {}
01546     assert not win32.closed
01547     assert core.state != 'ARMED'
01548     assert not (run_root.exists() and any(run_root.rglob('*')))
01549

01551 # -- N05-R12: the one cleanup deadline -----------------------------------
01552 
01553 def test_n05_r12_the_cleanup_deadline_is_published_once_and_never_extended(tmp_path):
01554     """N05-R12 (M08): the cleanup deadline is published atomically.
01555 
01556     Obligation: the first entry of the cleanup publishes one deadline
01557     and every later caller reuses it.  Entry:
01558     ``NativeCustodySession._cleanup_deadline_for`` and
01559     ``BrokerCore.cleanup_deadline``.  Trigger: two callers enter the
01560     cleanup at the same moment.  Oracle: every caller sees the same
01561     value.  Negative: the deadline is never extended and never
01562     replaced.  Returned state: the deadline of the evidence is the
01563     published one.  Salvage: none is needed: nothing was created.
01564     """
01565     clock = SteppingClock(1000.0, step=0.5)
01566     session, _holder, _clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=clock)
01567     values = []
01568     barrier = threading.Barrier(4)
01569 
01570     def enter():
01571         barrier.wait()
01572         values.append(session._cleanup_deadline_for())
01573 
01574     threads = [threading.Thread(target=enter) for _ in range(4)]
01575     for thread in threads:
01576         thread.start()
01577     for thread in threads:
01578         thread.join(5.0)
01579     assert len(values) == 4
01580     # Every caller saw the very same value: the deadline was published
01581     # once and it was not generated twice.
01582     assert len(set(values)) == 1, 'the cleanup deadline was generated twice'
01583     published = values[0]
01584     # The deadline is never extended by a caller that came later.
01585     clock.advance(100.0)
01586     assert session._cleanup_deadline_for() == published
01587     assert session.evidence()['cleanup_deadline'] == published
01588 
01589     # The core of the broker publishes its deadline the same way.
01590     clock = SteppingClock(1000.0, step=0.5)
01591     core = BrokerCore(win32=RecordingWin32(), plan=sealed_plan(),
01592                       run_root=tmp_path, clock=clock)
01593     values = []
01594     barrier = threading.Barrier(4)
01595 
01596     def enter_core():
01597         barrier.wait()
01598         values.append(core.cleanup_deadline())
01599 
01600     threads = [threading.Thread(target=enter_core) for _ in range(4)]
01601     for thread in threads:
01602         thread.start()
01603     for thread in threads:
01604         thread.join(5.0)
01605     assert len(set(values)) == 1, 'the cleanup deadline was generated twice'
01606 
01607 
01608 # -- N05-R13: the remaining budget is recomputed before every wait -------
01609 
01610 def test_n05_r13_the_remaining_budget_is_recomputed_before_every_wait(tmp_path):
01611     """N05-R13 (M08): no wait ever receives the budget twice.
01612 
01613     Obligation: the remaining budget is recomputed before every wait,
01614     every join and every cancel.  Entry: ``BrokerCore._close_readers``
01615     and ``BrokerCore._wait_timeout_ms``.  Trigger: the wait of a reader
01616     consumes the budget of the round.  Oracle: the join of the reader
01617     receives the time that is left and not the whole budget again.
01618     Negative: there is no fixed extra settle.  Returned state: the
01619     deadlines of the round are the published ones.  Salvage: the
01620     handle of the reader is released by its owner.
01621     """
01622     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01623     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01624     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
01625                       clock=clock)
01626     # The deadline of the round leaves less than the join cap of the
01627     # package: the remaining budget is visible in the two waits.
01628     core.bind_absolute_deadline(1001.5)
01629     core.arm()
01630     core.launch()
01631     reader = RecordingReader(clock, 0x44)
01632     core.readers.append(reader)
01633     core._close_readers()
01634     # The wait of the reader consumed the budget of the round and the
01635     # join received the time that was left.
01636     assert reader.waits, 'the reader was never waited for'
01637     assert reader.joins, 'the reader was never joined'
01638     assert reader.joins[0] < reader.waits[0], \
01639         'the join received the budget of the wait again'
01640     assert reader.joins[0] >= 0.0
01641 
01642     # The waits of the processes receive a decreasing budget: the
01643     # remaining is recomputed before every one of them.
01644     clock = SteppingClock(1000.0, step=1.0)
01645     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01646     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
01647                       clock=clock)
01648     core.arm()
01649     core.launch()
01650     core.stop('STOP')
01651     timeouts = [call[2] for call in win32.calls if call[0] == 'wait_process']
01652     assert timeouts, 'no process was waited for'
01653     assert timeouts == sorted(timeouts, reverse=True), \
01654         'the budget of the wait was not recomputed'
01655
