01657 # -- N05-R14: an await that is running reads the tightened deadline ------
01658 
01659 def test_n05_r14_an_await_that_is_running_reads_the_tightened_deadline():
01660     """N05-R14 (M08): a running await reads the shared deadline.
01661 
01662     Obligation: an await that is already running reads the deadline
01663     that was tightened after it began.  Entry:
01664     ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint._await`` and
01665     ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint.bind_deadline``.  Trigger: the read carries
01666     no deadline and the deadline is bound while the read runs.  Oracle:
01667     the read ends at the tightened deadline and the original worker and
01668     its handle are kept.  Negative: a cancellation that reports
01669     ``NOT_FOUND`` and never completes is unknown at the deadline.
01670     Returned state: the failure is named and the worker is retained.
01671     Salvage: the owner of the worker stops it.
01672     """
01673     entered = threading.Event()
01674     release = threading.Event()
01675 
01676     class Api:
01677         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01678             return 701
01679 
01680         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
01681             return 'NOT_FOUND'
01682 
01683         def close_handle(self, handle):
01684             pass
01685 
01686     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01687     # The endpoint is built with its **real** constructor and with the
01688     # injected primitives of the round: the fixture never bypasses the
01689     # constructor, so every invariant of the endpoint - the lock of the
01690     # admission among them - is really initialised and the admission is
01691     # never a permissive fallback.
01692     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(
01693         file_object=type('F', (), {'close': lambda self: None})(),
01694         name='inprocess', api=Api(), clock=clock, handle=702)
01695     # The request of the group enters **unexpired**: the round has no
01696     # deadline yet and the admission of the generation is the one of a
01697     # live round.  The initial expired refusal is the separate
01698     # obligation N10-R06 and it is not weakened here.
01699     assert endpoint._effective_deadline() is None, \
01700         'the request of the group did not enter with a live deadline'
01701 
01702     def action(request):
01703         entered.set()
01704         release.wait(5.0)
01705         return b''
01706 
01707     original = endpoint._read_block
01708     endpoint._read_block = action
01709 
01710     outcome = {}
01711 
01712     def run_read():
01713         try:
01714             endpoint.read(None, None)
01715         except BrokerError as error:
01716             outcome['code'] = error.code
01717         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01718             outcome['code'] = type(error).__name__
01719 
01720     reader = threading.Thread(target=run_read)
01721     reader.start()
01722     assert entered.wait(5.0), 'the read never entered the operation'
01723     # The request was really admitted and it really entered the
01724     # primitive: the deadline of the round is tightened **after** that
01725     # fact and never before it.  The initial expired refusal is the
01726     # separate obligation N10-R06 and it is not weakened here.
01727     assert endpoint._reader is not None, \
01728         'the worker of the endpoint was never started'
01729     assert endpoint._effective_deadline() is None, \
01730         'the request did not enter with a live deadline'
01731     # The deadline of the round is tightened while the read is running.
01732     tightened = clock.value + 0.5
01733     endpoint.bind_deadline(tightened)
01734     clock.advance(5.0)
01735     reader.join(5.0)
01736     # The tightened deadline is the one that the endpoint uses and the
01737     # lease of the round is never extended by the tightening.
01738     assert endpoint._effective_deadline() == tightened, \
01739         'the lease of the round was extended'
01740     assert outcome.get('code') in ('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT',
01741                                     'BROKER_PIPE_CANCEL_NOT_CONFIRMED'), \
01742         'the running await did not read the tightened deadline'
01743     # The original worker and its handle are kept: the unknown state of
01744     # the endpoint has a reachable owner.
01745     assert endpoint._retained, 'the worker of the endpoint was forgotten'
01746     worker = endpoint._retained[0]
01747     assert worker.thread_handle == 701
01748     assert worker.thread_handle_released is False
01749     assert endpoint.facts['retained_workers'] == 1
01750     # The salvage of the test owner: the worker is released.
01751     release.set()
01752     worker.join_bounded(5.0)
01753     endpoint._read_block = original
01754 
01755     # -- N1 correction 12 (M08-C): the wait of the endpoint is cropped
01756     #    to the time that is really left until the **current** effective
01757     #    deadline of the round.  The requested value of the wait is the
01758     #    fact of the test: it is recorded before and after the
01759     #    tightening.
01760     requested = []
01761     original_wait_done = _WindowsPipeWorker.wait_done
01762 
01763     def recording_wait_done(self, generation, timeout):
01764         requested.append(timeout)
01765         return original_wait_done(self, generation, timeout)
01766 
01767     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01768 
01769     class WaitApi:
01770         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01771             return 711
01772 
01773         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
01774             return 'REQUESTED'
01775 
01776         def close_handle(self, handle):
01777             return None
01778 
01779         def read_stream(self, handle, size):
01780             return b''
01781 
01782     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(
01783         file_object=type('F', (), {'close': lambda self: None})(),
01784         name='inprocess-wait', api=WaitApi(), clock=clock, handle=712)
01785     waiting = threading.Event()
01786 
01787     def blocking_action(request):
01788         waiting.wait(10.0)
01789         return b''
01790 
01791     endpoint._read_block = blocking_action
01792     holder = {}
01793 
01794     def run_read():
01795         try:
01796             endpoint.read(clock.value + 10.0, None)
01797         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01798             holder['error'] = error
01799 
01800     reader = threading.Thread(target=run_read)
01801     with unittest.mock.patch.object(_WindowsPipeWorker, 'wait_done',
01802                                     recording_wait_done):
01803         reader.start()
01804         try:
01805             deadline = time.monotonic() + 10.0
01806             while not requested and time.monotonic() < deadline:
01807                 time.sleep(0.005)
01808             assert requested, 'the endpoint never waited for its worker'
01809             # The deadline of the round is tightened while the read runs
01810             # and the next wait receives the time that is really left.
01811             endpoint.bind_deadline(clock.value + 0.01)
01812             deadline = time.monotonic() + 10.0
01813             while min(requested) > 0.01 + 1e-9 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
01814                 time.sleep(0.005)
01815             waiting.set()
01816             reader.join(10.0)
01817             assert not reader.is_alive(), 'the read of the round was left behind'
01818             facts = {'requested': list(requested)}
01819             assert max(requested) <= 0.05 + 1e-9, \
01820                 'the wait of the endpoint exceeded the poll cap of the package'
01821             assert min(requested) <= 0.01 + 1e-9, \
01822                 'the wait of the endpoint was not cropped to the remaining'
01823         finally:
01824             waiting.set()
01825             reader.join(10.0)
01826             endpoint.close()
01827 
01828     # -- N1 correction 12 (M08-C): the put of the reader of the channel
01829     #    is cropped to the time that is really left.  There is no
01830     #    positive floor beyond the remaining of the round.
01831     #    RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 13): the **first** frame of the
01832     #    reader is delivered **after** the instrumentation of the queue
01833     #    is installed.  The channel starts its reader when it is built
01834     #    and a frame that was put before the instrumentation is a frame
01835     #    that the group never saw.  The reader of the group therefore
01836     #    publishes an explicit event when it is really inside its first
01837     #    read and a second one when it is really inside its second
01838     #    read, and the group releases the two frames one after the
01839     #    other.  The reader never returns the end of the file before
01840     #    its second frame was delivered.
01841     class SlowReader:
01842         """A read end that delivers one frame per explicit release."""
01843 
01844         def __init__(self):
01845             self.blocks = [b'one\n', b'two\n']
01846             self.first_entered = threading.Event()
01847             self.release_first = threading.Event()
01848             self.second_entered = threading.Event()
01849             self.release_second = threading.Event()
01850 
01851         def read(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
01852             if not self.blocks:
01853                 return b''
01854             block = self.blocks[0]
01855             if block == b'one\n':
01856                 self.first_entered.set()
01857                 self.release_first.wait(10.0)
01858             else:
01859                 self.second_entered.set()
01860                 self.release_second.wait(10.0)
01861             self.blocks.pop(0)
01862             return block
01863 
01864         def close(self):
01865             return {'closed': True, 'retained_workers': 0,
01866                     'worker_thread_handle_released': True}
01867 
01868     class BlockWriter:
01869         def write(self, raw, deadline=None, cancel=None):
01870             return len(raw)
01871 
01872         def close(self):
01873             return {'closed': True, 'retained_workers': 0,
01874                     'worker_thread_handle_released': True}
01875 
01876     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01877     reader = SlowReader()
01878     channel = StreamChannel(None, None, read_endpoint=reader,
01879                              write_endpoint=BlockWriter(), clock=clock,
01880                              max_frames=2)
01881     puts = []
01882     original_put = channel._queue.put
01883 
01884     def recording_put(item, timeout=None):
01885         puts.append(timeout)
01886         return original_put(item, timeout=timeout)
01887 
01888     channel.bind_deadline(clock.value + 10.0)
01889     try:
01890         # The reader of the channel is really inside its **first** read
01891         # and the first frame was not delivered yet.
01892         assert reader.first_entered.wait(10.0), \
01893             'the reader of the channel never entered its first read'
01894         # The instrumentation is installed **before** the first frame
01895         # exists: the group observes every put of the reader.
01896         channel._queue.put = recording_put
01897         reader.release_first.set()
01898         deadline = time.monotonic() + 10.0
01899         while len(puts) < 1 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
01900             time.sleep(0.005)
01901         assert puts, 'the reader of the channel never put a frame'
01902         assert puts[0] == pytest.approx(0.5), \
01903             'the put of the reader did not use the wait of the package'
01904         # The deadline of the round is tightened while the reader waits
01905         # for its next frame and the second frame is delivered exactly
01906         # once after the tightening.
01907         channel.bind_deadline(clock.value + 0.02)
01908         assert reader.second_entered.wait(10.0), \
01909             'the reader of the channel never entered its second read'
01910         reader.release_second.set()
01911         deadline = time.monotonic() + 10.0
01912         while len(puts) < 2 and time.monotonic() < deadline:
01913             time.sleep(0.005)
01914         assert len(puts) >= 2, 'the reader of the channel never put a frame'
01915         assert min(puts) <= 0.02 + 1e-9, \
01916             'the put of the reader was not cropped to the remaining'
01917         assert max(puts) <= 0.5 + 1e-9
01918     finally:
01919         # The teardown of the owner of the test is unconditional and it
01920         # is bounded: the reader is released and the thread of the
01921         # channel is joined.
01922         reader.release_first.set()
01923         reader.release_second.set()
01924         channel._thread.join(10.0)
01925         assert not channel._thread.is_alive(), \
01926             'the reader thread of the channel was left behind'
01927         channel.close()
01928 
01929 
01930 # -- N05-R15: the worker coordinates by generation ------------------------
01931 
01932 def test_n05_r15_the_worker_coordinates_by_generation_and_keeps_its_handle():
01933     """N05-R15 (M08): the worker of an endpoint coordinates by request.
01934 
01935     Obligation: the submission and the cancellation of a worker are
01936     coordinated by the same generation, a done of an older generation
01937     is never the done of this one and a handle that could not be closed
01938     keeps its owner.  Entry: ``_WindowsPipeWorker.submit``,
01939     ``_WindowsPipeWorker.request_cancel`` and
01940     ``_WindowsPipeWorker.release_thread_handle``.  Trigger: a
01941     cancellation that arrives while a request is queued.  Oracle: the
01942     request that is submitted after the cancellation is refused and the
01943     handle of the thread is kept.  Negative: a request that does not
01944     fit into the queue is refused with a named error.  Returned state:
01945     the state of the worker names the cancellation.  Salvage: the owner
01946     of the worker releases it.
01947     """
01948     entered = []
01949     release = threading.Event()
01950 
01951     class Api:
01952         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01953             return 701
01954 
01955         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
01956             return 'NOT_FOUND'
01957 
01958         def close_handle(self, handle):
01959             raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
01960 
01961     def action(request):
01962         entered.append(request)
01963         release.wait(5.0)
01964         return b''
01965 
01966     worker = _WindowsPipeWorker(Api(), 702, kind='read', action=action,
01967                                 name='n05-r15-worker')
01968     worker.start()
01969     worker.wait_ready(2.0)
01970     generation = worker.submit(b'first')
01971     for _ in range(100):
01972         if entered:
01973             break
01974         time.sleep(0.02)
01975     assert entered == [b'first']
01976     worker.submit(b'second')
01977     # The cancellation stops the worker from accepting a new task.
01978     state = worker.request_cancel(time.monotonic() + 0.2)
01979     assert state == 'NOT_FOUND'
01980     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
01981         worker.submit(b'third')
01982     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_WORKER_CANCELLED'
01983     # The completion of an older generation is never the completion of a
01984     # newer one.
01985     assert worker.wait_done(generation + 1, 0.05) is False
01986     # The handle of the thread could not be closed: the original owner
01987     # keeps it.
01988     assert worker.release_thread_handle() is False
01989     assert worker.thread_handle == 701
01990     assert worker.thread_handle_released is False
01991     # The salvage of the test owner: the worker ends and its handle is
01992     # released.
01993     release.set()
01994     worker.stop_accepting()
01995     worker.join_bounded(5.0)
01996     assert not worker.is_alive()

01999 # -- N05-R16: the unknown state is latched atomically -------------------
02000 
02001 def test_n05_r16_the_unknown_state_is_latched_atomically(tmp_path):
02002     """N05-R16 (M08): the unknown state is a one way latch.
02003 
02004     Obligation: the latch of the unknown state and the application of a
02005     state share one owner, so that a late reply never overwrites the
02006     unknown state of the round.  Entry:
02007     ``NativeCustodySession._apply_state`` and
02008     ``NativeCustodySession._latch_unknown_state``.  Trigger: a reply
02009     that passes the check at the same moment as the failure of the
02010     background.  Oracle: the round stays unknown.  Negative: a status
02011     is never sent after the stop of the round.  Returned state: the
02012     late state is named and ignored.  Salvage: the close of the round
02013     is bounded and it ends.
02014     """
02015     for _ in range(50):
02016         session, holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
02017         session.start()
02018         barrier = threading.Barrier(2)
02019         outcome = {}
02020 
02021         def late_reply():
02022             barrier.wait()
02023             outcome['applied'] = session._apply_state('RUNNING')
02024 
02025         worker = threading.Thread(target=late_reply)
02026         worker.start()
02027         barrier.wait()
02028         session._latch_unknown_state()
02029         worker.join(5.0)
02030         # The unknown state of the round was never overwritten.
02031         assert session.state == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
02032         if outcome.get('applied') is False:
02033             assert any('CUSTODY_LATE_STATE_IGNORED' in failure
02034                        for failure in session.evidence()['failures'])
02035         # A status is never sent after the stop of the round.
02036         session._stopping = True
02037         sent = len(holder['peer'].outbound)
02038         session._last_heartbeat = clock.value - 61.0
02039         assert session._heartbeat_step() is False
02040         assert len(holder['peer'].outbound) == sent
02041         session._stopping = False
02042         session.stop()
02043

02045 # -- N05-R17: one owner of the frame budget ------------------------------
02046 
02047 def test_n05_r17_the_frame_budget_has_one_owner_in_both_directions(tmp_path):
02048     """N05-R17 (M08): the frame budget is one atomic owner.
02049 
02050     Obligation: the check and the commit of the frame budget are one
02051     atomic step in both directions.  Entry:
02052     ``NativeCustodySession._reserve_frame``.  Trigger: two directions
02053     check the budget at the same moment.  Oracle: only one of them
02054     takes the last frame of the budget.  Negative: the total of the two
02055     directions never exceeds the limit.  Returned state: the refused
02056     direction is named.  Salvage: nothing was created.
02057     """
02058     session, _holder, _clock = make_session(tmp_path)
02059     session.start()
02060     # Exactly one frame of the budget is left for the two directions.
02061     session._frames_sent = MAX_FRAMES - RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES - 1
02062     session._frames_received = 0
02063     outcomes = []
02064     barrier = threading.Barrier(2)
02065 
02066     def reserve(received):
02067         barrier.wait()
02068         try:
02069             session._reserve_frame(received=received)
02070             outcomes.append('accepted')
02071         except CustodyError as error:
02072             outcomes.append(error.code)
02073 
02074     threads = [threading.Thread(target=reserve, args=(index == 1,))
02075                for index in range(2)]
02076     for thread in threads:
02077         thread.start()
02078     for thread in threads:
02079         thread.join(5.0)
02080     assert sorted(outcomes) == ['CUSTODY_FRAME_BUDGET_EXCEEDED', 'accepted'], \
02081         'the two directions took the last frame of the budget together'
02082     assert session._frames_sent + session._frames_received <= MAX_FRAMES
02083     session.stop()
02084

02086 # -- N05-R18: the terminal exchange consumes its reservation -------------
02087 
02088 def test_n05_r18_the_terminal_exchange_consumes_its_reservation(tmp_path):
02089     """N05-R18 (M08): the terminal frames are inside the budget.
02090 
02091     Obligation: the frames of the terminal exchange consume the
02092     reservation that was kept for them and they are never refused
02093     because of it.  Entry: ``NativeCustodySession._reserve_frame`` and
02094     ``BrokerLoop._check_frame_budget``.  Trigger: the budget is nearly
02095     exhausted.  Oracle: the terminal frame is accepted and the
02096     non-terminal frame is refused.  Negative: a non-terminal frame
02097     never borrows the reservation.  Returned state: the refusal is
02098     named and the round fails closed.  Salvage: nothing was created.
02099     """
02100     session, _holder, _clock = make_session(tmp_path)
02101     session.start()
02102     # The total is at the limit of the non-terminal frames: only the
02103     # reservation of the terminal exchange is left.
02104     session._frames_sent = MAX_FRAMES - RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES
02105     session._frames_received = 0
02106     # The terminal frame consumes the reservation: it is legal.
02107     session._reserve_frame(terminal=True)
02108     # A non-terminal frame never borrows the reservation.
02109     with pytest.raises(CustodyError) as info:
02110         session._reserve_frame()
02111     assert info.value.code == 'CUSTODY_FRAME_BUDGET_EXCEEDED'
02112     session.stop()
02113 
02114     # The broker of the round applies the same rule to the frame that
02115     # it receives.
02116     plan = sealed_plan()
02117     core = BrokerCore(win32=RecordingWin32(), plan=plan, run_root=tmp_path)
02118     loop = BrokerLoop(core=core, transport=object(), plan=plan, nonce='a' * 32)
02119     loop._frames = MAX_FRAMES - RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES
02120     loop._sent_frames = 0
02121     loop._check_frame_budget(0)
02122     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
02123         loop._check_frame_budget(RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES)
02124     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_FRAME_BUDGET_EXCEEDED'

02127 # -- N05-R19: the unconfirmed items are coordinated again ---------------
02128 
02129 def test_n05_r19_the_unconfirmed_items_are_coordinated_again(tmp_path):
02130     """N05-R19 (M09): the attempt that ended is not a release.
02131 
02132     Obligation: the items that a close did not confirm are coordinated
02133     again inside the original deadline and the confirmed items are never
02134     closed twice.  Entry: ``NativeCustodySession.stop`` and
02135     ``NativeCustodySession._recover_close``.  Trigger: the close of the
02136     peer fails and the condition of the round recovers.  Oracle: the
02137     peer is coordinated again through the public stop and the deadline
02138     of the cleanup is not reset.  Negative: a recovery that arrives
02139     after the deadline keeps the resources with their owner.  Returned
02140     state: the state of the close is published.  Salvage: the owner of
02141     the peer releases it.
02142     """
02143     session, holder, clock = make_session(
02144         tmp_path, close_facts=dict(COMPLETE_CLOSE_FACTS,
02145                                    process_handle_released=False))
02146     session.start()
02147     peer = holder['peer']
02148     deadline = session._cleanup_deadline_for()
02149     first = session.stop()
02150     assert first['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
02151     assert peer.closed == 1
02152     assert session.broker is peer
02153     # The condition of the round recovered.
02154     peer.close_facts = dict(COMPLETE_CLOSE_FACTS)
02155     second = session.stop()
02156     assert peer.closed == 2, 'the peer was never coordinated again'
02157     assert session.broker is None
02158     assert second['broker_retained'] is False
02159     # The deadline of the cleanup was never reset.
02160     assert session._cleanup_deadline_for() == deadline
02161     assert second['cleanup_deadline'] == deadline
02162 
02163     # A recovery that arrives after the deadline keeps the resources of
02164     # the round with their owner.
02165     session, holder, clock = make_session(
02166         tmp_path, close_facts=dict(COMPLETE_CLOSE_FACTS,
02167                                    process_handle_released=False))
02168     session.start()
02169     peer = holder['peer']
02170     session.stop()
02171     clock.value += 1000.0
02172     session.stop()
02173     assert session.broker is peer
02174     assert 'CUSTODY_CLOSE_RECOVERY_EXPIRED' in session.evidence()['failures']
02175

02177 # -- N05-R20: the closed fact is committed when it is confirmed ----------
02178 
02179 def test_n05_r20_the_closed_fact_is_committed_only_when_it_is_confirmed():
02180     """N05-R20 (M09): a close is committed when it really happened.
02181 
02182     Obligation: the fact that an endpoint is closed is committed only
02183     when the close was confirmed, a failed close is recoverable and the
02184     close of an endpoint that is closed stays idempotent.  Entry:
02185     ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint.close`` and ``_PosixPipeEndpoint.close``.
02186     Trigger: the close of the file of the endpoint fails.  Oracle: the
02187     endpoint may try the close again and the fact becomes true when it
02188     is confirmed.  Negative: a proven close is never turned into a
02189     missing one and the descriptor is never closed twice.  Returned
02190     state: the facts of the close name the failure.  Salvage: the owner
02191     of the endpoint closes it later.
02192     """
02193     class Api:
02194         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
02195             return 701
02196 
02197         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
02198             return 'NOT_FOUND'
02199 
02200         def close_handle(self, handle):
02201             pass
02202 
02203     class BrokenFile:
02204         def __init__(self):
02205             self.attempts = 0
02206 
02207         def close(self):
02208             self.attempts += 1
02209             if self.attempts == 1:
02210                 raise OSError('close_failed')
02211 
02212     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
02213     # The endpoint is built with its **real** constructor and with the
02214     # injected primitives of the round: the fixture never bypasses the
02215     # constructor and every invariant of the endpoint is really
02216     # initialised.
02217     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=BrokenFile(),
02218                                     name='inprocess', api=Api(),
02219                                     clock=clock, handle=702)
02220     facts = endpoint.close()
02221     # The close was not confirmed: the fact is not committed and the
02222     # endpoint may try it again.
02223     assert facts['closed'] is False
02224     assert facts['error_type'] == 'OSError'
02225     assert endpoint._closed is False
02226     facts = endpoint.close()
02227     assert facts['closed'] is True
02228     assert endpoint._closed is True
02229     # A close of an endpoint that is closed is idempotent and it keeps
02230     # the fact that it is closed.
02231     again = endpoint.close()
02232     assert again['closed'] is True
02233     assert again['already_closed'] is True
02234     assert endpoint._file.attempts == 2, 'the file was closed a third time'
02235

02237 # -- N05-R21: the endpoint is the only owner of the files ----------------
02238 
02239 def test_n05_r21_the_endpoint_is_the_only_owner_of_the_files_of_the_channel():
02240     """N05-R21 (M09): the broker never closes the files of the endpoint.
02241 
02242     Obligation: the endpoint is the only owner of the files of the
02243     channel and the broker process never closes them by itself.  Entry:
02244     ``BrokerProcess.close``.  Trigger: the read end of the channel
02245     could not be closed and a worker of the endpoint is still alive.
02246     Oracle: the broker process did not close the standard output and
02247     the standard input by itself.  Negative: a live worker keeps the
02248     file and the round stays unknown.  Returned state: the facts of the
02249     close name the end that is still open.  Salvage: the owner of the
02250     channel closes it later.
02251     """
02252     closed = []
02253 
02254     class Channel:
02255         reader_finished = False
02256 
02257         def __init__(self):
02258             self.closes = 0
02259 
02260         def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
02261             self.deadline = deadline
02262 
02263         def close(self):
02264             self.closes += 1
02265             return {'write_end_closed': True, 'read_end_closed': False,
02266                     'reader_finished': False, 'reader_error': None,
02267                     'write_retained_workers': 0, 'read_retained_workers': 1,
02268                     'worker_thread_handle_released': False}
02269 
02270     class Process:
02271         pid = 1
02272         returncode = None
02273 
02274         def __init__(self):
02275             self.stdin = type('S', (), {'close': lambda self: closed.append('stdin')})()
02276             self.stdout = type('S', (), {'close': lambda self: closed.append('stdout')})()
02277 
02278         def wait(self, timeout=None):
02279             raise TimeoutError('still running')
02280 
02281         def terminate(self):
02282             closed.append('terminate')
02283 
02284     channel = Channel()
02285     broker = BrokerProcess(Process(), 'x', 'a' * 64, channel)
02286     broker.write_deadline = 1005.0
02287     facts = broker.close()
02288     # The endpoint is the only owner of the files: the broker process
02289     # never closed the standard input and the standard output by
02290     # itself.
02291     assert 'stdin' not in closed
02292     assert 'stdout' not in closed
02293     assert facts['channel_closed'] is True
02294     assert facts['read_end_closed'] is False
02295     assert facts['stdout_closed'] is False
02296     assert facts['channel_retained_workers'] == 1
02297     assert facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] is False
02298     assert facts['process_exited'] is False
02299     # The channel was closed by its owner and the end that is still
02300     # open keeps its owner.
02301     assert channel.closes == 1
02302

02304 # -- N05-R22: the strict facts of the close -----------------------------
02305 
02306 def test_n05_r22_the_strict_facts_of_the_close_are_required(tmp_path):
02307     """N05-R22 (M09): three facts are not a release.
02308 
02309     Obligation: the parent refuses a close that does not report the
02310     facts of the reader, of the endpoints and of the workers.  Entry:
02311     ``NativeCustodySession._perform_close``.  Trigger: the close of the
02312     peer reports the three old facts only.  Oracle: the original broker
02313     is kept and the missing facts are named.  Negative: a close that
02314     reports every fact releases the broker.  Returned state: the round
02315     is unknown and the broker is retained.  Salvage: the public stop of
02316     the round coordinates the peer again.
02317     """
02318     session, holder, _clock = make_session(
02319         tmp_path, close_facts={'channel_closed': True, 'process_exited': True,

02332     for key in ('channel_reader_finished', 'read_end_closed',
02333                 'write_end_closed', 'worker_thread_handle_released'):
02334         assert key in required

02336     session, holder, _clock = make_session(
02337         tmp_path, close_facts=dict(COMPLETE_CLOSE_FACTS))
02338     session.start()
02339     evidence = session.stop()
02340     assert session.broker is None
02341     assert evidence['broker_retained'] is False

02344 # -- N05-R23: the end of the attempt and the end of the closer ----------
02345 
02346 def test_n05_r23_the_end_of_the_attempt_and_the_end_of_the_closer_are_two_facts(tmp_path):
02347     """N05-R23 (M09): the attempt and the thread are two facts.
02348 
02349     Obligation: the end of the attempt of the close and the end of the
02350     thread that performs it are two different facts.  Entry:
02351     ``NativeCustodySession._await_close`` and
02352     ``NativeCustodySession._closer_main``.  Trigger: the closer is
02353     still alive when the attempt of the close ended.  Oracle: the
02354     waiter waits for the closer inside the same deadline and the
02355     evidence carries both facts.  Negative: the round never joins
02356     itself and never forms a cycle of waits.  Returned state: the
02357     closer that is still alive is named.  Salvage: the closer ends and
02358     the round is closed.
02359     """
02360     session, holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
02361     session.start()
02362     entered = threading.Event()
02363     release = threading.Event()
02364     original_finish = session._finish_close
02365 
02366     def slow_finish():
02367         # The end of the attempt is published and the closer stays
02368         # alive for a while.
02369         original_finish()
02370         entered.set()
02371         release.wait(5.0)
02372 
02373     session._finish_close = slow_finish
02374     try:
02375         assert session._start_bounded_close() is True
02376         assert entered.wait(5.0), 'the closer never ended its attempt'
02377         evidence = session.evidence()
02378         assert evidence['close_finished'] is True
02379         assert evidence['closer_alive'] is True
02380         assert evidence['closer_exited'] is False
02381         # The waiter waits for the closer inside the same deadline and it
02382         # never forms a cycle of waits.
02383         outcome = {}
02384 
02385         def waiter():
02386             outcome['result'] = session._await_close()
02387 
02388         thread = threading.Thread(target=waiter)
02389         thread.start()
02390         thread.join(0.2)
02391         assert thread.is_alive(), 'the waiter returned before the closer ended'
02392         # The deadline of the cleanup arrives while the closer is alive.
02393         clock.value += 100.0
02394         thread.join(5.0)
02395         assert not thread.is_alive()
02396         assert outcome.get('result') is False
02397         assert 'CUSTODY_CLOSER_NOT_FINISHED' in session.evidence()['failures']
02398         assert session.evidence()['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
02399         # The closer is still named by the round: it keeps its owner.
02400         assert session.evidence()['closer_alive'] is True
02401     finally:
02402         # The salvage of the test owner: the closer ends and it is
02403         # joined.  The salvage never changes the fact of the product
02404         # above.
02405         release.set()
02406         closer = session._closer
02407         if closer is not None:
02408             closer.join(5.0)
02409 
02410     # -- the waiter that arrives before the end of the attempt -------------
02411     session, holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
02412     session.start()
02413     entered = threading.Event()
02414     release = threading.Event()
02415     original_finish = session._finish_close
02416 
02417     def slow_attempt():
02418         # The attempt of the close is still in flight and the closer
02419         # stays alive inside it.
02420         entered.set()
02421         release.wait(5.0)
02422         original_finish()
02423 
02424     session._finish_close = slow_attempt
02425     try:
02426         assert session._start_bounded_close() is True
02427         assert entered.wait(5.0), 'the closer never began its attempt'
02428         outcome = {}
02429 
02430         def waiter():
02431             outcome['result'] = session._await_close()
02432 
02433         thread = threading.Thread(target=waiter)
02434         thread.start()
02435         thread.join(0.2)
02436         assert thread.is_alive(), 'the waiter returned before the attempt ended'
02437         # The attempt ends: the waiter still waits for the **real** exit
02438         # of the closer and it confirms it.
02439         release.set()
02440         thread.join(5.0)
02441         assert not thread.is_alive()
02442         assert outcome.get('result') is True
02443         assert not session._closer.is_alive()
02444         assert session.evidence()['closer_exited'] is True
02445     finally:
02446         release.set()
02447         closer = session._closer
02448         if closer is not None:
02449             closer.join(5.0)
02450 
02451     # -- the close of the calling thread has no closer of its own ----------
02452     session, holder, clock = make_session(tmp_path, clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
02453     session.start()
02454     # The close is performed by the calling thread itself: there is no
02455     # closer thread and no cleanup in flight, so the confirmation is
02456     # immediate.
02457     assert session._close_broker() == 'PERFORMED'
02458     assert session._closer is None
02459     assert session._await_close() is True
02460 
02461

### 206a7fe7f21343d89ca83e03a0e4dc7e670cd381:tools/test_v0_s3_native_custody_correction07.py
00001 """The frozen regressions of the N1 correction 07 of the native custody.
00002 
00003 This module carries the **18 frozen oracle groups** of the root package
00004 ``reports/v0-s3/product-gate0/native-custody-correction-07`` (the file
00005 ``regression-obligations.json``).  The obligations are frozen by the
00006 root and they are not written by the implementer: every group below is
00007 mapped to the production owner that has to carry it and to the test
00008 that drives it.
00009 
00010 The rules of this module are the rules of the whole N1 package.
00011 
00012 * It is **offline**.  It never starts a process, it never opens a
00013   socket, it never touches a database and it never reads the original
00014   budget ledger or the original shared counter.  The Windows primitives
00015   are driven through an **in-process** table of primitives that is
00016   injected into the production façade: the production code that is
00017   checked here is the code of a real run, only the native library is
00018   missing.
00019 
00020 * Every case drives the **actual production method**: the production
00021   ``WindowsApi`` with an injected primitive table, the production
00022   ``BrokerCore``, ``BrokerLoop``, ``BrokerProcess``, ``StreamChannel``,
00023   ``_WindowsPipeWorker``, ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint``, ``_StartupOwner``,
00024   ``_LauncherCustody`` and the production ``NativeCustodySession``.  A
00025   façade that only returns the right story would not prove anything and
00026   it is not used for the failure paths.
00027 
00028 * Every case records the state of the resources **at the moment the
00029   product returned**, before any salvage of the test owner.  The
00030   cleanup of the test owner is explicit and it is never used to
00031   declare that the product returned cleanly: a product that could not
00032   release a resource stays unknown and keeps its original reference.
00033 
00034 * The native claims of this package stay ``NOT_PROVEN``.  The module
00035   proves the behaviour of the Python owners and it never claims that a
00036   Windows API, a job object, a GPU, a port or a service was executed.
00037 
00038 The boundary of the process is not installed here: the guard of the
00039 batch lives in the report directory of the batch and the runner of the
00040 batch is its only authorised entry.
00041 """

00121 # -- the bounded facts of the real chain of N07-R15 -------------------------
00122 
00123 #: The directory in which the facts of the real chain are written.  The
00124 #: facts of one phase are written **before** the assertions of that
00125 #: phase: a group that fails has to leave its evidence on the disk and
00126 #: it may never depend on the representation of a failure of pytest.
00127 #:
00128 #: CORRECTION 09: the root of the correction 08 owns the **five** real
00129 #: documents of the failed batch of the correction 08 and they are never
00130 #: overwritten.  The diagnostics of this module are written in the **new**
00131 #: directory of the correction 09 and the two directories are never mixed.
00132 #: CORRECTION 10: the diagnostics of the group are written in the NEW
00133 #: directory of the correction 10.  The four real documents of the
00134 #: failed batch of the correction 09 stay in the directory of the
00135 #: correction 09 and they are never overwritten, never deleted and
00136 #: never replaced by a summary.  The directory may be named by the
00137 #: environment of the batch, so that the runner of the root may write
00138 #: the diagnostics into the attempt directory of its own plan.
00139 #: CORRECTION 11: the diagnostics of the group are written in the NEW
00140 #: directory of the correction 11.  The real documents of the failed
00141 #: batch of the correction 10 stay in the directory of the correction
00142 #: 10 and they are never overwritten, never deleted and never replaced
00143 #: by a summary.
00144 #: CORRECTION 12: the diagnostics of the group are written in the NEW
00145 #: directory of the correction 12.  The real documents of the failed
00146 #: batch of the correction 11 stay in the directory of the correction
00147 #: 11 and they are never overwritten, never deleted and never replaced
00148 #: by a summary.
00149 #: CORRECTION 13: the diagnostics of the group are written in the NEW
00150 #: directory of the correction 13.  The real documents of the failed
00151 #: batch of the correction 12 stay in the directory of the correction
00152 #: 12 and they are never overwritten, never deleted and never replaced
00153 #: by a summary.
00154 #: CORRECTION 14: the diagnostics of the group are written in the NEW
00155 #: directory of the correction 14.  The real documents of the failed
00156 #: batch of the correction 13 stay in the directory of the correction
00157 #: 13 and they are never overwritten, never deleted and never replaced
00158 #: by a summary.  The environment of the batch may still name the
00159 #: directory of the attempt of the plan of the root.
00160 F5_EVIDENCE_DIR = (Path(os.environ['LVW_N1_F5_EVIDENCE_DIR'])
00161                    if os.environ.get('LVW_N1_F5_EVIDENCE_DIR')
00162                    else (PACK_ROOT / 'reports' / 'v0-s3' / 'product-gate0'
00163                          / 'implementation-native-custody-correction-14'
00164                          / 'f5-evidence'))

00166 
00167 def _f5_write(phase, document):
00168     """Write the bounded facts of one phase of the real chain.
00169 
00170     The document is a plain JSON document and it is bounded: it never
00171     carries an unbounded list and it never carries the whole state of
00172     the round.  Every value of it is the value that the production
00173     returned: nothing of the real chain is replaced by a hard coded
00174     fact.
00175     """
00176     F5_EVIDENCE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
00177     path = F5_EVIDENCE_DIR / ('f5-%s.json' % phase)
00178     path.write_text(json.dumps(document, indent=1, sort_keys=True,
00179                                default=repr), encoding='utf-8')
00180     return str(path)

00182 
00183 def _f5_worker_facts(worker):
00184     """The bounded facts of one worker of one endpoint.
00185 
00186     The identity of the **original** handle of the thread of the worker
00187     is part of the facts: a worker that kept its handle is named by the
00188     handle that it really holds.
00189     """
00190     if worker is None:
00191         return None
00192     return {
00193         'kind': getattr(worker, 'kind', None),
00194         'name': getattr(worker, 'name', None),
00195         'state': getattr(worker, 'state', None),
00196         'thread_handle': getattr(worker, 'thread_handle', None),
00197         'thread_handle_released': bool(
00198             getattr(worker, 'thread_handle_released', False)),
00199         'alive': bool(worker.is_alive()),
00200         'joined': bool(getattr(worker, 'joined', False)),
00201         'error': getattr(worker, 'error', None),
00202     }
00203 
00204 
00205 def _f5_endpoint_facts(endpoint):
00206     """The bounded facts of one endpoint of the channel."""
00207     if endpoint is None:
00208         return None
00209     retained = list(getattr(endpoint, '_retained', []) or [])
00210     return {
00211         'name': getattr(endpoint, '_name', None),
00212         'facts': dict(getattr(endpoint, 'facts', {}) or {}),
00213         'reader': _f5_worker_facts(getattr(endpoint, '_reader', None)),
00214         'writer': _f5_worker_facts(getattr(endpoint, '_writer', None)),
00215         'retained': [_f5_worker_facts(worker) for worker in retained],
00216     }
00217

00219 def _f5_snapshot(phase, session, api, holder, channel_facts, channel_closes,
00220                  target):
00221     """The bounded facts of one phase of the real chain.
00222 
00223     The snapshot names the evidence of the round, the facts of the close
00224     of the broker, the facts of the close of the channel, the facts of
00225     the two endpoints of the channel with the identity of the retained
00226     original handles of their threads, the state of every worker, the
00227     ledger of the closes, the number of the targeted faults and the
00228     original deadline of the cleanup.
00229     """
00230     evidence = session.evidence()
00231     broker = holder.get('broker')
00232     return {
00233         'phase': phase,
00234         'session_evidence': evidence,
00235         'broker_close_facts': evidence.get('broker_close_facts'),
00236         'broker_own_close_facts': getattr(broker, 'close_facts', None),
00237         'broker_retained': session.broker is not None,
00238         'channel_closes': len(channel_closes),
00239         'channel_facts': list(channel_facts),
00240         'read_endpoint': _f5_endpoint_facts(holder.get('read_endpoint')),
00241         'write_endpoint': _f5_endpoint_facts(holder.get('write_endpoint')),
00242         'target_handle': target,
00243         'fault_handle': api.fault_handle,
00244         'fault_released': api.fault_released,
00245         'fault_hits': api.fault_hits,
00246         'close_ledger': list(api.close_ledger),
00247         'closed_handles': list(api.closed),
00248         'thread_handles': list(api.thread_handles),
00249         'cleanup_deadline': evidence.get('cleanup_deadline'),
00250         'close_generation': evidence.get('close_generation'),
00251     }

00253 
00254 class _F5Api:
00255     """The in-process façade of the primitives of the real chain.
00256 
00257     The façade is the only place where a fault of the round is
00258     injected.  The fault is bound to **one** original handle and no
00259     other handle of the round is affected by it.
00260 
00261     The reader of the chain is driven by an explicit **causal** state
00262     machine and it never guesses and it never sleeps on a timeout to
00263     reach the next state:
00264 
00265     * ``BOOTSTRAP_FRAMES_CONSUMED`` - the two frames of the round were
00266       delivered and the reader enters the primitive again;
00267     * ``READ_WAITING`` - the reader is really inside its read.  The
00268       event is published **before** any blocking wait, so that the
00269       owner of the test never waits for an event that the reader
00270       publishes only after a timeout;
00271     * the **explicit** unlock - the owner of the test, or the
00272       cancellation of the operating system, publishes the outcome that
00273       the reader really produces;
00274     * ``RESULT`` / ``EOF`` - the frame, the ordinary failure of the
00275       read or the end of the stream.
00276 
00277     The terminal frame of the round is published by the **write** of the
00278     stop and by nothing else: a reader that reads a terminal frame
00279     before the stop was asked for would answer a stop that never
00280     happened.
00281     """
00282 
00283     def __init__(self, *, block_the_reader=False, read_fault=None,
00284                  partial_frame=None):
00285         self.lock = threading.Lock()
00286         self.frames = []
00287         # The terminal frame of the round.  It is published by the
00288         # write of the stop and by nothing else.
00289         self.final = b''
00290         # The explicit outcomes of the reader.  Every entry is a pair
00291         # of a kind and of its payload: ``FRAME``, ``FAULT``,
00292         # ``PARTIAL`` and ``EOF``.
00293         self._outcomes = queue.Queue()
00294         # The explicit notification that the reader of the chain is
00295         # really inside its read.  It is published **before** the
00296         # blocking wait of the reader.
00297         self.read_waiting = threading.Event()
00298         # The explicit notifications of the outcomes that really
00299         # happened.
00300         self.fault_observed = threading.Event()
00301         self.eof_observed = threading.Event()
00302         self.closed = []
00303         # The ledger of the closes: every attempt is named with the
00304         # handle that it tried to close and with the fact that it was
00305         # the targeted fault.
00306         self.close_ledger = []
00307         self.thread_handles = []
00308         self.next_handle = 700
00309         # The fault of the round: the one handle that it names, the
00310         # flag that releases it and the number of the times that it
00311         # really fired.
00312         self.fault_handle = None
00313         self.fault_released = False
00314         self.fault_hits = 0
00315         # The behaviour of the reader of the chain.  The reader always
00316         # waits for an explicit outcome: the flag is kept for the
00317         # readability of the groups and it does not change the
00318         # behaviour.
00319         self.block_the_reader = block_the_reader
00320         self.read_fault = read_fault
00321         self.partial_pending = partial_frame
00322 
00323     # -- the explicit outcomes of the reader ------------------------------
00324     def publish_outcome(self, kind, payload=None):
00325         """Publish the outcome that the reader really produces.
00326 
00327         The publication is the **unlock** of the reader: the reader is
00328         inside its read and it waits for exactly this outcome.
00329         """
00330         self._outcomes.put((kind, payload))
00331         return payload
00332 
00333     def publish_read_fault(self):
00334         """Publish the ordinary failure of the read, explicitly.
00335 
00336         The failure is published by the owner of the test and it is
00337         never unlocked by the write of the stop: a failure that should
00338         happen first is not caused by the stop that comes later.
00339         """
00340         with self.lock:
00341             error = self.read_fault
00342             self.read_fault = None
00343         if error is not None:
00344             self.publish_outcome('FAULT', error)
00345         return error
00346 
00347     def publish_partial_frame(self):
00348         """Publish the frame that never ends and the end of the stream.
00349 
00350         The frame is published by the owner of the test and the end of
00351         the stream follows it: the drain of the channel then holds the
00352         remainder of a frame that never ended.
00353         """
00354         with self.lock:
00355             block = self.partial_pending
00356             self.partial_pending = None
00357         if block is not None:
00358             self.publish_outcome('PARTIAL', block)
00359             self.publish_outcome('EOF')
00360         return block
00361 
00362     def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
00363         with self.lock:
00364             self.next_handle += 1
00365             self.thread_handles.append(self.next_handle)
00366             return self.next_handle
00367 
00368     def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
00369         # The cancellation of the operating system releases the reader
00370         # that is really inside its read: the read ends with the end of
00371         # the stream.  Only the **reader** of the chain is released: the
00372         # cancellation of the writer of the channel never ends the read
00373         # of the reader.
00374         with self.lock:
00375             first = self.thread_handles[0] if self.thread_handles else None
00376         if handle == first:
00377             self.publish_outcome('EOF')
00378         return 'REQUESTED'
00379 
00380     def close_handle(self, handle):
00381         with self.lock:
00382             faulted = (self.fault_handle is not None
00383                        and handle == self.fault_handle
00384                        and not self.fault_released)
00385             if faulted:
00386                 self.fault_hits += 1
00387             self.close_ledger.append({'handle': handle, 'faulted': faulted})
00388             if faulted:
00389                 # The close of the one handle that the owner of the
00390                 # test named fails.  The stable code of the production
00391                 # façade is used and the error is never swallowed by the
00392                 # façade.
00393                 raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
00394             self.closed.append(handle)
00395 
00396     def read_stream(self, handle, size):
00397         with self.lock:
00398             if self.frames:
00399                 return self.frames.pop(0)
00400         # ``READ_WAITING``: the reader is really inside its read and the
00401         # event is published **before** the blocking wait.  The owner of
00402         # the test never waits for an event that the reader publishes
00403         # only after a timeout.
00404         self.read_waiting.set()
00405         try:
00406             kind, payload = self._outcomes.get(timeout=30.0)
00407         except queue.Empty:
00408             # The safety net of the wait.  Every real flow of the group
00409             # publishes its outcome explicitly and the timeout is never
00410             # the way that a state of the chain is reached.
00411             self.eof_observed.set()
00412             return b''
00413         if kind == 'FRAME':
00414             return payload
00415         if kind == 'FAULT':
00416             # An ordinary failure of the read: it is not a cancellation
00417             # and it is not a close of the round.
00418             self.fault_observed.set()
00419             raise payload
00420         if kind == 'PARTIAL':
00421             # A frame that never ends: the buffer of the channel keeps
00422             # the remainder of it.
00423             return payload
00424         self.eof_observed.set()
00425         return b''
00426 
00427     def write_stream(self, handle, data):
00428         # The terminal frame of the round is published by the **write**
00429         # and by nothing else: a reader that reads it before the stop
00430         # was asked for would answer a stop that never happened.
00431         with self.lock:
00432             final = self.final
00433             self.final = b''
00434         if final:
00435             self.publish_outcome('FRAME', final)
00436         return len(data)
00437 
00438 
00439 def _f5_build_chain(api, document, plan, clock, wall, run_root):
00440     """Build the **real** chain of one round.
00441 
00442     The chain is the one of the production launcher: the frames of the
00443     round are published first, then the endpoints, then the channel and
00444     then the handle of the broker.  Nothing of the chain is replaced by
00445     a stand-in and the facts of the close are the facts of the
00446     production.
00447     """
00448     holder = {}
00449     channel_closes = []
00450     channel_facts = []
00451 
00452     class Process:
00453         pid = 4242
00454         returncode = 0
00455 
00456         def wait(self, timeout=None):
00457             return 0
00458 
00459         def terminate(self):
00460             return None
00461 
00462     def launcher(plan_document_, run_root_, nonce):
00463         api.final = _reply_bytes('STOPPED', 'RELEASED', {}, plan, nonce, 3)
00464         api.frames = [_reply_bytes('READY', 'ARMED', {}, plan, nonce, 1),
00465                       _reply_bytes('STATUS', 'RUNNING', {}, plan, nonce, 2)]
00466         read_endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('read'),
00467                                               name='channel-read', api=api,
00468                                               clock=clock, handle=900)
00469         write_endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('write'),
00470                                                name='channel-write', api=api,
00471                                                clock=clock, handle=901)
00472         channel = StreamChannel(None, None, read_endpoint=read_endpoint,
00473                                  write_endpoint=write_endpoint, clock=clock)
00474         original_close = channel.close
00475 
00476         def capturing_close():
00477             channel_closes.append(1)
00478             facts = original_close()
00479             channel_facts.append(facts)
00480             return facts
00481 
00482         channel.close = capturing_close
00483         broker = BrokerProcess(Process(), 'C:/python.exe', 'a' * 64, channel)
00484         holder['broker'] = broker
00485         holder['read_endpoint'] = read_endpoint
00486         holder['write_endpoint'] = write_endpoint
00487         holder['channel'] = channel
00488         return broker
00489 
00490     session = NativeCustodySession(plan=document, run_root=run_root,
00491                                    parent_child_remaining_seconds=21600,
00492                                    launcher=launcher, clock=clock, wall=wall)
00493     return session, holder, channel_closes, channel_facts
00494 
00495 
00496 class _F5ExternalCancelOwner:
00497     """Another owner of the cancel flag of the channel.
00498 
00499     The owner of the flag is a **thread of the test** and it is not the
00500     close of the channel.  It waits for the explicit event that the
00501     reader of the chain is really inside its read, it sets the **same**
00502     cancel flag of the **same** channel **without naming an owner** and
00503     it publishes an explicit confirmation of that fact.
00504 
00505     The owner never asserts and it never raises into the thread of the
00506     owner of the test: a failure of the owner is **reported** to the
00507     owner of the test and the confirmation is published in **every**
00508     case, so that the owner of the test is never left waiting for a
00509     thread that died without a word.
00510     """
00511 
00512     def __init__(self, api, holder):
00513         self.api = api
00514         self.holder = holder
00515         # The explicit confirmation of the owner.  It is published after
00516         # the flag of the cancel was really set and it is published even
00517         # when the owner failed.
00518         self.published = threading.Event()
00519         self.error = None
00520         self.flag_set = None
00521         self.flag_owner = 'NOT_OBSERVED'
00522 
00523     def run(self):
00524         """Set the flag of the cancel and publish the confirmation."""
00525         try:
00526             # The bounded wait of the **explicit** event of the reader:
00527             # the owner never sets the flag before the reader of the
00528             # chain is really inside its read.  The return of the wait
00529             # is checked: a timeout is a failure of the owner and it is
00530             # never a silent guess.
00531             if not self.api.read_waiting.wait(15.0):
00532                 raise AssertionError(
00533                     'the reader of the chain was never inside its read')
00534             channel = self.holder.get('channel')
00535             if channel is None:
00536                 raise AssertionError(
00537                     'the channel of the round was never built')
00538             # The **same** cancel flag of the **same** channel and the
00539             # owner of the flag is **not** named: a cancellation of this
00540             # owner may never be taken for a cancellation of the close
00541             # of the channel and the error of the reader is kept.
00542             channel._cancel.set()
00543             self.flag_set = bool(channel._cancel.is_set())
00544             self.flag_owner = channel._cancel.owner()
00545         except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - it is reported
00546             self.error = error
00547         finally:
00548             # The wake-up of the owner of the test is bounded and it
00549             # happens in every case.
00550             self.published.set()
00551 
00552 
00553 # -- N07-R01: the control handle of an unconfirmed process is kept ---------
00554 
00555 def test_n07_r01_the_control_handle_of_an_unconfirmed_process_is_kept():
00556     """N07-R01 (M03): the last control handle of a process is kept.
00557 
00558     Obligation: an original process whose exit was not confirmed keeps
00559     its control handle and the recovery entry of the API owner; the
00560     thread handle is handled on its own and the primary error and the
00561     secondary errors coexist.  Entry:
00562     ``WindowsApi.create_process_suspended``.  Trigger: the birth query
00563     fails, the termination of the process fails, the wait of the release
00564     times out and the close of the handle would succeed.  Oracle: the
00565     control handle of the process is still with its owner, the thread
00566     handle was closed, the primary error is the birth query and the
00567     secondary errors are named.  Negative: a close that succeeds is
00568     never a proof that the process ended.  Returned state: the retained
00569     handles of the façade name the process and only the process.
00570     Salvage: the recovery of the owner is explicit and it keeps the
00571     handle as long as the exit is not confirmed.
00572     """
00573     def fail_birth(kernel32, *args):
00574         return 0
00575 
00576     def refuse_terminate(kernel32, *args):
00577         return 0
00578 
00579     def timeout_wait(kernel32, *args):
00580         return 258  # WAIT_TIMEOUT
00581 
00582     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'GetProcessTimes': fail_birth,
00583                                          'TerminateProcess': refuse_terminate,
00584                                          'WaitForSingleObject': timeout_wait})
00585     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00586     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
00587         api.create_process_suspended(executable='C:/tools/python.exe', arguments=['-s'],
00588                                      working_directory='C:/round', stdout_handle=0x21,
00589                                      stderr_handle=0x22, environment=None)
00590     # The primary error of the creation is the error that leaves and the
00591     # secondary errors of the release are named next to it.
00592     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_PROCESS_BIRTH_QUERY_FAILED'
00593     detail = info.value.detail or ''
00594     assert 'TerminateProcess' in detail
00595     assert 'WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed' in detail
00596     assert 'process_control_handle_retained:exit_not_confirmed' in detail
00597     # The control handle of the process is with its owner: the process
00598     # may still be alive and the handle is the only thing that may stop
00599     # it.  The thread handle is independent of the exit and it was
00600     # closed on its own.
00601     retained = api.retained_handles()
00602     assert [entry['kind'] for entry in retained] == ['process']
00603     assert retained[0]['owner'] == 'WINDOWS_API'
00604     assert getattr(retained[0]['handle'], 'value', retained[0]['handle']) \
00605         == kernel32.process_handle
00606     assert kernel32.process_handle in kernel32.live
00607     assert kernel32.thread_handle not in kernel32.live
00608     # The recovery of the owner never closes the control handle of a
00609     # process whose exit was not confirmed.
00610     report = api.release_retained()
00611     assert [item['kind'] for item in report] == ['process']
00612     assert report[0]['released'] is False
00613     assert kernel32.process_handle in kernel32.live
00614     assert api.retained_handles(), 'the control handle was forgotten'
00615 
00616 
00617 # -- N07-R02: the recovery keeps the handle until the exit is confirmed ---
00618 
00619 def test_n07_r02_the_recovery_keeps_the_handle_until_the_exit_is_confirmed(tmp_path):
00620     """N07-R02 (M03): the second recovery releases the confirmed handle.
00621 
00622     Obligation: the first coordination of a process whose exit was not
00623     confirmed keeps its control handle; a later coordination that
00624     confirms the exit closes it and forgets it; the recovery never opens
00625     a new budget and a cancellation is uploaded after every safe step.
00626     Entry: ``WindowsApi.release_retained`` and
00627     ``WindowsApi.create_process_suspended``.  Trigger: the wait of the
00628     release times out and the process really ends later.  Oracle: the
00629     first report names the handle as unreleased, the second report names
00630     it as released and the table of the façade is empty.  Negative: the
00631     deadline of the recovery is published once and it is never renewed.
00632     Returned state: the report of the recovery names the failures of
00633     every step.  Salvage: the salvage is the second coordination of the
00634     owner and it is explicit.
00635 
00636     RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 14, F2): the millisecond subcase of
00637     the group builds the **production** core and it built it with
00638     ``run_root=None``.  The production constructor does
00639     ``Path(run_root)`` and it refuses a ``None``: the boundary of the
00640     group never ran at all.  The subcase now drives the core with the
00641     **real** controlled run root of the test (the fixture ``tmp_path``,
00642     which does not change the identity of the node) and the boundary
00643     really executes.  The production is not changed, no permissive
00644     ``None`` fallback is added to it and no assertion of the boundary
00645     is skipped.
00646     """
00647     state = {'exited': False}
00648 
00649     def fail_birth(kernel32, *args):
00650         return 0
00651 
00652     def timeout_wait(kernel32, *args):
00653         return 0 if state['exited'] else 258
00654 
00655     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'GetProcessTimes': fail_birth,
00656                                          'WaitForSingleObject': timeout_wait})
00657     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00658     with pytest.raises(BrokerError):
00659         api.create_process_suspended(executable='C:/tools/python.exe', arguments=['-s'],
00660                                      working_directory='C:/round', stdout_handle=0x21,
00661                                      stderr_handle=0x22, environment=None)
00662     assert [entry['kind'] for entry in api.retained_handles()] == ['process']
00663     # The first coordination keeps the control handle: the exit was not
00664     # confirmed and the process may still be alive.
00665     report = api.release_retained()
00666     assert [item['kind'] for item in report] == ['process']
00667     assert report[0]['released'] is False
00668     assert 'WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed' in report[0]['failures']
00669     assert kernel32.process_handle in kernel32.live
00670     # The process really ends later, inside the original deadline.
00671     state['exited'] = True
00672     report = api.release_retained()
00673     assert [item['kind'] for item in report] == ['process']
00674     assert report[0]['released'] is True
00675     assert api.retained_handles() == []
00676     assert kernel32.process_handle not in kernel32.live
00677 
00678     # The cancellation of the recovery is uploaded after every safe step
00679     # and it never hides the primary error of the creation.
00680     #
00681     # CORRECTION 08 (M03): the second scenario of the group is an
00682     # **independent** scenario.  It has its own state, its own closure
00683     # and its own kernel, so that the confirmed exit of the first
00684     # scenario can never be observed here.  The old group reused the
00685     # state of the first scenario: the exit of the process was already
00686     # confirmed, the control handle had been closed inside the creation
00687     # and the recovery of the façade had nothing left to coordinate.
00688     state_two = {'exited': False}
00689     cancellation = KeyboardInterrupt('terminate_cancelled')
00690     terminations = []
00691 
00692     def fail_birth_two(kernel32, *args):
00693         return 0
00694 
00695     def timeout_wait_two(kernel32, *args):
00696         return 0 if state_two['exited'] else 258
00697 
00698     def cancel_terminate_two(kernel32, *args):
00699         terminations.append(getattr(args[0], 'value', args[0]))
00700         raise cancellation
00701 
00702     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'GetProcessTimes': fail_birth_two,
00703                                          'TerminateProcess': cancel_terminate_two,
00704                                          'WaitForSingleObject': timeout_wait_two})
00705     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00706     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
00707         api.create_process_suspended(executable='C:/tools/python.exe', arguments=['-s'],
00708                                      working_directory='C:/round', stdout_handle=0x21,
00709                                      stderr_handle=0x22, environment=None)
00710     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_PROCESS_BIRTH_QUERY_FAILED'
00711     # The injected cancellation of the termination really happened and
00712     # the cancellation that stays next to the primary error is the
00713     # **original** object and not a copy of its message.
00714     assert terminations, 'the cancellation of the termination never happened'
00715     assert info.value.lvw_cancelled is cancellation
00716     assert isinstance(info.value.lvw_cancelled, KeyboardInterrupt)
00717     # The primary error and the cancellation coexist: the primary error
00718     # is still the error that leaves the creation.
00719     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_PROCESS_BIRTH_QUERY_FAILED'
00720     # The control handle of the process is still with its owner: the
00721     # exit of the process was not confirmed and the recovery of the
00722     # façade can reach the original handle again.
00723     retained = api.retained_handles()
00724     assert [entry['kind'] for entry in retained] == ['process']
00725     assert getattr(retained[0]['handle'], 'value', retained[0]['handle']) \
00726         == kernel32.process_handle
00727     assert kernel32.process_handle in kernel32.live
00728     # The recovery of the façade raises the cancellation of the
00729     # termination and it keeps the control handle of the process.
00730     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt) as recovered:
00731         api.release_retained()
00732     assert recovered.value is cancellation
00733     assert api.retained_handles(), 'the control handle was forgotten'
00734     assert kernel32.process_handle in kernel32.live
00735 
00736     # -- N1 correction 13 (M08-C.2 and PF13-02): the millisecond
00737     #    adapters of the production **floor** the **actual** remaining
00738     #    and they never round it **up**.  A remainder that is smaller
00739     #    than the representable unit asks for **no** wait at all and the
00740     #    exit of a process is never inferred from a requested zero.
00741     #
00742     #    The fixture of the boundary uses a common **zero** clock
00743     #    origin: the remaining that the production measures is then the
00744     #    value that the group declares and not the difference of two
00745     #    large numbers.  A float that is labelled one millisecond need
00746     #    not remain exactly one millisecond after the subtraction of an
00747     #    epoch, and the production floor may refuse it correctly while
00748     #    the group demands one.  The expected values are therefore
00749     #    derived from the **actual** remaining and the rule itself is
00750     #    never weakened.
00751     origin = 0.0
00752     recorded = []
00753 
00754     def record_wait(kernel32, handle, timeout):
00755         recorded.append(timeout)
00756         return 258  # WAIT_TIMEOUT: the exit is not confirmed
00757 
00758     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(
00759         script={'WaitForSingleObject': record_wait})
00760     table = synthetic_table(kernel32)
00761     # The four exact cases: the exact zero, a remainder below the
00762     # representable unit, exactly the unit and a remainder just above
00763     # the unit.
00764     cases = ((0.0, 'zero'), (0.0002, 'below-unit'), (0.001, 'exact-unit'),
00765              (0.0011, 'one-over'))
00766     observed = []
00767     for remaining, label in cases:
00768         actual = remaining - origin
00769         expected = int(actual * 1000)
00770         api = WindowsApi(table=table)
00771         api.bind_deadline(remaining, lambda: origin)
00772         failures = []
00773         cancellations = []
00774         requested = api._wait_timeout_ms()
00775         confirmed = api._confirm_process_exit(
00776             kernel32.process_handle, failures, cancellations,
00777             timeout_ms=requested)
00778         observed.append({'label': label, 'remaining': remaining,
00779                          'actual': actual, 'requested': requested,
00780                          'confirmed': confirmed,
00781                          'failures': list(failures)})
00782         assert requested == expected, \
00783             'the millisecond adapter of the façade did not floor its value'
00784         assert requested * 0.001 <= actual, \
00785             'the requested wait of the façade exceeded the remaining'
00786         assert confirmed is False, \
00787             'the exit of the process was inferred from a requested wait'
00788     facts = {'observed': observed, 'recorded': list(recorded)}
00789     _f5_write('r02-millisecond-floor', facts)
00790     # A remainder that is smaller than the representable unit asks for
00791     # **no** primitive call at all: there is no new positive wait.
00792     assert recorded == [1, 1], \
00793         'the façade asked the primitive for a wait that it did not have'
00794 
00795     # The same boundary through the **recovery** adapter of the façade:
00796     # the recovery of the owner publishes its deadline once and it
00797     # floors the same actual remaining.
00798     recovery_observed = []
00799     for remaining, label in cases:
00800         actual = remaining - origin
00801         expected = int(actual * 1000)
00802         api = WindowsApi(table=table)
00803         api.bind_deadline(remaining, lambda: origin)
00804         failures = []
00805         cancellations = []
00806         requested = api._recovery_timeout_ms()
00807         confirmed = api._confirm_process_exit(
00808             kernel32.process_handle, failures, cancellations,
00809             timeout_ms=requested)
00810         recovery_observed.append({'label': label, 'remaining': remaining,
00811                                   'actual': actual, 'requested': requested,
00812                                   'confirmed': confirmed,
00813                                   'failures': list(failures)})
00814         assert requested == expected, \
00815             'the recovery adapter did not floor the actual remaining'
00816         assert requested * 0.001 <= actual, \
00817             'the requested wait of the recovery exceeded the remaining'
00818         assert confirmed is False, \
00819             'the exit of the process was inferred from a requested wait'
00820     facts = {'recovery': recovery_observed, 'recorded': list(recorded)}
00821     _f5_write('r02-millisecond-floor-recovery', facts)
00822     # The recovery adapter asks the primitive for the two positive
00823     # waits only: the two remainders below the unit ask for nothing.
00824     assert recorded == [1, 1, 1, 1], \
00825         'the recovery asked the primitive for a wait that it did not have'
00826 
00827     # The same boundary through the production adapter of the core: the
00828     # wait of a process is cropped to the **current** cleanup deadline
00829     # of the round and the exit is never inferred from a requested zero.
00830     core_observed = []
00831     for remaining, label in cases:
00832         actual = remaining - origin
00833         expected = int(actual * 1000)
00834         clock = ManualClock(origin)
00835         win32 = RecordingWin32()
00836         # RECONCILIATION (N1 correction 14, F2): the production core
00837         # builds its run root with ``Path(run_root)`` and it refuses a
00838         # ``None``: the boundary of the group is driven with the
00839         # **real** controlled run root of the test and not with a
00840         # permissive fallback.  The production is not changed and no
00841         # assertion of the boundary is skipped.
00842         core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
00843                           clock=clock)
00844         core.context.bind_absolute_deadline(clock.value + 10.0)
00845         core.cleanup_deadline(remaining)
00846         requested = core._wait_timeout_ms()
00847         record = {'process_handle': 1234, 'exited': False, 'resumed': True,
00848                   'role': 'PROBE'}
00849         core._wait_one_process(record)
00850         calls = [call for call in win32.calls if call[0] == 'wait_process']
00851         core_observed.append({'label': label, 'remaining': remaining,
00852                               'actual': actual, 'requested': requested,
00853                               'calls': [call[2] for call in calls],
00854                               'exited': record['exited']})
00855         assert requested == expected, \
00856             'the millisecond adapter of the core did not floor its value'
00857         assert requested * 0.001 <= actual, \
00858             'the requested wait of the core exceeded the remaining'
00859         if expected == 0:
00860             assert not calls, \
00861                 'the core asked the primitive for a wait that it did not have'
00862             assert record['exited'] is False, \
00863                 'the exit of the process was inferred from a requested zero'
00864         else:
00865             assert calls and calls[0][2] == expected, \
00866                 'the core did not ask the primitive for the cropped wait'
00867     facts = {'core': core_observed}
00868     _f5_write('r02-millisecond-floor-core', facts)
