00871 # -- N07-R03: a pipe that was handed over has exactly one owner -----------
00872 
00873 def test_n07_r03_a_pipe_that_was_handed_over_has_exactly_one_owner(tmp_path):
00874     """N07-R03 (M05): the handover of a pipe has one owner.
00875 
00876     Obligation: the ends of a pipe that were created are handed over to
00877     the core and to its readers in one provable step; the API owner
00878     never closes an end that it handed over and the tables of the two
00879     owners agree.  Entry: ``WindowsApi.create_pipe``,
00880     ``WindowsApi.handover`` and ``BrokerCore._launch_role``.  Trigger: a
00881     successful creation of the two pipes of a role.  Oracle: the façade
00882     does not name the ends any more, the core names them with their
00883     reader as the owner and the recovery of the façade is empty.
00884     Negative: the recovery of the façade never closes an end that a live
00885     reader reads from.  Returned state: the table of the façade is
00886     empty and the table of the core names the ends.  Salvage: the core
00887     releases its own ends.
00888     """
00889     def create_pipe(kernel32, *args):
00890         read = kernel32.allocate('pipe_read')
00891         write = kernel32.allocate('pipe_write')
00892         args[0]._obj.value = read
00893         args[1]._obj.value = write
00894         return 1
00895 
00896     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe})
00897     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00898     core = BrokerCore(win32=api, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
00899     try:
00900         core.arm()
00901         core.launch()
00902         # The façade does not name the ends of the pipes any more: the
00903         # core and its readers are their only owner.
00904         kinds = sorted(entry['kind'] for entry in api.retained_handles())
00905         assert kinds == [], 'the facade still names a handed over handle'
00906         assert api.handovers(), 'the handover was not recorded'
00907         # The table of the core names the read ends with their reader as
00908         # the owner and the owner is an object and not a name.
00909         readers = [item for item in core._pending.pending()
00910                    if item['owner'] == 'READER']
00911         assert readers, 'the readers do not own their read end'
00912         for item in readers:
00913             assert item['owner_ref'] is not None
00914         # The recovery of the façade never closes an end that the core
00915         # owns: it has nothing to release.
00916         report = api.release_retained()
00917         assert report == []
00918         assert api.retained_handles() == []
00919     finally:
00920         core.stop('STOP')
00921 
00922 
00923 # -- N07-R04: the ends of a pipe are cleaned up independently -------------
00924 
00925 def test_n07_r04_the_ends_of_a_pipe_are_cleaned_up_independently():
00926     """N07-R04 (M05): the cleanup of the ends of a pipe is independent.
00927 
00928     Obligation: the two ends of a pipe are closed on their own when the
00929     inherit flag could not be cleared; a cancellation of the inherit
00930     flag and a cancellation of the close of one end never skip the close
00931     of the other end; the end that could not be closed keeps its owner
00932     and the cancellation propagates with the primary error next to it.
00933     Entry: ``WindowsApi.create_pipe``.  Trigger: the inherit flag of the
00934     read end raises a cancellation and the close of the read end raises
00935     a cancellation.  Oracle: the safe close of both ends ran, the
00936     cancellation is the error that leaves and the primary error stays
00937     reachable.  Negative: a cancellation never becomes a string and it
00938     never hides the error that caused the pipe.  Returned state: the
00939     table of the façade names the end that is still open.  Salvage: the
00940     recovery of the owner closes the end that is still open.
00941     """
00942     def create_pipe(kernel32, *args):
00943         read = kernel32.allocate('pipe_read')
00944         write = kernel32.allocate('pipe_write')
00945         kernel32.pipe_read_handle = read
00946         kernel32.pipe_write_handle = write
00947         args[0]._obj.value = read
00948         args[1]._obj.value = write
00949         return 1
00950 
00951     def cancel_inherit(kernel32, *args):
00952         raise KeyboardInterrupt('inherit_cancelled')
00953 
00954     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe,
00955                                          'SetHandleInformation': cancel_inherit})
00956     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00957     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt) as info:
00958         api.create_pipe('stdout')
00959     # The cancellation is the error that leaves and it is not a string.
00960     assert isinstance(info.value, KeyboardInterrupt)
00961     # The safe close of both ends ran before the cancellation left.
00962     assert 'CloseHandle' in kernel32.names()
00963     assert not kernel32.live
00964     assert api.retained_handles() == []
00965 
00966     def refuse_inherit(kernel32, *args):
00967         return 0
00968 
00969     def cancel_read_close(kernel32, *args):
00970         handle = getattr(args[0], 'value', args[0])
00971         if handle == kernel32.pipe_read_handle:
00972             raise KeyboardInterrupt('read_close_cancelled')
00973         kernel32.live.pop(handle, None)
00974         return 1
00975 
00976     kernel32 = SyntheticKernel32(script={'CreatePipe': create_pipe,
00977                                          'SetHandleInformation': refuse_inherit,
00978                                          'CloseHandle': cancel_read_close})
00979     api = WindowsApi(table=synthetic_table(kernel32))
00980     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt) as info:
00981         api.create_pipe('stdout')
00982     # The write end was closed even though the close of the read end was
00983     # cancelled.
00984     assert kernel32.pipe_write_handle not in kernel32.live
00985     # The end that could not be closed keeps its original reference with
00986     # the façade.
00987     retained = api.retained_handles()
00988     assert [entry['kind'] for entry in retained] == ['pipe_read']
00989     assert retained[0]['owner'] == 'WINDOWS_API'
00990     # The primary error and the cancellation coexist: the cancellation is
00991     # not a BrokerError and the primary error stays reachable.
00992     assert isinstance(info.value, KeyboardInterrupt)
00993     assert isinstance(info.value.lvw_primary, BrokerError)
00994     assert info.value.lvw_primary.code == 'BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED'
00995 
00996 
00997 # -- N07-R05: the read end of a live reader is never closed ---------------
00998 
00999 def test_n07_r05_the_read_end_of_a_live_reader_is_never_closed(tmp_path):
01000     """N07-R05 (M06): the read end of a live reader is protected.
01001 
01002     Obligation: the read end of every reader is protected by its owner
01003     in every branch of the coordination - a cancellation of the wait, a
01004     cancellation of the join and an ordinary failure of the
01005     coordination; the readers that follow are still coordinated and the
01006     pending pass refuses to close an end whose owner is still using it.
01007     Entry: ``BrokerCore._close_readers``, ``BrokerCore._close_one_reader``
01008     and ``BrokerCore._close_pending``.  Trigger: the wait of a live
01009     reader is cancelled and the coordination of another reader fails.
01010     Oracle: the read ends of the live readers are not closed, the other
01011     reader was cleaned up and the pending pass names the end that it
01012     refused to close.  Negative: the pending pass never closes an end
01013     under a live owner.  Returned state: the states of the readers and
01014     the failures of the core name every reader.  Salvage: the owner of
01015     the live reader releases it later.
01016     """
01017     class LiveReader:
01018         """A reader that is alive and whose wait is cancelled."""
01019 
01020         def __init__(self, handle, name):
01021             self.handle = handle
01022             self.name = name
01023             self.error = None
01024             self.ident = 1
01025             self.finished = _CancellingEvent()
01026 
01027         def is_alive(self):
01028             return True
01029 
01030         def join(self, timeout=None):
01031             raise KeyboardInterrupt('reader_join_cancelled')
01032 
01033         def stop(self):
01034             return None
01035 
01036     class DeadReader:
01037         """A reader that really stopped."""
01038 
01039         def __init__(self, handle, name):
01040             self.handle = handle
01041             self.name = name
01042             self.error = None
01043             self.ident = 1
01044             self.finished = threading.Event()
01045             self.finished.set()
01046 
01047         def is_alive(self):
01048             return False
01049 
01050         def join(self, timeout=None):
01051             return None
01052 
01053         def stop(self):
01054             return None
01055 
01056     class UnansweringReader:
01057         """A reader that cannot say whether it is alive."""
01058 
01059         def __init__(self, handle, name):
01060             self.handle = handle
01061             self.name = name
01062             self.error = None
01063             self.ident = 1
01064             self.finished = threading.Event()
01065 
01066         def is_alive(self):
01067             raise RuntimeError('alive_unknown')
01068 
01069         def join(self, timeout=None):
01070             raise RuntimeError('alive_unknown')
01071 
01072         def stop(self):
01073             return None
01074 
01075     win32 = RecordingWin32()
01076     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01077     core.arm()
01078     # The three read ends are handles of the round: the façade of the
01079     # primitives owns them until they are closed.
01080     for handle in (0x51, 0x52, 0x53):
01081         win32.live[handle] = 'pipe_read'
01082     dead = DeadReader(0x52, 'dead-reader')
01083     live = LiveReader(0x51, 'live-reader')
01084     blind = UnansweringReader(0x53, 'blind-reader')
01085     for reader, handle in ((dead, 0x52), (live, 0x51), (blind, 0x53)):
01086         core.readers.append(reader)
01087         core._pending.add(handle, 'pipe_read', role='COMFY')
01088         core._pending.transfer(handle, 'READER', owner_ref=reader)
01089     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
01090         core._close_readers()
01091     # The read end of the live reader was not closed and the reader that
01092     # really stopped was cleaned up.
01093     assert 0x51 not in win32.closed
01094     assert 0x51 in win32.live
01095     assert 0x52 in win32.closed
01096     names = [state['name'] for state in core.reader_states]
01097     assert 'dead-reader' in names
01098     assert 'live-reader' in names
01099     # The pending pass refuses to close the end whose owner cannot say
01100     # that it stopped: the guard is the fact of the owner.
01101     core._close_pending()
01102     assert 0x53 not in win32.closed
01103     assert 0x53 in win32.live
01104     assert any(item.get('code') == 'BROKER_PENDING_HANDLE_IN_USE'
01105                for item in core.failures)
01106 
01107 
01108 # -- N07-R06: the cancellation of a wait reaches the top ------------------
01109 
01110 def test_n07_r06_the_cancellation_of_a_wait_reaches_the_top(tmp_path):
01111     """N07-R06 (M06): a cancellation of a wait is never swallowed.
01112 
01113     Obligation: a cancellation of the wait of a process and a
01114     cancellation of the reading of its exit code are propagated to the
01115     caller of the stop; the processes that follow are still coordinated.
01116     Entry: ``BrokerCore.stop``, ``BrokerCore._wait_the_processes`` and
01117     ``BrokerCore._wait_one_process``.  Trigger: the wait of the first
01118     process raises a cancellation and the exit code of the second raises
01119     a cancellation.  Oracle: the cancellation is the error that leaves
01120     the stop and it is named as a cancellation.  Negative: the
01121     cancellation never becomes an ordinary evidence of the round.
01122     Returned state: the failures of the core name the cancellation and
01123     the later processes were waited for.  Salvage: the handles of the
01124     round are released by their owner.
01125     """
01126     class CancellingWaitWin32(RecordingWin32):
01127         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
01128             super().__init__(**kwargs)
01129             self.cancelled = False
01130 
01131         def wait_process(self, process, timeout_ms):
01132             self.calls.append(('wait_process', process, timeout_ms))
01133             if not self.cancelled:
01134                 self.cancelled = True
01135                 raise KeyboardInterrupt('wait_cancelled')
01136             for entry in self.created:
01137                 if entry['handle'] == process:
01138                     return entry['exited']
01139             return True
01140 
01141     win32 = CancellingWaitWin32()
01142     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01143     core.arm()
01144     core.launch()
01145     with pytest.raises(KeyboardInterrupt):
01146         core.stop('STOP')
01147     cancellations = [item for item in core.failures if item.get('cancellation')]
01148     assert cancellations, 'the cancellation was not named'
01149     assert any(item['stage'] == 'wait' for item in cancellations)
01150     # The processes that follow the cancelled one were still waited for.
01151     waited = [call for call in win32.calls if call[0] == 'wait_process']
01152     assert len(waited) >= 2, 'the wait of the next process never ran'
01153 
01154     # The second sub path drives the **exit code** of the process: the
01155     # cancellation of the exit code reaches the top and it is the
01156     # original object that leaves the stop.
01157     #
01158     # CORRECTION 08 (M06): the contract froze the **propagation** of the
01159     # cancellation and the fact that its origin is verifiable.  It did
01160     # not freeze the internal stage of the failure: the production
01161     # names the stage of the **group** of the processes and the stage of
01162     # the origin is not a stage that the production publishes.  The
01163     # group therefore names the stage that the production really
01164     # publishes and it proves the origin of the cancellation in three
01165     # further ways: the number of the injections, the identity of the
01166     # original SystemExit and the order of the calls of the façade.
01167     class CancellingExitWin32(RecordingWin32):
01168         """A façade whose exit code raises a cancellation."""
01169 
01170         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
01171             super().__init__(**kwargs)
01172             self.exit_code_calls = 0
01173             self.cancellation = SystemExit('exit_cancelled')
01174 
01175         def exit_code(self, process):
01176             self.calls.append(('exit_code', process))
01177             self.exit_code_calls += 1
01178             raise self.cancellation
01179 
01180     win32 = CancellingExitWin32()
01181     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01182     core.arm()
01183     core.launch()
01184     with pytest.raises(SystemExit) as info:
01185         core.stop('STOP')
01186     # The **original** object of the cancellation is the error that
01187     # leaves the stop: it is never replaced by a copy of a message.
01188     assert info.value is win32.cancellation
01189     # The cancellation was really injected by the method of the exit
01190     # code: the number of the injections is recorded and it is not zero.
01191     assert win32.exit_code_calls >= 1, 'the exit code was never read'
01192     # The stage of the group of the processes is the stage that the
01193     # production publishes and the type of the error is the type of the
01194     # injected cancellation.
01195     cancellations = [item for item in core.failures if item.get('cancellation')]
01196     assert cancellations, 'the cancellation of the exit code was not named'
01197     assert any(item['stage'] == 'wait' for item in cancellations)
01198     assert any(item['error_type'] == 'SystemExit' for item in cancellations)
01199     # The origin of the cancellation is the exit code and not the wait:
01200     # the wait of the process really completed before the exit code was
01201     # read.
01202     names = [call[0] for call in win32.calls]
01203     assert 'wait_process' in names
01204     assert names.index('exit_code') > names.index('wait_process')
01205     # The processes that follow the cancelled one were still waited
01206     # for and their original handles were really released.
01207     assert len([call for call in win32.calls
01208                 if call[0] == 'wait_process']) >= 2, \
01209         'the wait of the next process never ran'
01210     for entry in win32.created:
01211         assert entry['handle'] not in win32.live
01212         assert entry['thread'] not in win32.live
01213 
01214 
01215 # -- N07-R07: the recovery of a record synchronises the tables ------------
01216 
01217 def test_n07_r07_the_recovery_of_a_record_synchronises_the_tables(tmp_path):
01218     """N07-R07 (M06): the recovery of a handle updates every table.
01219 
01220     Obligation: the close of a handle that could not be closed keeps its
01221     owner and its name; the recovery that closes it really updates the
01222     pending table, the record of the round and the retained resources of
01223     the round.  Entry: ``BrokerCore.stop`` and ``BrokerCore._close_handle``.
01224     Trigger: the close of the job handle of the round fails twice.
01225     Oracle: the second stop closes the handle, the record and the
01226     retained resources of the round agree with the fact of the close and
01227     the handle was closed exactly once.  Negative: a handle that was
01228     really closed is never named as a resource any more and it is never
01229     closed twice.  Returned state: the tables of the core are empty.
01230     Salvage: the handles that are still open are released by the owner
01231     of the façade.
01232     """
01233     class SlowWin32(RecordingWin32):
01234         """A façade whose close of the job handle fails twice."""
01235 
01236         def __init__(self, **kwargs):
01237             super().__init__(**kwargs)
01238             self.attempts = {}
01239 
01240         def close_handle(self, handle):
01241             self.calls.append(('close_handle', handle))
01242             if handle == getattr(self, 'job_handle', None):
01243                 count = self.attempts.get(handle, 0)
01244                 if count < 2:
01245                     self.attempts[handle] = count + 1
01246                     raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
01247             super().close_handle(handle)
01248 
01249     win32 = SlowWin32()
01250     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path)
01251     core.arm()
01252     core.launch()
01253     job = core.job
01254     win32.job_handle = job
01255     try:
01256         core.stop('STOP')
01257         # The handle that could not be closed keeps its owner and it is
01258         # named: it is never replaced by a copy of an error message.
01259         assert core.job is job
01260         assert job in win32.live
01261         retained = [item for item in core._retained_resources
01262                     if item['handle'] is job]
01263         assert retained, 'the handle that could not be closed was not named'
01264         assert retained[0]['owner'] == 'CORE_RETAINED'
01265         pending = [item for item in core._pending.pending()
01266                    if item['handle'] == job]
01267         assert pending, 'the handle that could not be closed lost its owner'
01268         # The second stop coordinates the handle again and every table of
01269         # the core agrees with the fact of the close.
01270         core.stop('STOP')
01271         assert core.job is None
01272         assert core.job_closed is True
01273         assert core._pending.pending() == []
01274         assert core._retained_resources == []
01275         assert job not in win32.live
01276         for record in core.records:
01277             assert record['process_handle'] is None
01278             assert record['thread_handle'] is None
01279             assert record['process_handle_closed'] is True
01280             assert record['thread_handle_closed'] is True
01281         # The handle was closed exactly once.
01282         assert len(win32.closed) == len(set(win32.closed)), \
01283             'a handle was closed twice'
01284     finally:
01285         for handle in list(win32.live):
01286             RecordingWin32.close_handle(win32, handle)
01287

01289 # -- N07-R08: the endpoint owns its worker before the start ---------------
01290 
01291 def test_n07_r08_the_endpoint_owns_its_worker_before_the_start():
01292     """N07-R08 (M07): the endpoint owns the worker from its creation.
01293 
01294     Obligation: a worker of an endpoint is owned by the endpoint before
01295     its thread starts; a start that did not return keeps its owner and
01296     the close of the endpoint never closes the file under a worker that
01297     is starting or that is inside its operation.  Entry:
01298     ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint._start`` and ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint.close``.
01299     Trigger: the start of the worker raises before the thread exists and
01300     a start that raises after the thread really started.  Oracle: the
01301     endpoint names the worker in both cases and the file is closed only
01302     after the worker really stopped.  Negative: the handle of the
01303     thread of the worker is closed before the file.  Returned state: the
01304     facts of the endpoint name the retained worker.  Salvage: the owner
01305     of the worker stops it later.
01306     """
01307     order = []
01308 
01309     class Api:
01310         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01311             return 701
01312 
01313         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
01314             return 'NOT_FOUND'
01315 
01316         def close_handle(self, handle):
01317             order.append('thread')
01318 
01319     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
01320     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('read'), name='inprocess',
01321                                     api=Api(), clock=clock, handle=702)
01322     endpoint._file.close = lambda: order.append('file')
01323 
01324     class NeverStartedWorker(broker_module._WindowsPipeWorker):
01325         def start(self):
01326             raise RuntimeError('worker_start_failed')
01327 
01328     original = broker_module._WindowsPipeWorker
01329     broker_module._WindowsPipeWorker = NeverStartedWorker
01330     try:
01331         with pytest.raises(RuntimeError):
01332             endpoint.read(None, None)
01333         # The endpoint is the owner of the worker from the moment of its
01334         # creation: the start that did not return never lost it.
01335         assert endpoint._reader is not None
01336         assert endpoint._reader in endpoint._retained
01337         assert endpoint.facts['retained_workers'] == 1
01338         # The close of the endpoint never closes the file under a worker
01339         # that never came up.
01340         facts = endpoint.close()
01341         assert facts['closed'] is False
01342         assert 'file' not in order
01343     finally:
01344         broker_module._WindowsPipeWorker = original
01345 
01346     order = []
01347     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('read'), name='inprocess',
01348                                     api=Api(), clock=clock, handle=702)
01349     endpoint._file.close = lambda: order.append('file')
01350 
01351     class StartedThenFailedWorker(broker_module._WindowsPipeWorker):
01352         def start(self):
01353             super().start()
01354             raise RuntimeError('worker_start_failed_after_start')

01364         # handle of its thread is closed before the file.

01368             'the file was closed before the worker really stopped'

01373 # -- N07-R09: the launcher keeps the owner of the process and the channel --
01374 
01375 def test_n07_r09_the_launcher_keeps_the_owner_of_the_process_and_the_channel(tmp_path):
01376     """N07-R09 (M07): the launcher owns the process and the channel.
01377 
01378     Obligation: the original process of the round is registered with the
01379     launcher from the moment of its creation; a channel that exists and
01380     that could not be handed over is closed by the launcher through the
01381     channel and never by closing the standard input and the standard
01382     output of the process by itself; a resource that could not be
01383     released keeps its original reference.  Entry:
01384     ``_adopt_broker_process``, ``_LauncherCustody`` and
01385     ``release_retained_launches``.  Trigger: the construction of the
01386     handle of the broker fails and the termination of the process fails.
01387     Oracle: the channel was closed, the files of the process were not
01388     closed by the launcher and the process keeps its owner.  Negative: a
01389     pid is never used to find a process again and the table of the
01390     launcher never names the same resource twice.  Returned state: the
01391     table of the launcher names the process.  Salvage: the recovery of
01392     the launcher is explicit and it names what it kept.
01393 
01394     CORRECTION 12 (M07-A): the launch that never built its channel
01395     owns the **original raw streams** that it could not close, and the
01396     **same** owner retries them.  A retained reference without a
01397     recovery action is not an ownership: the first release returns the
01398     original reference of the raw stream, the second release really
01399     closes it, the confirmed close happens exactly once and the table of
01400     the launcher names the stream no more.  The process and its
01401     original handle are never released a second time.
01402     """
01403     class Process:
01404         pid = 4242
01405         returncode = None
01406 
01407         def __init__(self):
01408             self.terminated = 0
01409             self.waited = 0
01410             self.closed = []
01411             self.stdin = type('S', (), {'close': self._close})()
01412             self.stdout = type('S', (), {'close': self._close})()
01413 
01414         def _close(self):
01415             self.closed.append('stream')
01416 
01417         def terminate(self):
01418             self.terminated += 1
01419             raise RuntimeError('terminate_failed')
01420 
01421         def wait(self, timeout=None):
01422             self.waited += 1
01423             raise RuntimeError('wait_failed')
01424 
01425     class Channel:
01426         reader_finished = True
01427 
01428         def __init__(self):
01429             self.closes = 0
01430 
01431         def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
01432             self.deadline = deadline
01433 
01434         def close(self):
01435             self.closes += 1
01436             return {'write_end_closed': True, 'read_end_closed': True,
01437                     'reader_finished': True, 'reader_error': None,
01438                     'write_retained_workers': 0, 'read_retained_workers': 0,
01439                     'worker_thread_handle_released': True}

01453                                   executable='C:/python.exe', executable_sha256='a' * 64)

01460     assert process.terminated == 1
01461     assert process.waited == 1

01469     # the handover removes the resource from it.

01471     custody.retain('process', process, 'first')

01475     assert custody.items() == []
01476     # The recovery of the launcher names the resources that it kept.
01477     custody.retain('process', process, 'kept')
01478     report = custody.release()
01479     assert report['retained'] == ['process']
01480     # The public recovery entry of the launcher of the package is the
01481     # same finite entry and it never loses the original reference.
01482     public = release_retained_launches()
01483     assert 'process' in public['retained']
01484 
01485     # -- N1 correction 11 (M07): the aggregate forgets the process of a
01486     #    launch **only** when its exit was confirmed **and** its
01487     #    original handle was released.  A close of the handle that
01488     #    failed keeps the process with its owner, and the **same** owner
01489     #    retries only the incomplete part: it never terminates and never
01490     #    waits a second time.  The table of the launcher never
01491     #    resurrects a process that was already forgotten.
01492     class Handle:
01493         """The original handle of a process of a launch."""
01494 
01495         def __init__(self):
01496             self.closes = 0
01497             self.fail = True
01498 
01499         def Close(self):
01500             self.closes += 1
01501             if self.fail:
01502                 raise RuntimeError('handle_close_failed')
01503             return True
01504

01515

01517             self.terminated += 1

01520             self.waited += 1

01645     # -- N1 correction 13 (M07-A.1): the **ownership history** of the
01646     #    original files survives the completion of the channel.  A
01647     #    channel that really closed the original files of the process
01648     #    keeps them: a later release of the same owner may not become
01649     #    their raw closer, not even when the active slot of the channel
01650     #    was cleared and the original HANDLE of the process still has
01651     #    to be retried.
01652     class CountingFile:
01653         """One original file that counts its own closes."""
01654 
01655         def __init__(self, name):
01656             self.name = name
01657             self.closes = 0
01658 
01659         def close(self):
01660             self.closes += 1
01661             return True
01662 
01663     class FailingHandle:
01664         """The original HANDLE of a process: its first close fails."""
01665 
01666         def __init__(self):
01667             self.attempts = 0
01668             self.closed = 0
01669 
01670         def Close(self):
01671             self.attempts += 1
01672             if self.attempts == 1:
01673                 raise RuntimeError('handle_close_failed')
01674             self.closed += 1
01675             return True
01676 
01677     class HistoryProcess:
01678         """A process whose original files are taken by a real channel."""
01679 
01680         pid = 4244
01681         returncode = 0
01682 
01683         def __init__(self):
01684             self.stdin = CountingFile('stdin')
01685             self.stdout = CountingFile('stdout')
01686             self._handle = FailingHandle()
01687             self.terminated = 0
01688             self.waited = 0
01689 
01690         def terminate(self):
01691             self.terminated += 1
01692 
01693         def wait(self, timeout=None):
01694             self.waited += 1
01695             return 0

01697     class HistoryApi:
01698         """The primitive table of the endpoints of the real channel."""
01699 
01700         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01701             return 704
01702

01704             return 'REQUESTED'
01705 
01706         def close_handle(self, handle):
01707             return None
01708 
01709         def read_stream(self, handle, size):
01710             # The reader of the channel really ends: the end of the
01711             # file is the ordinary end of the read.
01712             return b''

01763                                        HistoryOsView()):
01764             first = history_custody.release()
01765         facts = {'first': first,
01766                  'stdin_closes': history_process.stdin.closes,
01767                  'stdout_closes': history_process.stdout.closes,
01768                  'handle_attempts': history_process._handle.attempts,
01769                  'handle_closed': history_process._handle.closed,
01770                  'terminated': history_process.terminated,
01771                  'waited': history_process.waited,
01772                  'raw_streams': [item['name']
01773                                  for item in history_aggregate.raw_streams],
01774                  'process_owned': history_aggregate.process is history_process}
01775         _f5_write('r09-completed-channel-first', facts)
01776         assert history_process.stdin.closes == 1, \
01777             'the channel did not close the original input of the process'
01778         assert history_process.stdout.closes == 1, \
01779             'the channel did not close the original output of the process'
01780         assert history_process._handle.attempts == 1, \
01781             'the original handle of the process was not closed once'
01782         assert history_process._handle.closed == 0, \
01783             'the failing close of the original handle was read as a close'
01784         assert history_process.terminated == 1
01785         assert history_process.waited == 1
01786         # The original process keeps its owner: the original HANDLE
01787         # could not be released.
01788         assert history_aggregate.process is history_process, \
01789             'the process whose handle failed lost its owner'
01790         # The launch owns **no** raw stream: the channel took the
01791         # original files and it closed them.
01792         assert history_aggregate.raw_streams == [], \
01793             'the launch owns a raw stream that the channel took'
01794         # The second release of the **same** owner: the exit is not
01795         # confirmed again, the original HANDLE is retried and the
01796         # original files of the process are **not** closed a second time.
01797         with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os',
01798                                        HistoryOsView()):
01799             second = history_custody.release()
01800         facts = {'second': second,
01801                  'stdin_closes': history_process.stdin.closes,
01802                  'stdout_closes': history_process.stdout.closes,
01803                  'handle_attempts': history_process._handle.attempts,
01804                  'handle_closed': history_process._handle.closed,
01805                  'terminated': history_process.terminated,
01806                  'waited': history_process.waited,
01807                  'raw_streams': [item['name']
01808                                  for item in history_aggregate.raw_streams],
01809                  'process_owned': history_aggregate.process is history_process}
01810         _f5_write('r09-completed-channel-second', facts)
01811         assert history_process.stdin.closes == 1, \
01812             'the retry of the launch closed the original input a second time'
01813         assert history_process.stdout.closes == 1, \
01814             'the retry of the launch closed the original output a second time'
01815         assert history_process._handle.attempts == 2, \
01816             'the retry of the launch did not retry the original handle'
01817         assert history_process._handle.closed == 1, \
01818             'the retry of the launch did not close the original handle'
01819         assert history_process.terminated == 1, \
01820             'the retry of the launch terminated the process a second time'
01821         assert history_process.waited == 1, \
01822             'the retry of the launch waited for the process a second time'
01823         assert history_aggregate.process is None, \
01824             'the process whose handle was released kept its owner'
01825         assert history_aggregate.raw_streams == [], \
01826             'the launch owns a raw stream that the channel closed'
01827     finally:
01828         # The teardown of the owner of the test is unconditional: the
01829         # channel is closed and its thread is joined with a bounded
01830         # wait.
01831         try:
01832             history_channel.close()
01833         except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the evidence keeps the failure
01834             pass
01835         if history_thread is not None:
01836             history_thread.join(10.0)

01839 # -- N07-R10: a closer whose start did not return is unknown --------------
01840 
01841 def test_n07_r10_a_closer_whose_start_did_not_return_is_unknown(tmp_path):
01842     """N07-R10 (M07): a start that did not return is unknown.
01843 
01844     Obligation: a closer whose start did not return is **unknown** and
01845     not a thread that ended; the original owner of the closer is kept and
01846     the round stays unknown; a later attempt may try again.  Entry:
01847     ``NativeCustodySession._start_bounded_close`` and
01848     ``NativeCustodySession._await_closer_exit``.  Trigger: the start of
01849     the closer raises before the thread exists.  Oracle: the failure is
01850     named, the state of the start is ``STARTING`` and the wait for the
01851     closer reports that the state is unknown.  Negative: a join that
01852     raises because the thread was never started is never read as the end
01853     of the closer.  Returned state: the evidence names the unknown
01854     closer.  Salvage: a later attempt of the close is still possible.
01855     """
01856     session, _holder, _clock = make_session(tmp_path)
01857     session.start()
01858 
01859     class NeverStartedThread:
01860         def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
01861             self.ident = None
01862             self.daemon = True
01863 
01864         def start(self):
01865             raise RuntimeError('closer_start_failed')
01866 
01867         def is_alive(self):
01868             return False
01869 
01870         def join(self, timeout=None):
01871             raise RuntimeError('cannot join thread before it is started')
01872 
01873     with unittest.mock.patch('tools.v0_s3_native_custody.threading.Thread',
01874                               NeverStartedThread):
01875         assert session._start_bounded_close() is False
01876     evidence = session.evidence()
01877     assert any('CUSTODY_CLOSER_START_FAILED' in failure
01878                for failure in evidence['failures'])
01879     assert evidence['closer_start_state'] == 'STARTING'
01880     assert evidence['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
01881     # The original owner of the closer is kept: the reference was never
01882     # dropped and the closer is never taken for a thread that ended.
01883     assert session._closer is not None
01884     assert session._closer in session._closer_threads
01885     # The wait for the closer reports that the state is unknown and it
01886     # never claims that the closer ended.
01887     assert session._await_close() is False
01888     assert 'CUSTODY_CLOSER_START_UNKNOWN' in session.evidence()['failures']
01889     assert session.evidence()['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
01890     # The latch of the closer is not permanent: a later attempt may try
01891     # again and the public stop of the round is the recovery entry.
01892     assert session._closer_started is False
01893     session.stop()
01894     assert session.evidence()['state'] == 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'

01897 # -- N07-R11: the cancellation and the operation are one atomic step ------
01898 
01899 def test_n07_r11_the_cancellation_of_a_worker_is_repeated_and_bounded():
01900     """N07-R11 (M08): the cancellation of a worker is really repeated.
01901 
01902     Obligation: the cancellation of the operation of a worker is
01903     repeated while the operation is in flight and it is never released
01904     by the stop event that the cancellation set itself; the completion of
01905     an older generation is never the completion of this one; a request
01906     that waits for the worker is never performed after the cancellation.
01907     Entry: ``_WindowsPipeWorker.request_cancel``,
01908     ``_WindowsPipeWorker.submit`` and ``_WindowsPipeWorker.run``.
01909     Trigger: the cancellation of the operating system reports that the
01910     operation was not found.  Oracle: the request was really repeated
01911     and the queued request was never performed.  Negative: the retry
01912     never waits on the stop event of the worker and it never uses the
01913     done of an older generation.  Returned state: the state of the
01914     worker names the cancellation.  Salvage: the owner of the worker
01915     releases it.
01916     """
01917     entered = threading.Event()
01918     may_enter_io = threading.Event()
01919     second_entered = threading.Event()
01920 
01921     class Api:
01922         def __init__(self):
01923             self.lock = threading.Lock()
01924             self.cancels = []
01925 
01926         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01927             return 701
01928 
01929         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
01930             with self.lock:
01931                 self.cancels.append(handle)
01932             return 'NOT_FOUND'
01933 
01934         def close_handle(self, handle):
01935             return None
01936 
01937     api = Api()
01938 
01939     def action(request):
01940         # The worker is inside the operation of the round.  The test
01941         # decides when the operation may really enter the system call,
01942         # so that the cancellation of the round is issued inside the
01943         # window that the check of the worker opens.
01944         entered.set()
01945         if request == b'second':
01946             second_entered.set()
01947         may_enter_io.wait(5.0)
01948         return b''
01949 
01950     worker = _WindowsPipeWorker(api, 702, kind='read', action=action,
01951                                 name='n07-r11-worker')
01952     worker.start()
01953     try:
01954         worker.wait_ready(2.0)
01955         generation = worker.submit(b'first')
01956         assert entered.wait(5.0), 'the worker never entered the operation'
01957         # The second request waits for the worker while the first
01958         # operation is in flight.
01959         worker.submit(b'second')
01960         # The cancellation is issued while the first operation runs and
01961         # the operating system reports that the operation was not found.
01962         state = worker.request_cancel(time.monotonic() + 0.3)
01963         assert state == 'NOT_FOUND'
01964         # The request was really repeated: the cancellation was not sent
01965         # once and dropped, and the retry never waited on the stop event
01966         # that it set itself.
01967         with api.lock:
01968             repeats = len(api.cancels)
01969         assert repeats >= 2, 'the cancellation was sent once only'
01970         # The completion of an older generation is never the completion of
01971         # this one.
01972         assert worker.wait_done(generation + 1, 0.05) is False
01973         # The operation may now really enter the system call and end.
01974         may_enter_io.set()
01975         # The request that was in flight really completes.
01976         assert worker.wait_done(generation, 5.0), \
01977             'the request that was in flight never completed'
01978         worker.join_bounded(5.0)
01979         assert not worker.is_alive()
01980         # The request that was queued was never performed and it was
01981         # never completed: the cancellation of the round reached the
01982         # worker before the operation could start.
01983         assert not second_entered.is_set(), \
01984             'the queued request was performed after the cancellation'
01985         assert worker.wait_done(generation + 1, 0.05) is False
01986     finally:
01987         may_enter_io.set()
01988         worker.stop_accepting()
01989         worker.join_bounded(5.0)
01990         assert not worker.is_alive()
01991 
01992     # The check of the cancellation and the publication of the intent to
01993     # enter the operation are **one** critical section of the worker.
01994     # This is the auxiliary proof of the group: the behaviour above is
01995     # the oracle and the lock is only the reason why the interleaving is
01996     # impossible and not improbable.
01997     source = (PACK_ROOT / 'tools/v0_s3_native_broker.py').read_text(encoding='utf-8')
01998     tree = ast.parse(source)
01999     run = None
02000     for node in ast.walk(tree):
02001         if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef) and node.name == '_WindowsPipeWorker':
02002             for item in node.body:
02003                 if isinstance(item, ast.FunctionDef) and item.name == 'run':
02004                     run = item
02005     assert run is not None, 'the worker of the endpoint is gone'
02006     critical = None
02007     for node in ast.walk(run):
02008         if isinstance(node, ast.With):
02009             for entry in node.items:
02010                 expression = entry.context_expr
02011                 if (isinstance(expression, ast.Attribute)
02012                         and expression.attr == '_condition'):
02013                     critical = node
02014     assert critical is not None, 'the worker does not take its lock any more'
02015     section = ast.unparse(critical)
02016     assert 'self._cancelled' in section
02017     assert "self.state = 'IN_IO'" in section
02018     assert 'self.inside.set()' in section
02019

02021 # -- N07-R12: every segment of a partial write reads the latest deadline --
02022 
02023 def test_n07_r12_every_segment_of_a_partial_write_reads_the_latest_deadline():
02024     """N07-R12 (M08): the deadline of a partial write is never a copy.
02025 
02026     Obligation: every segment of a partial write reads the latest
02027     effective deadline of the round; a deadline that was shortened
02028     while the write was running ends the write and no further segment
02029     is written.  Entry: ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint._write_block`` and
02030     ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint._current_write_deadline``.  Trigger: the
02031     deadline of the round is shortened after the first segment.  Oracle:
02032     the write ends with a timeout and only the first segment was
02033     written.  Negative: the copy of the deadline of the first segment
02034     never lets a further segment through.  Returned state: the write
02035     fails with a named error.  Salvage: the owner of the endpoint
02036     closes it.
02037     """
02038     writes = []
02039     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
02040     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('write'), name='inprocess',
02041                                     api=None, clock=clock, handle=702)
02042 
02043     class Api:
02044         def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
02045             return 701
02046 
02047         def write_stream(self, handle, data):
02048             writes.append(bytes(data))
02049             if len(writes) == 1:
02050                 # The deadline of the round is shortened while the write
02051                 # is running and the clock passes it.
02052                 endpoint.bind_deadline(clock.value + 0.5)
02053                 clock.value += 10.0
02054             return 1
02055 
02056         def cancel_synchronous_io(self, handle):
02057             return 'REQUESTED'
02058 
02059         def close_handle(self, handle):
02060             return None
02061 
02062     endpoint._api = Api()
02063     try:
02064         with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
02065             endpoint.write(b'abcdef', clock.value + 100.0)
02066         assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT'
02067         assert len(writes) == 1, 'a segment was written after the deadline'
02068     finally:
02069         endpoint.close()

02072 # -- N07-R13: every side effect checks the shared context -----------------
02073 
02074 def test_n07_r13_every_side_effect_checks_the_shared_context(tmp_path):
02075     """N07-R13 (M08): the budget of the round is one budget.
02076 
02077     Obligation: every side effect of the startup checks the shared
02078     context before it happens and after it happened; a request that is
02079     already expired is refused before it is submitted and a pipe that
02080     would be created after the deadline is never created; the endpoint
02081     reads the absolute deadline of the round from the context; the
02082     recovery of the launcher uses the remaining budget and never a fixed
02083     number of seconds.  Entry: ``BrokerCore.arm``,
02084     ``BrokerCore._launch_role``, ``_WindowsPipeEndpoint._effective_deadline``
02085     and ``_terminate_and_close_process``.  Trigger: the deadline of the
02086     round arrives before the arm and before the launch.  Oracle: nothing
02087     was created and the endpoint reads the deadline of the context.
02088     Negative: an expired deadline never starts a new wait.  Returned
02089     state: the refusal is named.  Salvage: the resources of the round
02090     are released by their owner.
02091     """
02092     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
02093     win32 = RecordingWin32()
02094     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
02095                       clock=clock)
02096     core.arm()
02097     core.bind_absolute_deadline(1000.0)
02098     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
02099         core.launch()
02100     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
02101     assert 'create_pipe' not in win32.names()
02102     assert 'create_process_suspended' not in win32.names()
02103 
02104     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
02105     win32 = RecordingWin32()
02106     core = BrokerCore(win32=win32, plan=sealed_plan(), run_root=tmp_path,
02107                       clock=clock)
02108     core.bind_absolute_deadline(999.0)
02109     with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
02110         core.arm()
02111     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
02112     assert 'create_job' not in win32.names()
02113 
02114     # The endpoint reads the absolute deadline of the round from the
02115     # context of the round and it never keeps a deadline of its own.
02116     clock = ManualClock(1000.0)
02117     context = _RoundContext(clock=clock)
02118     context.bind_absolute_deadline(1000.0)
02119     endpoint = _WindowsPipeEndpoint(file_object=_FakeFile('read'), name='inprocess',
02120                                     api=None, clock=clock, handle=702, context=context)
02121     assert endpoint._effective_deadline(None) == 1000.0
02122     assert endpoint._effective_deadline(2000.0) == 1000.0
02123 
02124     # The recovery of the launcher uses the remaining budget of the round
02125     # and never a fixed number of seconds.
02126     class WaitingProcess:
02127         pid = 1
02128         returncode = None
02129 
02130         def __init__(self):
02131             self.timeouts = []
02132             self.terminated = 0
02133 
02134         def terminate(self):
02135             self.terminated += 1
02136 
02137         def wait(self, timeout=None):
02138             self.timeouts.append(timeout)
02139             return 0
02140 
02141     process = WaitingProcess()
02142     custody = _LauncherCustody()
02143     custody.retain('process', process, 'test')
02144     report = custody.release(deadline=time.monotonic() + 1.5)
02145     assert report['released'] == ['process']
02146     assert 0 < process.timeouts[0] <= 1.5, \
02147         'the recovery opened a budget of its own'
02148 
02149     # -- N1 correction 12 (M08-E): the launch honours the **published**
02150     #    cleanup of the round and the **public stop** of the round.  A
02151     #    launch that is prepared after the cleanup of the round expired
02152     #    creates no process, it hashes no executable and it builds no
02153     #    channel - even when the absolute lease of the round is still in
02154     #    the future.
02155     document = _live_plan_document()
02156     observations = {'popen': 0}
02157     real_subprocess = broker_module.subprocess
02158 
02159     class FakeProcess:
02160         pid = 4242
02161         returncode = 0
02162 
02163         def __init__(self):
02164             self.stdin = type('S', (), {'close': lambda self: None})()
02165             self.stdout = type('S', (), {'close': lambda self: None})()
02166             self.stderr = type('S', (), {'close': lambda self: None})()
02167 
02168         def terminate(self):
02169             return None
02170 
02171         def wait(self, timeout=None):
02172             return 0
02173 
02174     def fake_popen(command, **kwargs):
02175         observations['popen'] += 1
02176         return FakeProcess()
02177 
02178     class _OsView:
02179         """A view of the module os that reports the platform."""
02180 
02181         name = 'nt'
02182 
02183         def __getattr__(self, item):
02184             return getattr(os, item)
02185 
02186     class _SubprocessView:
02187         """A view of the module subprocess whose Popen is the fake."""
02188 
02189         def Popen(self, command, **kwargs):
02190             return fake_popen(command, **kwargs)
02191 
02192         def __getattr__(self, item):
02193             return getattr(real_subprocess, item)
02194 
02195     paused = threading.Event()
02196     resume = threading.Event()
02197     original_command = broker_module.build_broker_command
02198 
02199     def pausing_command(**kwargs):
02200         command = original_command(**kwargs)
02201         # The launch is paused in its preparation: the next effect of
02202         # the launch is the creation of the process.
02203         paused.set()
02204         resume.wait(30.0)
02205         return command
02206 
02207     session = NativeCustodySession(plan=document, run_root=tmp_path,
02208                                    parent_child_remaining_seconds=21600,
02209                                    launcher=None, clock=time.monotonic,
02210                                    wall=time.time, nonce='a' * 32)
02211     with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os', _OsView()), \
02212             unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'subprocess',
02213                                        _SubprocessView()), \
02214             unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'build_broker_command',
02215                                        pausing_command):
02216         starter = threading.Thread(target=session.start)
02217         starter.start()
02218         try:
02219             assert paused.wait(15.0), 'the launch was never prepared'
02220             # The **public stop** of the round publishes the cleanup of
02221             # the round while the absolute lease of the round stays in
02222             # the future.
02223             session.stop()
02224             resume.set()
02225             starter.join(20.0)
02226             assert not starter.is_alive(), 'the launch was left behind'
02227         finally:
02228             resume.set()
02229             starter.join(20.0)
02230     failures = list(session.evidence()['failures'])
02231     facts = {'popen': observations['popen'], 'failures': failures,
02232              'cleanup': session.evidence()['cleanup_deadline']}
02233     _f5_write('r13-launch-stop', facts)
02234     assert observations['popen'] == 0, \
02235         'the launch created a process after the public stop of the round'
02236     assert any('BROKER_STARTUP_CANCELLED' in failure
02237                for failure in failures), \
02238         'the launch was not refused by the public stop of the round'
02239 
02240     # The published cleanup of the round is part of the effective
02241     # minimum of the launch: a round whose cleanup expired may not
02242     # create a process any more.
02243     # The **own** owner of this launch: the table of the launcher is
02244     # shared by every launch of the batch and a launch that another
02245     # check of the batch retained is never confused with the resources
02246     # of this one.  The baseline is taken **before** this launch.
02247     before = {id(item['resource']) for item in _launcher_custody_items()
02248               if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'}
02249     context = _RoundContext(clock=ManualClock(1000.0))
02250     context.bind_absolute_deadline(1100.0)
02251     context.cleanup_deadline(999.0)
02252     with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os', _OsView()), \
02253             unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'subprocess',
02254                                        _SubprocessView()):
02255         with pytest.raises(BrokerError) as info:
02256             broker_module.launch_broker(sealed_plan(), tmp_path, 'a' * 32,
02257                                         context=context)
02258     own = [item for item in _launcher_custody_items()
02259            if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'
02260            and id(item['resource']) not in before]
02261     owner = own[0]['resource'] if own else None
02262     facts = {'code': info.value.code, 'popen': observations['popen'],
02263              'own_owners': len(own),
02264              'owner_process': bool(owner is not None
02265                                    and owner.process is not None),
02266              'owner_channel': bool(owner is not None
02267                                    and owner.channel is not None),
02268              'owner_raw': len(owner.raw_streams) if owner is not None else None}
02269     _f5_write('r13-launch-cleanup', facts)
02270     assert info.value.code == 'BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'
02271     assert observations['popen'] == 0, \
02272         'the launch created a process after the cleanup of the round expired'
02273     # The refused launch of **this** check kept exactly one owner and
02274     # that owner is an **empty** bookkeeping owner: no process, no
02275     # channel and no raw stream of it exists.
02276     assert len(own) == 1, \
02277         'the refused launch did not keep exactly one owner of its own'
02278     assert owner.process is None, \
02279         'the refused launch kept a process of its own'
02280     assert owner.channel is None, \
02281         'the refused launch kept a channel of its own'
02282     assert owner.raw_streams == [], \
02283         'the refused launch kept a raw stream of its own'
02284     # The empty owner of **this** launch converges: the bounded
02285     # recovery of the launcher removes it and it never touches the
02286     # launches that another check of the batch retained.
02287     release_retained_launches()
02288     assert not [item for item in _launcher_custody_items()
02289                 if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'
02290                 and item['resource'] is owner], \
02291         'the refused launch kept its owner in the table of the launcher'
02292 
02293     # -- N1 correction 13 (M08-E): the **public stop** of the round
02294     #    refuses the **next** distinct effect of the launch.  The stop
02295     #    is published while the hash of the executable of the launch is
02296     #    really in flight and the channel of the round is never built.
02297     #    The process that was already admitted is **not** undone: it
02298     #    keeps its owner in the table of the launcher and it stays
02299     #    reachable and recoverable by the recovery of the launcher.
02300     hash_paused = threading.Event()
02301     hash_release = threading.Event()
02302     hash_calls = []
02303 
02304     class _HashView:
02305         """A view of the module hashlib that pauses inside the digest."""
02306 
02307         def sha256(self, *args, **kwargs):
02308             hash_calls.append(1)
02309             hash_paused.set()
02310             hash_release.wait(20.0)
02311             return hashlib.sha256(*args, **kwargs)
02312 
02313         def __getattr__(self, item):
02314             return getattr(hashlib, item)
02315 
02316     before = {id(item['resource']) for item in _launcher_custody_items()
02317               if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'}
02318     stopped = NativeCustodySession(plan=document,
02319                                    run_root=tmp_path / 'stop-during-hash',
02320                                    parent_child_remaining_seconds=21600,
02321                                    launcher=None, clock=time.monotonic,
02322                                    wall=time.time, nonce='a' * 32)
02323     with unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'os', _OsView()), \
02324             unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'subprocess',
02325                                        _SubprocessView()), \
02326             unittest.mock.patch.object(broker_module, 'hashlib', _HashView()):
02327         starter = threading.Thread(target=stopped.start)
02328         starter.start()
02329         try:
02330             assert hash_paused.wait(15.0), \
02331                 'the hash of the executable of the launch never ran'
02332             # The **public stop** of the round is published while the
02333             # hash of the executable is really in flight.
02334             stopped.stop()
02335             hash_release.set()
02336             starter.join(20.0)
02337             assert not starter.is_alive(), 'the launch was left behind'
02338         finally:
02339             hash_release.set()
02340             starter.join(20.0)
02341     own = [item for item in _launcher_custody_items()
02342            if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'
02343            and id(item['resource']) not in before]

02346              'owner_channel': bool(owner is not None
02347                                    and owner.channel is not None),

02349                                    and owner.process is not None),
02350              'failures': list(stopped.evidence()['failures'])}
02351     _f5_write('r13-launch-stop-during-hash', facts)
02352     assert len(hash_calls) == 1, \
02353         'the hash of the executable of the launch did not run exactly once'
02354     assert len(own) == 1, \
02355         'the refused launch did not keep exactly one owner of its own'
02356     # The **next** distinct effect of the launch was refused: the
02357     # channel of the round was never built.

02359         'the launch built a channel after the public stop of the round'

02380                 if item['kind'] == 'aggregate'
02381                 and item['resource'] is owner], \
02382         'the refused launch kept its owner in the table of the launcher'
02383     # The table of the launcher is bound to a **current** context of the
02384     # round after this subcase: the published cleanup deadline of the
02385     # round that was stopped above must not refuse the recovery of a
02386     # later check of the batch.  The production binding is used and the
02387     # table of the launcher is never emptied by hand.
02388     current = _RoundContext(clock=time.monotonic)
02389     current.bind_absolute_deadline(time.monotonic() + 3600.0)
02390     broker_module._LAUNCHER_CUSTODY.bind_context(current)
02391 
02392     # -- N1 correction 13 (PF13-03): the **public stop** of the round
02393     #    while the process of the round is **admitted** and the launch
02394     #    is paused between the creation of the process and the read of
02395     #    the executable.  The launch resumes after the stop: no
02396     #    executable read of the executable happens and the channel of
02397     #    the round is never constructed.  The counters of the seams are
02398     #    the evidence: a channel that is absent is never a proof that
02399     #    no constructor was attempted.
02400     popen_paused = threading.Event()
02401     popen_release = threading.Event()
02402     popen_calls = []
02403     hash_calls = []
02404     read_calls = []
02405     channel_calls = []
02406     original_channel = broker_module.StreamChannel
02407     original_read_bytes = Path.read_bytes
02408 
02409     def counting_read_bytes(target):
02410         # The **read** of the executable is counted as well as the
02411         # digest: a digest that was never computed does not prove that
02412         # the executable was never read.
02413         read_calls.append(1)
02414         return original_read_bytes(target)
02415 
02416     class CountingChannel(original_channel):
02417         """The production channel with a counter of its constructions.
02418 
02419         The class is the production one and the counter only observes
02420         that a construction was **attempted**: the absence of a channel
02421         is never a proof that no constructor ran.
02422         """
02423 
02424         def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
02425             channel_calls.append(1)
02426             super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
02427 
02428     class _CountingHashView:
02429         """A view of the module hashlib that counts the digests."""
02430 
02431         def sha256(self, *args, **kwargs):
02432             hash_calls.append(1)
02433             return hashlib.sha256(*args, **kwargs)
02434 
02435         def __getattr__(self, item):
02436             return getattr(hashlib, item)
02437 
02438     created = {}
02439 
02440     def pausing_popen(command, **kwargs):
02441         """The creation of the process pauses the launch of the round."""
02442         popen_calls.append(1)
02443         created['process'] = FakeProcess()
02444         popen_paused.set()
02445         popen_release.wait(20.0)
02446         return created['process']
02447 
02448     class _PausingSubprocessView:
02449         """A view of the module subprocess whose Popen pauses."""
02450 
02451         def Popen(self, command, **kwargs):
02452             return pausing_popen(command, **kwargs)
02453 
02454         def __getattr__(self, item):
02455             return getattr(subprocess, item)
02456 
02457     admitted = NativeCustodySession(plan=document,
02458                                    run_root=tmp_path / 'stop-while-popen',
02459                                    parent_child_remaining_seconds=21600,
02460                                    launcher=None, clock=time.monotonic,

02468                                        CountingChannel), \
02469             unittest.mock.patch.object(Path, 'read_bytes',
02470                                        counting_read_bytes):
02471         starter = threading.Thread(target=admitted.start)

02473         try:

02475                 'the process of the round was never created'

02481             assert not starter.is_alive(), 'the launch was left behind'
02482         finally:
02483             popen_release.set()
02484             starter.join(20.0)
02485     facts = {'popen_calls': len(popen_calls),

02490     _f5_write('r13-stop-while-popen', facts)

02496     # the seams are the evidence: the absence of a channel is never a

02500     assert hash_calls == [], \
02501         'the executable of the round was digested after the public stop'
02502     assert channel_calls == [], \
